Affine Springer fiber and the small quantum group Thanks: R.B. is partly supported by the NSF grant DMS-2101507. Z.Y. is supported by the Simon Foundation. M.M. is supported by the Research Grants Council of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region, Project No. CUHK 14307022, 14309323, 14307825.
Roman Bezrukavnikov, Pablo Boixeda Alvarez, Michael McBreen, Zhiwei Yun

Abstract
. We find a new geometric incarnation for the principal block in the category of modules over a quantum group at a root of unity, realizing it as a full subcategory of microsheaves on a certain affine Springer fiber. We also prove a related geometric Langlands type equivalence with wild ramification, identifying the latter category with a category of coherent sheaves on the Springer resolution for the dual group. This can also be viewed as a version of homological mirror symmetry for the Springer resolution.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14D24; Secondary 22E57

1.
Introduction

The results of this article have several sources of motivation belonging, respectively,
to representation theory, geometric Langlands duality and homological mirror symmetry.

From the representation theory perspective, the main result (see Theorem
1.1.4
) is a new geometric realization of
a category of modules over the quantum group at a root of unity. Earlier theorems of this nature
identified a category of quantum group modules with perverse sheaves on the affine or
semi-infinite flag manifold, which implies the relation between the appropriate version of the affine Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials to numerical invariants of representations.
Theorem
1.1.4
below relates quantum group modules to a different geometric object: it asserts
its equivalence with a full subcategory
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
of
microsheaves
on a certain affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
, where
\psi=t\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)
and
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}
is regular semisimple;
or more precisely, microsheaves on a certain symplectic variety
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
supported on
a conical Lagrangian subset homeomorphic to
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.
The known and expected applications (see
[
23
]
and Theorem
1.3.2
below) are also different.

The category
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
of microsheaves on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
also admits a description in terms of perverse sheaves: it turns out to be equivalent to
a full subcategory in the category of perverse sheaves on the affine flag variety
Fl
of
G
,
Whittaker-equivariant with respect to the first congruence subgroup
G[t^{-1}]^{1}
in the group of negative loops
G[t^{-1}]

equipped with the additive character given by
\psi
;
the space
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
above is the shifted Hamiltonian reduction of
T^{*}\textup{Fl}
by the action of
G[t^{-1}]^{1}
(by a shifted Hamiltonian reduction we understand the quotient of the preimage of
d\psi=\psi_{1}dt
under the moment map).
We believe that the microlocal description of the category of (partially) Whittaker sheaves
is a general phenomenon with strong potential applications in representation theory, see

1.4.4
below. To make sense of Whittaker sheaves in an arbitrary sheaf-theoretic context (without necessarily the Artin-Schreier sheaves or exponential
D
-modules at our disposal), we use the notion of Kirillov category introduced by Gaitsgory
[
26
]
.

The quotient of the affine flag variety by the congruence subgroup
G[t^{-1}]^{1}
is the moduli stack
of
G
-bundles on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
with level structures at zero and infinity, so the study of sheaves on this space belongs in geometric Langlands duality. Accordingly, we establish an equivalence (see Theorem
1.1.3
) between the derived category of microsheaves and the derived category of coherent
sheaves on a stack of Langlands parameters which can be identified with the derived category
of quantum group modules based on
[
2
]
and
[
48
]
.

On the other hand, we can also realize the symplectic variety
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
as the moduli space of Higgs bundles
on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
with a regular singularities at zero and an irregular one at infinity (of order
2
), as explained in
[
9
]
. There is a corresponding Betti space
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bet},\psi}
, diffeomorphic to a quotient of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
by an action of the lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
of cocharacters of a maximal torus
T\subset G
. The space
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bet},\psi}
is (for suitable choices of parameters) a smooth affine variety of finite type, and thus comes equipped with a contractible space of Weinstein structures. Microsheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bet},\psi}
may be identified with a version of the Fukaya category, using the general theory developed in
[
29
]
. One expects that the category of microsheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is equivalent to a suitable
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-graded version of the Fukaya category of
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bet},\psi}
.

Combining the above discussions with the geometric Langlands type equivalence in Theorem
1.1.3
, one would thus establish homological mirror symmetry (HMS) between
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bet},\psi}
and a space
of Langlands parameters. While this case of HMS was among our sources of motivation,
we do not work with Fukaya categories in this paper, thus
this statement remains conjectural.

1.1.
Kirillov category and microsheaves on the affine Springer fiber

1.1.1.
Affine Springer fiber

Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{C}
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)
be the field of Laurent series in one variable
t
, and let
G(\!(t)\!)
be the associated loop group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)=\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)
. Let
\mathbf{I}\subset G(\!(t)\!)
be an Iwahori subgroup. Let
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}
be regular semisimple and
\psi=t\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)
. We denote by
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}=\{g\in G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1}\psi\in\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{I})\}
the affine Springer fiber of
\psi
in the affine flag variety
\textup{Fl}=G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}
. Note that
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
has irreducible components in bijection with the extended affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\rtimes W
, and each component has dimension equal to that of the flag variety
\mathcal{B}
of
G
(in fact some components can be identified with
\mathcal{B}
). It also carries a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by loop rotation (scaling
t
).

Just as the usual Springer fibers, the affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
can be embedded into a symplectic variety
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
as a Lagrangian. Moreover, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
extends to
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
, and all points of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
contracts to points in
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
under this action. This is not a general structure for any affine Springer fiber but crucially relies on the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-symmetry on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
. A more general construction of this kind is done in our previous paper
[
9
]
.

1.1.2.
Kirillov category

In geometric representation theory we often encounter sheaf-theoretic versions of Whittaker models whose definition involves an Artin-Schreier sheaf on the affine line, so a priori only makes sense over a characteristic
p
base field, or in the context of
D
-modules where we replace Artin-Schreier sheaf by the exponential
D
-module. Gaitsgory
[
26
]
gives a way to define such geometric Whittaker categories in any sheaf-theoretic context (in particular in the Betti setting), when the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on the space involved extends to an action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
(where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
by scaling). We apply his construction
1
1

1

We will need a variant of his construction where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariance is replaced by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromicity, which we develop in §
2.3
.
to the moduli stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
of
G

-bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with Iwahori level structure at
0
and the level structure given by the group
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
, the kernel of the linear character
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\to\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\psi_{1}}\mathbb{G}_{a}
, where
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\ker(G[\![t^{-1}]\!]\to G)
is the first congruence subgroup at
\infty
. The
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
comes from the residual symmetry of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}
; the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action is by rotation on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
fixing
0
and
\infty
. If we do not impose Iwahori level structure at
0
we get a moduli stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. Let

\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})),\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0}))

be the Kirillov categories of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
; they are localizations of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equviariant sheaves on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
and
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
by killing sheaves that are also
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant. We can work with various sheaf-theoretic contexts (see §
1.5.1
), with characteristic zero coefficient field
E
.

Let
G^{\vee}
be the Langlands dual group of
G
, as a split reductive group over
E
, equipped with a maximal split torus
T^{\vee}
.

Our first result is a geometric Langlands type equivalence beyond tame ramification. It gives a description of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
in terms of coherent sheaves on a stack of Langlands parameters.

1.1.3 Theorem

(See Theorem
8.3.1
)
.

There is a canonical equivalence

(1.1)

\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})).

Here

•

The left side is the full subcategory of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
generated by the image of the pullback
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
under the action of the affine Hecke category on
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
(by modifying the bundles at
0
).

•

On the right side,
\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})
is the abelian category of
T^{\vee}
-equivariant coherent sheaves on the Springer resolution
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}=T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
of the nilpotent cone of
G^{\vee}
with set-theoretic support on the zero section
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
(the flag variety of
G^{\vee}
).

The equivalence is compatible with the following actions:

(1)

The actions of the constructible and coherent affine Hecke categories on both sides, under the monoidal equivalence of the two realizations of the affine Hecke categories
[
8
]
.

(2)

The actions of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
on the right side by tensoring, and on the left side via a monoidal functor
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
to a certain depth one Hecke category
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
that acts on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
via modification of bundles at
\infty
.

Combining this result with known coherent realization of the category of modules over a quantum group we arrive at the following:

1.1.4 Theorem

(See Corollary
8.3.2
(1))
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
is the heart of the perverse
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
(see Theorem
1.1.3
). Let
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}
be the principal block category in the category of
\mathbb{X}^{*}(T)
-graded modules over the small quantum group
u_{q}
at a root of unity. Then
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
is equivalent
to the category of finitely generated modules with nilpotent central character
over the Koszul dual to
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}
.

1.2.
Microsheaves and microlocal fiber functor for Satake category

As mentioned in the beginning,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is an incarnation of a category of microsheaves on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
in the classical sheaf-theoretic context. We now make this statement more precise.

Following Nadler and Shende
[
42
,
43
]
, we consider the category
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
of microsheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
supported on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
. More details on microsheaves will be recalled in §
3.1
and §
4.7
. Let
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
be the full subcategory of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
consisting of microsheaves supported on finitely many irreducible components of
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and with cohomologically bounded and finite-dimensional microstalks.

A general construction for Kirillov categories provides a microlocalization functor

\widetilde{M}:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi}).

1.2.1 Theorem

(See Theorem
7.5.4
and Proposition
7.6.2
)
.

The functor
\widetilde{M}
restricts to a full embedding of categories

(1.2)

M:\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\hookrightarrow\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi}).

Moreover,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is the full subcategory of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
consisting of sheaves whose image under
\widetilde{M}
lies in
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
.

We expect the full embedding (
1.2
) to be an equivalence. We plan to come back to this question later.

1.2.2.
Microlocal fiber functor for the Satake category

Along the way we also prove a result about the Satake category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}=\textup{Perv}(G[\![t]\!]\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!])
that is simple to state and is of independent interest. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we view
(t^{\lambda},\psi)
as a point in the cotangent bundle of
\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]
at the point
t^{\lambda}
. Let
\mu_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\to\textup{Vect}_{E}
be the functor of taking microstalk (or local Morse group) at
(t^{\lambda},\psi)
. This functor was considered by Evans and Mirković
[
22
]
who showed that the dimension of
\mu_{\lambda}(\textup{IC}_{\mu})
(where
\textup{IC}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
is the intersection complex of the Schubert variety
\textup{Gr}_{\leq\mu}
) is the multiplicity of
\lambda
in the irreducible
G^{\vee}
-module
V_{\mu}
with highest weight
\mu
. Our next result gives a categorification of their numerical result; this was conjectured
(in a somewhat different form) in
[
24
]
.

1.2.3 Theorem
.

The direct sum of the microstalks functors

\mu=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\mu_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\cong\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}

has a canonical monoidal structure. Under the geometric Satake equivalence
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\cong\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})
,
\mu
is canonically isomorphic to the restriction functor
\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
up to composing with the inversion on
T^{\vee}
.

In particular, composing
\mu
with the forgetful functor, the resulting functor
\mu^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\to\textup{Vect}
has a canonical structure of a fiber functor for the Tannakian category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
.

1.3.
Applications

1.3.1.
Consequences for
K
-groups

Consequences of theorems in §
1.1
at the level of Grothendieck groups are
used in the recent work of Feng-Le Hung
[
23
]
that solves cases of the Breuil-Mézard conjecture on the deformation space of Galois representations. The precise results used in
loc.cit.
are:

1.3.2 Theorem

(See Theorem
8.4.9
)
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups

K_{0}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))\to\textup{H}_{2N}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})

where
N=\dim\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
,
that intertwines both the left and right actions of the affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\rtimes W
on both sides:

(1)

the left side is equipped with a left action of
\widetilde{W}
by tensoring with
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
and the action of
N_{G^{\vee}}(T^{\vee})
; it is also equipped with a right
\widetilde{W}
-action from convolution by the equivariant
K
-group of the Steinberg variety of
G^{\vee}
.

(2)

The right side is equipped with a left
\widetilde{W}
-action by translation of the centralizer of
\psi
on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and by the monodromy action by varying
\psi
; it is also equipped with a Springer type right
\widetilde{W}
-action constructed by Lusztig (up to tensoring by the sign character).

1.3.3.
Cohomology of affine Springer fiber and center of small quantum group

Another application is to the study of the center of small quantum group, see
[
35
]
and
[
10
]
. The main result of
[
10
]
is an injective homomorphism (conjecturally an isomorphism) from
\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Fl}_{\psi})
to the center (endomorphisms of the identity functor) of
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}
.

Theorem
1.1.4
provides a new way to construct and study this homomorphism. Namely, we have a natural map from
\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Fl}_{\psi})
to
endomorphisms of the identity functor in
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})

preserving the homological degree. Using the purity of
\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Fl}_{\psi})

one can check that Koszul duality sends this action to an action
by degree zero endomorphisms of the Koszul dual triangulated category resulting
in a homomorphism
\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Fl}_{\psi})\to\textup{End}(\textup{Id}_{u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}})
.
We plan to develop this application elsewhere.

1.4.
Further directions

1.4.1.
Coherent description of all microsheaves on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}

We expect Theorem
1.1.3
to extend to a full embedding from the larger category
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
to the category of coherent sheaves on a Betti moduli space for
G^{\vee}
, a special case of the Betti moduli spaces introduced in
[
40
]
, see also
[
9
, Section 4]
.

Let
B^{\vee,+}
and
B^{\vee,-}
be a pair of opposite Borel subgroups of
G^{\vee}
such that
B^{\vee,+}\cap B^{\vee,-}=T^{\vee}
. Consider the ”big cell”

\mathcal{W}=B^{\vee,-}B^{\vee,+}\subset G^{\vee}.

Let
\mathcal{U}^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}
be the unipotent variety and
\mathcal{W}_{0}=\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\vee}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}^{\vee}}|_{\mathcal{W}_{0}}
be the restriction of the Springer resolution
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}^{\vee}}
to
\mathcal{W}_{0}
. The maximal torus
T^{\vee}
acts on
\mathcal{W}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
by conjugation.

1.4.2 Conjecture
.

There is a full embedding

(1.3)

\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})\hookrightarrow\textup{QCoh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}).

extending the equivalence (
1.1
) in Theorem
1.1.3
in the following sense:

•

The Kirillov side
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
of (
1.1
) is viewed as full subcategory of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
by Theorem
1.2.1
;

•

The coherent side
D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
of (
1.1
) is identified with a full subcategory of
\textup{QCoh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
supported on
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
(fiber over
1\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
).

Moreover, the embedding (
1.3
) intertwines the natural actions of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on both sides.

The lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
acts on the symplectic variety
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
preserving its Lagrangian
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
(see Section
4.5
), and the statement that it induces an action on
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
is part of the conjecture (see Section
4.7.3
). On the coherent side
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
acts by tensoring via the equivalence
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)=\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
.

1.4.3.
Other homogeneous affine Springer fibers

In
[
9
, Conjecture 5.0.2]
we formulated a more general conjecture for any homogeneous element
\psi
in the loop Lie algebra, of which Conjecture
1.3
is a special case. To connect the two, we observe that
[\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}/\textup{Ad}(T^{\vee})]
is the Betti moduli space denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{\textup{Bet},\psi}^{0,G^{\vee}}
in
loc.cit.
, because the braid attached to the slope one element
\psi
is the full twist
\beta=w_{0}^{2}
(see
[
9
, Example 4.1.3]
).

In a sequel to this work, we will prove generalizations of Theorem
1.1.3
to homogeneous elements
\psi
with slope of the form
1/m
.

1.4.4.
Microsheaves on usual Springer fibers

Let
e
be a nilpotent element in
\mathfrak{g}
,
S_{e}\subset\mathcal{N}
be the (nilpotent part of the) Slodowy slice through
e
and
\widetilde{S}_{e}
be the preimage of
S_{e}
under the Springer resolution. Then the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{e}
is a conical Lagrangian in
\widetilde{S}_{e}
, and we may consider the category of microsheaves
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{B}_{e}}(\widetilde{S}_{e})
. Our construction in this paper suggests a sheaf-theoretic model for
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{B}_{e}}(\widetilde{S}_{e})
, namely a full subcategory in the category
\Kir(V_{e}\backslash\mathcal{B})
, where
V_{e}
is the kernel of
the character
\psi_{e}:U_{e}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}

for a Premet subgroup
U_{e}
and its character
\psi_{e}
whose differential equals the linear functional defined by
e
. For example, if
e
is even and
\{e,h,f\}
is an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple, then
\textup{Lie}\ U_{e}
is the sum of negative weight spaces of
\mathfrak{g}
under
h
. We expect this category to be equivalent to the category of finite dimensional modules
over the corresponding
W
-algebra with an integral regular central character. Notice
that this category is closely connected to the
D
-modules (or Harish-Chandra bimodules) realization of the asymptotic Hecke category
[
37
]
and to character

D
-modules
[
13
]
. We expect the above topological realization to yield a generalization
of these results to the constructible setting.

1.4.5.
Characteristic
p
version

We expect that our results can be extended to the setting when the coefficient field
E
has positive characteristic. The analogue of Theorem
1.1.4
is a Koszul duality between the category of microsheaves and a regular block in the category
of
(G^{\vee}_{1},T^{\vee})
-modules where
G^{\vee}_{1}
is the kernel of the Frobenius morphism for the reductive
group
G^{\vee}
over
E
. Notice that a key ingredient in the proof of
Theorem
1.1.4
, the relation of modules over the Lusztig quantum group and perverse
sheaves on the affine Grassmannian
[
2
]
has a positive characteristic analogue known as the Finkelberg–Mirković conjecture, which was established
in
[
18
]
with some restrictions on
\textup{char}(E)
. However, the proof of Theorem
1.1.4
relies also on the formality from weights
argument which is not available in positive characteristic, thus the generalization requires
a different technical implementation of the strategy outlined in Remark
8.1.2
.

1.4.6.
Coherent duality for affine Springer fibers

We briefly describe a heuristic relation of our results and the more general

[
9
, Conjecture 1.4.1]
to the conjectural equivalence between two coherent categorifications
of the double affine Hecke algebra (DAHA), which is a local counterpart of the classical limit
of the geometric Langlands duality.

Varagnolo and Vasserot
[
49
, Theorem 2.5.6]
introduced a categorification of DAHA coming from the derived category of equivariant coherent sheaves on the loop group analogue of the Steinberg variety (a similar construction
for the spherical, degenerate DAHA appeared in
[
12
]
). One expects that the monoidal categories constructed this way from two Langlands dual group
G
,
G^{\vee}
are naturally equivalent, see

[
12
, §7.9]
for the similar conjecture in the spherical case. Next, this equivalence of monoidal
categories should come with compatible equivalence of module categories as follows.

We fix an invariant quadratic form yielding a vector space isomorphism
\mathfrak{t}\cong\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
and
an isomorphism of Chevalley spaces
\mathfrak{t}/W\cong\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}/W
. Let
e\in\gamma((t))
and
e^{\vee}\in\gamma^{\vee}((t))
be regular semisimple topologically nilpotent elements whose images in the Chevalley space
match under this isomorphism. Then one expects an equivalence

DCoh(\textup{Fl}_{e})\cong DCoh^{Z_{e^{\vee}}}(\textup{Fl}_{e^{\vee}})

where
Z_{e^{\vee}}
is the centralizer of
e^{\vee}
and
\textup{Fl}_{e}
,
\textup{Fl}_{e^{\vee}}
are the affine Springer fibers; (
1.4.6
) should be an equivalence of module
categories over the two coherent categorifications of DAHA.

The heuristic of
S^{1}
-localization proposed by Ben-Zvi and Nadler
[
5
]
suggests that
(
1.4.6
) should imply another equivalence of categories obtained, respectively, from the left
hand side by quantization and from the right hand side by passing to the similar object for the
finite dimensional group
G^{\vee}
replacing the corresponding loop group. In the special case when
e=ts
as above, the two categories can be arguable identified with the two sided of the equivalence
in Theorem
1.1.3
.

1.5.
Notation and convention

1.5.1.
Sheaf-theoretic contexts

Let
X
be an algebraic stack of finite type over an algebraically closed field
k
. Let
E
be a field of characteristic zero depending on the sheaf-theoretic context. We will use the notation
D(X)
to denote any of the following
E
-linear sheaf-theoretic categories in different contexts:

•

Étale: take
E=\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
where
\textup{char}(k)\neq\ell
. Let
D^{b}(X,E)
be the bounded derived category of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-complexes of sheaves for the étale topology of
X
. We will reserve the notation
D(X)
for a variant of
D^{b}(X,E)
by formally adding a half Tate twist as in §
1.5.2
.

•

Betti:
k=\mathbb{C}
and
E
is any field of characteristic zero field. We take
D(X)
to be the bounded derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
with
E
-coefficients for the classical topology on
X(\mathbb{C})
.

•

De Rham:
\textup{char}(k)=0
and
E=k
. We take
D(X)
to be the bounded derived category of algebraic
D
-modules on
X
.

The main body of the paper is written in the étale context, although all arguments in the paper work for the Betti and de Rham contexts equally well.

1.5.2.
Half Tate twist

In the étale setting, we have Tate twist as a local system on
\textup{Spec}\ k

\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1)=\varprojlim_{n}\mu_{\ell^{n}}(k),\quad E(1):=E\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(1).

For any
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(X,E)
we denote
\mathcal{F}(1)=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{E}E(1)\in D^{b}(X,E)
.

We form the doubled category

\displaystyle D(X):=D^{b}(X,E)\oplus D^{b}(X,E).

Denote
(0,E)\in D(X)
as
E(1/2)
, and in general
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\in D(X)
as
\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{G}(1/2)
. This way we have formally introduced a half Tate twist to
D^{b}(X,E)
.
Tensor product in
D(X)
obey the usual rules
E(n/2)\otimes E(m/2)=E((n+m)/2)
.

For
\mathcal{F}\in D(X)
we denote

\mathcal{F}\langle{n}\rangle=\mathcal{F}[n](n/2)\in D(X).

1.5.3.
Averaging functors

Suppose an algebraic group
G
acts on s stack
X
, we denote
D_{G}(X)=D(G\backslash X)
. We have the forgetful functor
\omega_{G}:D_{G}(X)\to D(X)
to be
p^{*}[\dim G]\cong p^{!}[-\dim G]
, where
p:X\to[X/G]
is the quotient map. It has a left adjoint
\textup{Av}_{G,!}
and a right adjoint
\textup{Av}_{G,*}

\textstyle{D_{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{G}}
\textstyle{D(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{G,*}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{G,!}}

We have
\textup{Av}_{G,!}(-)\cong a_{!}(E\langle{\dim G}\rangle\boxtimes(-))
, and
\textup{Av}_{G,*}(-)\cong a_{*}(E\langle{-\dim G}\rangle\boxtimes(-))
where
a:G\times X\to X
is the action map.

The same convention applies to averaging along the
G
-action against a rank one character local system
\mathcal{L}
on
G
. Notation:
\textup{Av}_{G,\mathcal{L},!}
, or
\textup{Av}_{\mathcal{L},!}
if
G
is clear from the context.

1.5.4.

We use
Vect
to denote the abelian category of finite-dimensional
E
-vector spaces. For an algebraic group
H
over
E
, we denote by
\textup{Rep}(H)
the category of finite-dimensional algebraic representations of
H
over
E
.

2.
Kirillov category

We elaborate on Gaitsgory’s construction of the Kirillov category, which will serve as a model in the usual sheaf-theoretic context for talking about microsheaves. We also give an alternative construction of the Kirillov category in §
2.3
that is more flexible for applications.

We will freely use material about monodromic sheaves such as monodromic vanishing cycles and monodromic Fourier transform, which are reviewed and further developed in Appendix
A
.

2.1.
Kirillov category after Gaitsgory
[
26
]

Let
\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the group of affine transformations of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Let
X
be a stack with an action of
Aff
.

2.1.1 Definition
.

The Kirillov category for the stack
X
with
Aff
-action is the quotient

\Kir(X)=D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)/D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)

under the forgetful functor
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)\hookrightarrow D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
.

Denote the quotient functor by
\kappa:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\to\Kir(X)
.

Let
\Kir_{*}(X)\subset D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
be the kernel of the averaging functor

\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},*}:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\to D_{\textup{Aff}}(X).

In other words,
\Kir_{*}(X)
is the right orthogonal to the full subcategory
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)\subset D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
. Let
\kappa_{*}:\Kir_{*}(X)\hookrightarrow D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
be the inclusion, then
\kappa\circ\kappa_{*}:\Kir_{*}(X)\to\Kir(X)
is an equivalence. The composition
\Kir(X)\cong\Kir_{*}(X)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{*}}D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
is a right adjoint of
\kappa
.

Similarly
\kappa
has a left adjoint
\kappa_{!}
whose image
\Kir_{!}(X)
is the left orthogonal of
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)
in
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
. We have the following recollement diagram

(2.1)

\textstyle{D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},*}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},!}}
\textstyle{\Kir(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{!}}

2.1.2.
Functoriality

Suppose
f:Y\to X
is an
Aff
-equivariant map. The usual adjunctions
(f_{!},f^{!})
and
(f^{*},f_{*})
pass to the quotient and induce adjoint pairs between
\Kir(Y)
and
\Kir(X)
.

2.1.3 Example
.

Let
X=\textup{Aff}
with the left translation action. Then Fourier transform along
\mathbb{G}_{a}
identifies
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
with
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)
, where
Y\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
with the action of
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
t\cdot(b,s)=(t^{-1}b,ts)
. We may identify
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)\cong D(\mathbb{A}^{1})
by restricting to
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\{1\}\subset Y
. Under these equivalence,
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)\cong D(\{0\})\xrightarrow{i_{*}}D(\mathbb{A}^{1})
,
\Kir(X)\cong D(\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\})
and the diagram (
2.1
) is the usual recollement diagram for the closed subset
\{0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{1}
and its open complement
\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}
.

2.1.4 Example
.

Let
X=\textup{pt}
. Then
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)=D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
and
\Kir(X)=0
.

2.1.5 Example
.

Let
X=\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the tautological action of
Aff
by affine transformations. Then monodromic Fourier transform (see §
A.3
) homogeneous Fourier transform
gives an equivalence
\mathrm{FT}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
where
\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action by inverse of dilation. Then
\mathrm{FT}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
restricts to an equivalence
D_{\textup{Aff}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\{0\})\subset D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
. Passing to the quotients we get an equivalence

(2.2)

\overline{\mathrm{FT}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a}-\{0\}).

Restricting along
i_{1}:1\hookrightarrow\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a}-\{0\}
gives an equivalence

(2.3)

i_{1}^{*}[-1]:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a}-\{0\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D(\textup{Vect}).

The composition of (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) gives an equivalence

(2.4)

\Phi_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D(\textup{Vect}).

Under this equivalence,
E\in\textup{Vect}
corresponds to either
\delta=i_{0*}E\in\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
(where
i_{0}:\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
), or
j_{*}E[1](1)
(where
j:\mathbb{G}_{a}-\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
), or
j_{!}E[1]
. Moreover,
\Phi_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
is monoidal with respect to the additive convolution on
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
and the tensor product on
D(\textup{Vect})
. The diagram (
2.1
) is the usual recollement diagram for the closed subset
\{0\}\subset\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
and its open complement.

This example has an obvious generalization to the case where
X=Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
, with the tautological action of
Aff
just on the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-factor. The conclusion is that we have an equivalence

D(Y)\cong\Kir(Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a})

given by sending
\mathcal{F}\in D(Y)
to either
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\delta
, or
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes j_{!}E[1]
, or
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes j_{*}E[1](1)
. This example will be further generalized in §
2.1.6
to allow nontrivial
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the
Y
-factor.

2.1.6.
A product situation

Let
Y
be a stack with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Let
X=Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be equipped with the following action of
Aff
: it acts on the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-factor in the usual way, and acts on
Y
through its quotient
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and the given
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
Y
. If we want to emphasize the diagonal action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we write
\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Note that restriction to
Y\times\{1\}
induces an equivalence

i_{1}^{*}[-1]:D_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D(Y).

Let
j:Y\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\})\hookrightarrow Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the inclusion. Define the functor

J:D(Y)\xrightarrow{(i_{1}^{*})^{-1}[1]}D_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}))\xrightarrow{j_{*}}D_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\to\Kir(X).

Let
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)\subset D(Y)
be the full subcategory given by the image of the forgetful map
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)\to D(Y)
. Let
D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\subset D_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
be the essential image of the forgetful map
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\to D_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
. Finally, let
\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)\subset\Kir(X)
be the image of
D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
under the projection to
\Kir(X)
. Then the functor
J
above restricts to a functor

J^{\mathrm{mon}}:D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)\to\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X).

Consider the monodromic vanishing cycles functor with respect to the function
p_{2}:X=Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
(see §
A.2
)

\phi:D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y).

For
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
,
\phi(\mathcal{G})=0
, therefore
\phi
factors through
\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)
:

\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}:\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y).

2.1.7 Lemma
.

In the above situation,
J^{\mathrm{mon}}
and
\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}
give inverse equivalences
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)\cong\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)
.

Proof.

We view
\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
as a line bundle over
\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash Y
, the monodromic Fourier transform reviewed in §
A.3
gives an equivalence

\mathrm{FT}:D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}\textup{-mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}\textup{-mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1})

Here
\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
denotes the dual line to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. The one-dimensional torus
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with weight
1
and on
\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
with weight
-1
. Under the Fourier transform,
D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}\textup{-mon}}_{\textup{Aff}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
(image of the pullback from
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)
) are sent to
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times\{0\})
. Taking quotients, we get an equivalence

(2.5)

\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathrm{FT}}}D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}\textup{-mon}}_{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\{0\}))\xrightarrow{i_{1}^{*}[-1]}D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)

where the last equivalence is given up to a shift by restricting to
Y\times\{1\}
. By Lemma
A.3.4
applied to the constant section
\xi:Y\to Y\times\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
given by
y\mapsto(y,1)
, we see that (
2.5
) is naturally isomorphic to
\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}
. This shows that
\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}
is an equivalence.

We give a natural isomorphism
\alpha:\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}\circ J^{\mathrm{mon}}\cong\textup{id}
. For
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)
, we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}))
the unique object such that
i_{1}^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}=\mathcal{F}
. Then
J^{\mathrm{mon}}(\mathcal{F})=j_{!}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}[1]
. Let
a:Y\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be given by
(y,s)\mapsto(sy,s)
, then
J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F}\cong j_{!}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}[1]=a_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes E)[1]
. Let
i_{t}:Y\times\{t\}\hookrightarrow Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the inclusion for
t\in\{0,1\}
. In particular,
i_{0}^{*}(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})=0
. By definition (
A.3
),
\phi(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})
fits into the distinguished triangle
\phi(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})\to 0\to i_{1}^{*}(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})\to
.
This gives a functorial isomorphism

\phi(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})\cong i_{1}^{*}(J^{\mathrm{mon}}\mathcal{F})[-1]=i_{1}^{*}a_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes E)=\mathcal{F},

which gives an isomorphism
\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}\circ J^{\mathrm{mon}}\cong\textup{id}
.
∎

2.1.8 Remark
.

Let
f:Y\to Y^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map. Then the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{J^{\mathrm{mon}}}
\textstyle{\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f\times\textup{id})_{*}}
\textstyle{D^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J^{\mathrm{mon}}}
\textstyle{\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}(Y^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})}

The same is true for
f_{!},f^{*}
and
f^{!}
.

2.2.
Comparison with Whittaker category

Suppose we are in either of the following situations:

•

\textup{char}(k)=p>0
and we work with
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-sheaves. We write
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
for the Artin-Schreier sheaf on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
associated to a fixed nontrivial character
\psi:\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

•

\textup{char}(k)=0
and we work with
D
-modules. We write
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
for the exponential
D
-module on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

In either case, consider the category
D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathcal{L}_{\psi})}(X)
of sheaves on
X
equivariant under
\mathbb{G}_{a}
with respect to the character sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
. The following proposition, due to Gaitsgory, was his motivation for introducing the Kirillov category.

2.2.1 Proposition

(Gaitsgory
[
26
, 1.6]
)
.

Let
\omega_{\psi}:D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathcal{L}_{\psi})}(X)\to D(X)
be the forgetful functor. The image of the functor

\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\psi}}
\textstyle{D(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)}

lands in
\Kir_{*}(X)
. The resulting functor

\mathbb{K}_{*}:D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X)\to\Kir_{*}(X)

is an equivalence with inverse (left adjoint)

\textstyle{\mathbb{K}^{*}:\Kir_{*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\textstyle{D(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\psi,!}}
\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X).}

Proof.

For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X)
,
\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}\omega_{\psi}\mathcal{F}
is killed by
\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},*}
because
\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},*}\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}\omega_{\psi}\mathcal{F}=\textup{Av}_{\textup{Aff},*}\omega_{\psi}\mathcal{F}=\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},*}\omega_{\psi}\mathcal{F}=0
. Therefore
\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}\omega_{\psi}\mathcal{F}\in\Kir_{*}(X)
.

Clearly
(\mathbb{K}^{*},\mathbb{K}_{*})
are adjoint. Now we check the counit
c:\mathbb{K}^{*}\circ\mathbb{K}_{*}\cong\textup{Av}_{\psi,!}\omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}\omega_{\psi}\to\textup{id}
is an isomorphism. It suffices to check the same statement for a smooth
Aff
-equivariant covering
X^{\prime}\to X
. We may take the action map
a:X^{\prime}=\textup{Aff}\times X\to X
where the action of
Aff
on
X^{\prime}
is via the left translation on
Aff
. Thus we reduce to the case
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y
with a diagonal action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Performing Fourier transform in the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
direction (denote the dual affine line by
\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, and
\check{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\backslash\{0\}
) we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\psi}}
\textstyle{D(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}}
\textstyle{D(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\psi,!}\circ\omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\textstyle{D(\{1\}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{D(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}}
\textstyle{D(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\textstyle{D(\{1\}\times Y)}

Here
i:\{1\}\times Y\hookrightarrow\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y
is the closed embedding. All vertical functors are Fourier transform. The composition of the top row is
\mathbb{K}^{*}\circ\mathbb{K}_{*}
. We need to check that the composition of the bottom row is the identity. But for
\mathcal{G}\in D(\{1\}\times Y)
,
\omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}i_{*}\mathcal{G}=u_{*}(E\boxtimes\mathcal{G})
where
u:\mathbb{G}_{m}\times Y\hookrightarrow\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y
is the open embedding
(t,y)\mapsto(t,ty)
. Restricting to
\{1\}\times Y
which is in the image of
u
, we get
i^{*}u_{*}(E\boxtimes\mathcal{G})\cong\mathcal{G}
.

To show the unit map
\textup{id}\to\mathbb{K}_{*}\circ\mathbb{K}^{*}
is an isomorphism, the argument is similar. We reduce to the case
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y
with a diagonal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Under the Fourier transform,
\Kir_{*}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)
is identified with
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\{0\})\times Y)
, embedded into
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y)
via
j_{*}
, where
j:(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\{0\})\times Y\to\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y
is the open embedding. Now use the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Kir_{*}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\psi,!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\textstyle{\Kir_{*}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\{0\})\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\textstyle{D(\{1\}\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},*}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\check{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\{0\})\times Y)}

The compositum of the top row is
\mathbb{K}_{*}\circ\mathbb{K}^{*}
, and the bottom row is clearly the identity.
∎

Similarly the functor

\textstyle{\mathbb{K}_{!}:D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\psi}}
\textstyle{D(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\mathbb{G}_{m},!}}
\textstyle{\Kir_{!}(X)}

is an equivalence with inverse (right adjoint)

\textstyle{\mathbb{K}^{!}:\Kir_{!}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\textstyle{D(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{\psi,*}}
\textstyle{D_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)}(X).}

2.3.
Alternative construction

We give an alternative construction of the Kirillov category that makes certain symmetries more transparent. The setup is the same as §
2.1
. This construction is only used in the rest of paper to make sense of integral transforms on Kirillov categories given by kernel sheaves that are
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic (as opposed to
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant), see §
2.3.9
. Such integral transforms will allow us to define Hecke symmetries on automorphic Kirillov categories (see §
6.4
and §
7.1.4
).

In this subsection alone, we work with cocomplete versions of sheaf categories (ind-constructible sheaves).

2.3.1.
Monodromic category

Let
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)\subset D(X)
be the full subcategory generated by the image of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
under the forgetful functor, i.e., unipotently monodromic complexes. By Verdier
[
50
, Section 5]
, every object
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
carries a unipotent action of the tame fundamental group
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
(unipotent means a topological generator acts unipotently). Moreover, all morphisms in
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
are
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
-equivariant. In other words, the identity functor of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
carries a unipotent action by
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{E}=\textup{Spf}\ \widehat{E[\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})]}
, where the completion is with respect to powers of the augmentation ideal of the group algebra
E[\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})]
. With a choice of a topological generator of
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
,
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
is isomorphic to the formal multiplicative group over
E
. The category of ind-coherent sheaves
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
is by construction equivalent to complexes of
E
-vector spaces with a unipotent action of
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
, under which convolution on
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
(using the group structure of
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
) becomes tensor product of
\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
-representations. We denote the convolution monoidal structure on
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
by

(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star).

The monoidal unit of this monoidal structure is the skyscraper sheaf
\delta_{E}
supported at the reduced point of
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
.

On the other hand,
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
carries another (symmetric) monoidal structure, given by the derived
!
-tensor product of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
-modules. The monoidal unit in this case is the dualizing sheaf
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
. We denote the resulting monoidal category by

(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}).

We claim that there is canonical action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
on
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
. Indeed, let
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
act by identity, its endomorphism ring
\textup{End}_{\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}})\cong\widehat{E[\pi^{t}_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})]}
acts on the identity functor of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
by monodromy as we discussed before, and these structures determine the action of an arbitrary object in
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
by writing it as a complex where terms are direct sums of
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
.

2.3.2.
Weak Kirillov category

Let
D_{\textup{Aff}\textup{-mon}}(X)\subset D(X)
be the full subcategory generated by the image of
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)
under the forgetful functor. It is also the full subcategory of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
consisting of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant objects.

The
weak Kirillov
category of
X
is defined as the quotient

\Kir^{w}(X):=D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)/D_{\textup{Aff}\textup{-mon}}(X).

We have the adjunction

(2.6)

\textstyle{\Kir(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Forg
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Kir^{w}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{*}}

where
Forg
is the functor forgetting
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariance, and
\textup{Av}_{*}
is the
*
-averaging with respect to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.

2.3.3.
Weak Kirillov category of
\mathbb{G}_{a}

Let
X=\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the standard action of
Aff
by translation and dilation, the category
\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
is the quotient of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
by constant complexes. Note that
\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
carries a monoidal structure under additive convolution on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
(we use the
!
-version).

Monodromic Fourier transform as in §
A.3
gives an equivalence
\mathrm{FT}^{w}:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
, which induces an equivalence

(2.7)

\Phi^{w}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{m}).

Taking stalk at
1\in\check{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
and recording the monodromy action, we obtain an equivalence

i^{*}_{1}:D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\cong\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}).

Consider the composition

\Psi:\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\Phi^{w}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}}D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\check{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\xrightarrow{i^{*}_{1}[-1]}\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}).

Note that we added a shift
[-1]
in the second step, so that the skyscraper
\delta\in\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
maps to the skyscraper object
E
supported at the reduced point of
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
. It is easy to see that
\Psi
is carries a canonical structure of a monoidal equivalence

\Psi:(\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a}),\star)\cong(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star).

2.3.4.

Back to the general setup of
X
with
Aff
-action. The action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
on
X
gives an action of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
(monoidal under additive convolution) on
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
. The action of the constant sheaf
\underline{E}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
on
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
results in
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant sheaves on
X
, therefore the action passes to the quotients to give an action of
(\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a}),\star)
on
\Kir^{w}(X)
. Through the equivalence
\Psi
, we get an action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
on
\Kir^{w}(X)
.

Next we spell out a compatibility structure between the actions of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
and
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
on
\Kir^{w}(X)
. For
\mathcal{K}\in\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})=\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
and
\mathcal{F}\in\Kir^{w}(X)
, the monodromy action of
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
on
\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{F}
is the same as the diagonal action on each factor: i.e., the following diagram is commutative (where
\mathrm{mon}_{\mathcal{K}}
denotes the monodromy action
\pi_{1}^{t}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\to\textup{Aut}(\mathcal{K})
)

(2.8)

\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{mon}_{\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\times\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{mon}_{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathrm{mon}_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\textup{Aut}(\mathcal{K})\times\textup{Aut}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\textup{Aut}(\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{F})}

and is functorial in
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{F}
.

This follows from the fact that the action map
\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X\to X
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. Now let

\alpha_{\star}:\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\Kir^{w}(X)\cong\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\otimes\Kir^{w}(X)\to\Kir^{w}(X)

be the action functor by convolution. Reformulating the monodromy action in terms of the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
-action, we note that
\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})\otimes\Kir^{w}(X)
carries an action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})\otimes(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})\cong(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}\times\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
. Let
m_{\mathfrak{G}}:\mathfrak{G}_{E}\times\mathfrak{G}_{E}\to\mathfrak{G}_{E}
be the multiplication, then the diagram (
2.8
) implies that
\alpha_{\star}
is equivariant under
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
, where it acts on the left side via the monoidal functor

(2.9)

m_{\mathfrak{G}}^{!}:(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})\to(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}^{2}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}).

2.3.5.
Categorical statement

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete dg category over
E
with an action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
. We may consider the
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
-equivariant category
\mathcal{C}^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}
equipped with adjoint functors

(2.10)

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Forg
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Av}_{!}}

The category
\mathcal{C}^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}
is defined as the totalization of the semi-cosimplicial diagram of dg categories

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\cdots}

where the functors use the co-action of
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
on
\mathcal{C}
(right adjoint to the action functor), and the
!
-pullback along the multiplication map of
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
. The monad
\textup{Forg}\circ\textup{Av}_{!}
acting on
\mathcal{C}
is given by the convolution
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(-)
. Note that
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
is an algebra object in
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
. Therefore,
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
can also be identified with the category of
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(-)
-modules in
\mathcal{C}
.

On the other hand, if
\mathcal{C}
is linear over
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
, i.e., it is equipped with an action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
, we may consider its fiber
\mathcal{C}_{1}
over the reduced point
1\in\mathfrak{G}_{E}
, which fits into the adjunction

(2.11)

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}

We say that the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
-action and the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
-action on
\mathcal{C}
are
compatible
if the convolution action functor

\alpha_{\star}:\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

is equivariant under the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
-actions, where it acts on the left side via the monoidal functor (
2.9
).

2.3.6 Example
.

Let
X
be a formal scheme of that is smooth over
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
, with structure map
\pi:X\to\mathfrak{G}_{E}
. In this situation, letting
\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{IndCoh}(X)
. Then
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
acts on
\mathcal{C}
where
\mathcal{K}\in\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})
acts by
\pi^{!}\mathcal{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}(-)
. Let
i:X_{1}\to X
be the closed embedding of the fiber over
1\in\mathfrak{G}_{E}
, then
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is identified with
\operatorname{IndCoh}(X_{1})
, and
i_{*}
and
i^{!}
have their usual meaning.

On the other hand, if
X
admits an action of
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
, we have an action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
on
\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{IndCoh}(X)
by convolution. If moreover the structure map
\pi
is
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
-equivariant (which implies that
X\cong\mathfrak{G}_{E}\times X_{1}
), then the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
and
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
-actions on
\mathcal{C}
are compatible.

In our application, we shall take
\mathcal{C}=\Kir^{w}(X)
with the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
-action coming from
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromy and the
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
-action by
\Kir^{w}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
. Then we may identify
\mathcal{C}_{1}
with
\Kir(X)
, so that the adjunction (
2.11
) becomes the adjunction (
2.6
).

2.3.7 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete dg category over
E
admitting compatible actions of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
and
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
. Then the composition

\Upsilon:\mathcal{C}_{1}\xrightarrow{i_{*}}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\textup{Av}_{!}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}

is fully faithful with right adjoint given by the composition

\Upsilon^{R}:\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\xrightarrow{\textup{Forg}}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{i^{!}}\mathcal{C}_{1}.

If moreover
\mathcal{C}
is generated by the image of
\mathcal{C}_{1}
under
i_{*}
under colimits, then
\Upsilon
and
\Upsilon^{R}
are inverse equivalences.

Proof.

The adjunction
(\Upsilon,\Upsilon^{R})
is the concatenation of the adjunctions (
2.10
) and (
2.11
). To show full-faithfulness of
\Upsilon
, we need to show that the unit map
\textup{id}_{\mathcal{C}_{1}}\to\Upsilon^{R}\circ\Upsilon
is an isomorphism. Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
, we need to show that the canonical map

(2.12)

\mathcal{F}\to i^{!}(\textup{Forg}\circ\textup{Av}_{!}(i_{*}\mathcal{F}))

is an isomorphism. Recall that the monad
\textup{Forg}\circ\textup{Av}_{!}
is given by the convolution with the algebra object
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\in(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
. The functor
i_{*}
being conservative, it suffices to show that

i_{*}\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}i^{!}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(i_{*}\mathcal{F}))

is an isomorphism. Note that
i_{*}i^{!}(-)\cong\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}(-)
where
\delta\in(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
is the skyscraper sheaf at
1
. We reduce to show that

(2.13)

i_{*}\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(i_{*}\mathcal{F})).

The compatibility between the actions of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
and
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
implies a canonical isomorphism

(2.14)

\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(i_{*}\mathcal{F}))\cong\alpha_{\star}(m^{!}_{\mathfrak{G}}\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F})).

Here we use
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\otimes}}
to denote the action of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}^{2}),\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\otimes}})
on
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\mathcal{C}
. Now
m^{!}_{\mathfrak{G}}\delta\cong\omega_{\Delta^{-}}
where
\Delta^{-}\subset\mathfrak{G}_{E}\times\mathfrak{G}_{E}
is the anti-diagonal. Consider
\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\mathcal{C}
as linear over
\mathfrak{G}_{E}^{2}
, the object
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F}
is scheme-theoretically supported on
\mathfrak{G}_{E}\times\{1\}
, which intersects the anti-diagonal transversally at
(1,1)\in\mathfrak{G}_{E}^{2}
. Therefore we have a canonical isomorphism
\omega_{\Delta^{-}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F})\cong\delta\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E})\otimes\mathcal{C}
, which implies

\alpha_{\star}(m^{!}_{\mathfrak{G}}\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F}))\cong\alpha_{\star}(\omega_{\Delta^{-}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\otimes}}(\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F}))\cong\alpha_{\star}\delta\boxtimes i_{*}\mathcal{F}\cong i_{*}\mathcal{F}.

Combined with (
2.14
) we get the desired isomorphism (
2.13
). We omit checking that the isomorphism thus obtained is the same as the one induced by the canonical map (
2.12
). This proves that
\Upsilon
is fully faithful.

If
\mathcal{C}
is generated by
i_{*}\mathcal{C}_{!}
, then
\Upsilon
is essentially surjective because
\mathcal{C}^{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}
is generated by the image of
\textup{Av}_{!}
under colimits. Therefore
\Upsilon
is an equivalence.
∎

Applying the above lemma to
\mathcal{C}=\Kir^{w}(X)
, we obtain:

2.3.8 Corollary
.

The functor

(2.15)

\Kir(X)\xrightarrow{\textup{Forg}}\Kir^{w}(X)\xrightarrow{\textup{Av}_{!}}\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}

is an equivalence with right adjoint

\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}\xrightarrow{\textup{Forg}}\Kir^{w}(X)\xrightarrow{\textup{Av}_{*}}\Kir(X).

Moreover, the category
\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}
is equivalent to the category of
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(-)
-modules in
\Kir^{w}(X)
.

2.3.9.
Integral transform for Kirillov categories

By Corollary
2.3.8
, we may henceforth use
\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}
as an alternative definition of the Kirillov category
\Kir(X)
. The advantage of this alternative definition is that it allows us to see more symmetry on the Kirillov category. A typical situation is the following. Consider a correspondence

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overleftarrow{c}}
\scriptstyle{\overrightarrow{c}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y}

in the category of stacks with
Aff
-action. We construct a functor

(2.16)

D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\backslash C)\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Kir(X),\Kir(Y))

as follows. For
\mathcal{K}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(C)
, we have the usual integral transform

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{K}}:=\overrightarrow{c}_{!}(\overleftarrow{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathcal{K}):D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)\to D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(Y).

When
\mathcal{K}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\backslash C)
, the functor
\widetilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{K}}
sends
D_{\textup{Aff}\textup{-mon}}(X)
to
D_{\textup{Aff}\textup{-mon}}(Y)
, hence inducing a functor

\phi^{w}_{\mathcal{K}}:\Kir^{w}(X)\to\Kir^{w}(Y)

equivariant under the actions of
(\operatorname{IndCoh}(\mathfrak{G}_{E}),\star)
. Passing to
\mathfrak{G}_{E}
-equivariant objects, it induces a functor

\phi_{\mathcal{K}}:\Kir(X)\cong\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}\to\Kir^{w}(Y)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}\cong\Kir(Y).

Here we apply Corollary
2.3.8
to
X
and
Y
. The construction of (
2.16
) is compatible with compositions of correspondences in the obvious sense.

2.3.10 Remark
.

Let
\Kir^{w}(X)^{\heartsuit}\subset\Kir^{w}(X)
be the heart of the perverse t-structure. We also equip
\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}
with the t-structure pulled back from
\textup{Forg}:\Kir^{w}(X)^{(\mathfrak{G}_{E},\star)}\to\Kir^{w}(X)
, whose heart is equivalent to
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(-)
-modules in
\Kir^{w}(X)^{\heartsuit}
(note that
\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\star(-)
is a t-exact endofunctor of
\Kir^{w}(X)
). One can check that the equivalence (
2.15
) is t-exact. We thus get an equivalence of abelian categories

(2.17)

\Kir(X)^{\heartsuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\omega_{\mathfrak{G}_{E}}\mathrm{-mod}(\Kir^{w}(X)^{\heartsuit}).

When
X
is a scheme of finite type over
k
, the derived equivalence (
2.15
) can be obtained from the abelian category equivalence (
2.17
) by passing to the derived categories of both sides and using Beilinson’s theorem
[
4
, Theorem 1.3]
.

2.4.
Relevant locus

Let
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:T^{*}X\to(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})^{*}
be the moment map for the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
T^{*}X
. If
x
is the coordinate on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
, then
dx
gives a nonzero element in
(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})^{*}
, which we denote by
1
.

We define the Hamiltonian reduction of
T^{*}X
under the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action with respect to the moment value
1
as

T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}:=\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(1)/\mathbb{G}_{a}.

2.4.1 Definition
.

The
relevant locus
for the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
X
is the image of the projection
T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(1)\to X
(a constructible substack of
X
). By abuse of language we also call the image of the projection
T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}\to X/\mathbb{G}_{a}
the relevant locus. A geometric point in the relevant locus of
X
is called a
relevant point
; otherwise it is called
irrelevant
.

2.4.2 Lemma
.

A geometric point
x\in X(K)
(where
K
is an algebraically closed field) is relevant if and only if the homomorphism

(2.18)

\textup{Aut}_{X/\mathbb{G}_{a}}(x)\to\mathbb{G}_{a,K}

is trivial, i.e., the stabilizer of
x
under
\mathbb{G}_{a}
is trivial.

Equivalently,
x
is irrelevant if and only if the homomorphism (
2.18
) is nonzero, i.e.,
\mathbb{G}_{a}
fixes
x
.

Proof.

By definition,
x
is relevant if and only if the restriction of the moment map
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
to its cotangent fiber
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a},x}:T^{*}_{x}X\to(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})_{K}
is nonzero. Dually,
x
is relevant if and only if the infinitesimal action map
(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})_{K}\to T_{x}X
is nonzero (here
T_{x}X
is the degree zero cohomology of the tangent complex of
X
at
x
). This happens if and only if the stabilizer of
x
under
\mathbb{G}_{a}
has
0
Lie algebra, which means the stabilizer is trivial.
∎

2.4.3 Lemma
.

Let
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
be a locally closed
Aff
-stable smooth substacks of
X
such that all geometric points in
X\backslash Y
are irrelevant. Then

(1)

Both functors
i_{!}:\Kir(Y)\to\Kir(X)
and
i_{*}:\Kir(Y)\to\Kir(X)
are equivalences.

(2)

The natural transformation
i_{!}\to i_{*}:\Kir(Y)\to\Kir(X)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Decomposing
i
as a closed embedding followed by an open embedding, we are reduced to treat the case where
i
is either a closed embedding or an open embedding. The proof for both cases follows along the same lines, so in the following assume we are in either of the two cases.

Now let
X^{\prime}=X-Y
, which consists of irrelevant points. We first show that
\Kir(X^{\prime})=0
. Indeed, let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X^{\prime})
. Since all points in
U
are irrelevant, by (
2.4.2
), all points in
U
are fixed by
\mathbb{G}_{a}
. This implies that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant (which is a property since
\mathbb{G}_{a}
is contractible), hence its
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure extends uniquely to an
Aff
-equivariant structure, which implies
\mathcal{F}
becomes zero in
\Kir(X^{\prime})=D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X^{\prime})/D_{\textup{Aff}}(X^{\prime})
.

We prove (2). For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)
, we have a distinguished triangle
i_{!}\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}\mathcal{F}\to k_{*}k^{*}i_{*}\mathcal{F}
where
k:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
. Since
k^{*}i_{*}\mathcal{F}\in\Kir(X^{\prime})=0
, we see that
i_{!}\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}\mathcal{F}
becomes an isomorphism in
\Kir(X)
.

We prove (1). Since
i_{!}
and
i_{*}
are full embeddings, it suffices to show that they are essentially surjective. For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
, we have a distinguished triangle
i_{!}i^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\to k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{F}
. Since
k^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\Kir(X^{\prime})=0
, we conclude that
i_{!}i^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
becomes an isomorphism in
\Kir(X)
. The same argument shows that
\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}\mathcal{F}
becomes an isomorphism in
\Kir(X)
.

∎

2.4.4 Corollary
.

Suppose
\{Y_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is a finite collection of disjoint, locally closed
Aff
-stable smooth substacks of
X
. Assume there exists an
Aff
-invariant stratification
X=\sqcup_{\alpha\in I}X_{\alpha}
such that
Y_{\alpha}\subset X_{\alpha}
and all geometric points in
X_{\alpha}\backslash Y_{\alpha}
are irrelevant (here by stratification we mean each
X_{\alpha}
is locally closed and smooth,
I
has a partial order such that
\overline{X}_{\alpha}=\cup_{\beta\leq\alpha}X_{\beta}
for all
\alpha\in I
). Then the images of
i_{\alpha!}:\Kir(Y_{\alpha})\to\Kir(X)
generate
\Kir(X)
, and
\Kir(X)
admits a semi-orthogonal decomposition into
\{\Kir(Y_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in I}
.

Proof.

Let
j_{\alpha}:X_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion. Since
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
form an
Aff
-invariant stratification of
X
, the images of
j_{\alpha!}:\Kir(X_{\alpha})\to\Kir(X)
generate
\Kir(X)
, and
\Kir(X)
admits a semi-orthogonal decomposition into
\{\Kir(X_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in I}
. By Lemma
2.4.3
, the
!
-extension functors
\Kir(Y_{\alpha})\to\Kir(X_{\alpha})
are equivalences, hence the same statements are true if
\Kir(X_{\alpha})
is replaced with
\Kir(Y_{\alpha})
for each
\alpha\in I
.
∎

3.
Recollections on microsheaves

In this section we assume the base field
k=\mathbb{C}
, and work with the analytic topology on various smooth complex varieties. We will review the basics of microsheaves, and recall a few properties which we will have use for later, as well as some general context and motivation. None of this material is original.

3.1.
Microsheaves

3.1.1.
Microsheaves on cotangent bundles

We follow
[
32
]
and
[
42
]
to recall the formalism of microsheaves.

Let
N
be a smooth complex manifold, and let
\mathrm{Sh}(N)
be the dg category of unbounded complexes of sheaves on
N
. For any subset
\Lambda\subset T^{*}N
, let
\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda}(N)
be the full subcategory of sheaves with singular support contained in
\Lambda
. For an open subset
\Omega\subset T^{*}N
(with respect to the usual topology on a complex manifold), let
\mathrm{Sh}(N;\Omega)
be the quotient
\mathrm{Sh}(N)/\mathrm{Sh}_{T^{*}N-\Omega}(N)
. The assignment
\mu\mathrm{Sh}^{pre}:\Omega\mapsto\mathrm{Sh}(N;\Omega)
is a presheaf of stable
\infty
-categories. Let
\Omega\mapsto\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\Omega)
be the sheafification of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}^{pre}
. It is a sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories.

For any open
\Omega\subset T^{*}N
, an object
\mathcal{F}\in\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\Omega)
has a well-defined singular support
SS(\mathcal{F})
, which is a conical subset in
\Omega
. For a subset
\Lambda\subset T^{*}N
and open
\Omega\subset T^{*}N
, we let
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda}(\Omega)
denote the category of sections of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\Omega)
with singular support in
\Lambda\cap\Omega
.

For
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Sh}(N)
, in
[
32
]
a complex of sheaves
\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
on
T^{*}N
is defined. We have

\textup{Hom}_{\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\Omega)}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=\textup{H}^{0}({\Omega,\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})}).

The support of
\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is contained in
SS(\mathcal{F})\cap SS(\mathcal{G})
.

3.1.2.
Extension to stacks

We refer to
[
43
, Section 4]
for more details on microsheaves on a stack. We will only need the case of a global quotient
\mathfrak{X}=[N/G]
where
N
is a smooth scheme over
\mathbb{C}
. In this case,
T^{*}\mathfrak{X}=[\mu^{-1}(0)/G]
where
\mu:T^{*}N\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is the moment map for the
G
-action on
N
. For
\Omega\subset\mu^{-1}(0)
that is
G
-invariant, we can still form
\mu\mathrm{Sh}^{pre}(\Omega/G):=\mathrm{Sh}(\mathfrak{X})/\mathrm{Sh}_{\mu^{-1}(0)-\Omega}(\mathfrak{X})
.

3.1.3 Definition
.

Define a sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
over the stack
T^{*}\mathfrak{X}
by sheafifying the above presheaf. We denote its global section category by
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(T^{*}\mathfrak{X})=\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mu^{-1}(0)/G)
.

3.1.4.
Microsheaves on exact symplectic manifolds

An exact sympectic manifold is a (smooth, real) manifold
X
equipped with a real one-form
\lambda
, such that
\omega=d\lambda
is a symplectic form. The image of
\lambda
under the isomorphism of vector bundles
T^{*}X\to TX
defined by
\omega
is called the Liouville vector-field. The flow of this vector field is called the Liouville flow.

3.1.5 Example
.

Let
N
be a manifold, and let
X=T^{*}N
be equipped with the usual symplectic structure. The action of
\mathbb{R}^{>0}
dilating the cotangent fibers is the Liouville flow for the canonical Liouville form, which we denote
\lambda_{N}
.

Exact symplectic manifolds bear a close relation to contact manifolds. To an exact symplectic manifold
(X,\lambda)
, we associate the contact manifold
(Y,\alpha)=(X\times\mathbb{R},\lambda+dt)
.
2
2

2

One can also build a symplectic manifold from a contact manifold, though we will not need to do so here.

We will consider contact manifolds
(Y,\alpha)
and symplectic manifolds
(X,\omega)
equipped with an extra structure called
Maslov data
. This structure endows them with sheaves of categories locally modeled on the microsheaf categories defined by by Kashiwara and Schapira in
[
32
]
. We briefly sketch the definition of Maslov data below; a good reference for this material is
[
21
, Section 3]
.

3.1.6 Remark
.

We will use the same notation
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
, and the same word microsheaves, for the sheaves of categories on a contact or symplectic manifold. As we recall below, there is a simple relationship between the two, and in practice this should not cause any confusion.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable presentable symmetric monoidal category (eventually, this will be the category of coefficients for our microsheaves, and we may take it to be dg
\mathbb{Z}
-modules or dg
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces).

3.1.7 Definition
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a real symplectic manifold. There is a canonical sequence of maps

(3.1)

X\to\operatorname{B}\operatorname{U}\to\operatorname{B}(\operatorname{U}/\operatorname{O})\to\operatorname{B}^{2}\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{C})

where the first map classifies the stable tangent bundle to
X
(viewed as a symplectic bundle), the second map is the delooping of the quotient map
\operatorname{U}\to\operatorname{U}/\operatorname{O}
, and the last map is induced by a de-looping of the
J
-homomorphism.
Maslov data
for
(X,\lambda)
is a choice of null-homotopy of the resulting map
X\to\operatorname{B}^{2}\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{C})
.

We will denote such a choice of nullhomotopy by the variable
\eta
below.

3.1.8 Example
.

When
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{Z}-\operatorname{mod}
,
\operatorname{B}^{2}\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{C})
is given by
\operatorname{B}^{2}(\mathbb{Z})\oplus\operatorname{B}^{3}(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
. A choice of Maslov data is canonically identified with a choice of trivialization of the complex line bundle
\det(T_{\mathbb{C}}X)^{\otimes 2}
and a class in
H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
.

Maslov data over other commutative rings
R
can then be obtained by composition with
\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbb{Z}-\operatorname{mod})\to\operatorname{Pic}(R-\operatorname{mod})
.

Maslov data for contact manifolds is defined similarly to the symplectic case, where the contact distribution plays the role of the tangent bundle. In particular, Maslov data for an exact symplectic manifold
(X,\lambda)
determines Maslov data for
(X\times\mathbb{R},\lambda+dt)
.

One natural way of producing Maslov data is as follows.

3.1.9 Definition
.

A stable polarization of
X
is a section of the Lagrangian Grassmanian bundle
\operatorname{LGr}(TX\oplus\mathbb{R}^{2n})\to X
for some
n\geq 0
.

We consider stable polarizations up to homotopy: they are classified by null-homotopies of the composition

(3.2)

X\to\operatorname{B}\operatorname{U}\to\operatorname{B}(\operatorname{U}/\operatorname{O})

of the first two maps in Equation
3.1
. A choice of stable polarization defines Maslov data for
X
by composition.

3.1.10 Example
.

If
X=T^{*}N
is a cotangent bundle, then it carries a natural polarization, and thus natural Maslov data, which we denote
\eta_{N}
. Similarly, if
Y=S^{*}N
is the cosphere bundle of a manifold
N
, equipped with the canonical contact form
\alpha_{N}
, then it carries a natural (contact) polarization, and thus natural Maslov data, which we also denote
\eta_{N}
.

The papers
[
47
]
and
[
42
, Theorem 1.1]
endow any contact manifold
(Y,\alpha)
equipped with a choice of Maslov data
(Y,\alpha,\eta)
with a sheaf of categories
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
, which we call microsheaves.

Suppose
(Y,\alpha,\eta)=(S^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n},\alpha_{N},\eta_{N})
. Then the stalk of this sheaf at
p\in S^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the category

(3.3)

\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathrm{Sh}_{S^{*}\setminus p}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

where
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
denotes sheaves on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with coefficients in
\mathcal{C}
