. This is essentially the category defined by Kashiwara and Schapira in
[
32
]
, except that one works here with coefficients in a presentable category, rather than with bounded complexes.

The same is true, non-canonically, for general
(Y,\alpha,\eta)
. Any homotopy of Maslov data
\eta\to\eta^{\prime}
induces an isomorphism on microsheaves. On the other hand, by the contact version of Darboux’s theorem,
(Y,\alpha)
is locally isomorphic, as a contact manifold, to the cosphere bundle
S^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}
of a vector space. Thus the sheaf
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
is always locally isomorphic to (
3.3
).

3.1.11 Definition
.

Given an exact symplectic manifold equipped with a choice of Maslov data
(X,\lambda,\eta)
, we consider the associated contact manifold
(X\times\mathbb{R},\lambda+dt,\eta)
equipped with the induced Maslov data. We then define
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
on
X
as the pullback of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
on
X\times\mathbb{R}
along the natural embedding
i_{0}:X\to X\times 0\subset X\times\mathbb{R}
.

We call an object of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(X)
a
microsheaf
.

3.1.12 Proposition
.

The support of a microsheaf is a closed coisotropic subset of
X
stable under the Liouville flow.

Given any subset
S\subset X
, we can consider the subsheaf
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{S}\subset\mu\mathrm{Sh}
of objects supported on
S
.

3.1.13 Remark
.

One could also pull back
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
on
X\times\mathbb{R}
along the graph of any function
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
; this corresponds to shifting the Liouville form by
df
.

3.1.14 Remark
.

The dependence of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
on the Maslov data
\eta
is mild: any isotopy
\eta\to\eta^{\prime}
induces an equivalence on microsheaves. On the other hand, the stalks of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
can depend quite non-trivially on the form
\lambda
.

One of the main results of
[
42
]
is that in good situations, the global sections of
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
along the so-called
core

\mathfrak{c}(X,\lambda)\subset X
are invariant with respect to (sufficiently nice) exact isotopies of
\lambda
.

3.1.15 Proposition
.

Any isomorphism of
(X,\lambda,\eta)
with an open subset
\Omega\subset(T^{*}N,\lambda_{N},\eta_{N})
identifies
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
with the sheaf defined in Section
3.1.1
.

3.1.16.
Comparing two constructions of microsheaves

Suppose a Lie group
G
acts on a manifold
N
, preserving an open subset
\Omega\subset T^{*}N
, and let
X=\Omega/\!\!/_{\theta}G=\mu^{-1}(\theta)/G
be a symplectic reduction at some moment value
\theta\in(\mathfrak{g}^{*})^{G}
. The cotangent polarization on
T^{*}N
restricts to
\Omega
.

The pullback
\pi^{*}TX
is the middle cohomology of the sequence
\mathfrak{g}\to T\Omega\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}
defined by the group action and moment map. As explained by Nadler and Shende in
[
43
]
, any choice of
G
-equivariant splitting
T\Omega\cong\pi^{*}TX\oplus\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*}
identifies
G
-equivariant stable polarizations of
\Omega
and stable polarizations of the quotient.

3.1.17 Proposition
.

[
43
]

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a complex exact symplectic manifold, which occurs as an open subset of
T^{*}\mathfrak{X}=T^{*}(X/G)
. Endow
W
with the quotient polarization, with associated Maslov data
\eta
. Then Definition
3.1.11
) and Definition
3.1.3
agree after restriction to
W
.

3.1.18.
Objects from smooth lagrangians

3.1.19 Definition
.

Let
(X,\lambda,\eta)
be an exact symplectic manifold equipped with Maslov data. Let
L\subset X
be a smooth Lagrangian submanifold, and let
\Omega\cong T^{*}L
be a Weinstein neighborhood of
L
.
Secondary Maslov data
for
L
is a choice of homotopy from the cotangent Maslov data on
T^{*}L
to the restriction
\eta|_{\Omega}
.

3.1.20 Proposition
.

[
42
]

Any Lagrangian submanifold
L\subset X
such that
\lambda|_{L}=0
, equipped with secondary Maslov data, defines a fully faithful functor
\textup{Loc}(L)\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(X)
.

The condition
\lambda|_{L}=0
can be relaxed to
\lambda|_{L}=df
under certain conditions : see Theorem
3.2.4
.

3.2.
Microsheaves and Fukaya categories

In this section, we recall the relationship between microsheaves and the Fukaya category. This material is motivational, and will not be used anywhere else in this paper.

An exact symplectic manifold
X
is called Liouville if the Liouville flow is complete, and
X
is convex, in the sense that it admits an exhaustion
\{X^{k}\subset X|k\in\mathbb{N}\}
by compact submanifolds with boundaries, such that the Liouville vector field is outward pointing along each boundary. We call such a pair
(X^{k},\lambda)
a
Liouville domain
.

3.2.1 Definition
.

The core
\mathfrak{c}(X,\lambda)\subset X
(sometimes called the skeleton) of a Liouville manifold
(X,\lambda)
is the maximal flow-invariant subset of
X^{k}
(for
k\gg 0)
which is disjoint from
\partial X^{k}
.

This does not depend on the choice of exhaustion. The core is a compact subset of
X
.

3.2.2 Proposition
.

Any compactly supported object in
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(X)
is supported in
\mathfrak{c}(X,\lambda)
.

3.2.3 Definition
.

Let
\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda)=\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathfrak{c}(X,\lambda)}(X,\lambda)
.

A Liouville form
\lambda
is
sufficiently Weinstein
if
\mathfrak{c}(X,\lambda)
is sufficiently isotropic
[
43
, Definition 9.10]
. This holds, for example, if it is Whitney-stratified by isotropic submanifolds. There is a corresponding notion of sufficiently Weinstein homotopies between sufficiently Weinstein Liouville manifolds.

It is proven in
[
43
]
that a sufficiently Weinstein homotopy
\lambda\to\lambda^{\prime}
induces an equivalence of
\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda)
with
\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda^{\prime})
. One consequence of this is the following.

3.2.4 Theorem
.

[
43
, Theorem 1.2]

Let
L
be a compact exact Lagrangian submanifold of the sufficiently Weinstein manifold
(X,\lambda,\eta)
, equipped with secondary Maslov data. There is a fully faithful functor
\operatorname{Loc}(L)\to\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda)
.

3.2.5 Theorem
.

[
29
, Theorem 1.4]

Let
(X,\lambda,\eta)
be a Liouville manifold admitting homological cocores.
3
3

3

We refer to
[
29
]
for a definition of homological cocores.

Let
\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda)^{c}
denote the category of compact objects. Then there is a canonical equivalence of dg categories

\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda)^{c}\cong\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(X,\lambda)^{\operatorname{op}}

where
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(X,\lambda)
is the (opposite) category of the idempotent-completed pre-triangulated closure of the wrapped Fukaya category of
X
.

3.3.
Microlocalization of the Kirillov category

We are back in the situation of §
2.1
, where we further assume that
X=[N/G]
is a quotient of a smooth scheme
N
over
\mathbb{C}
by an action of a group
G
. We further suppose that the
G
-action extends to an action of a group
\widetilde{G}
that fits into an exact sequence
1\to G\to\widetilde{G}\to\textup{Aff}\to 1
.

Let
\mathfrak{Aff}
be the Lie algebra of
Aff
. Let
\mathfrak{g}_{m}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{a}
be the Lie algebras of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\mathbb{G}_{a}
respectively. We have a decomposition
\mathfrak{Aff}^{*}=\mathfrak{g}_{a}^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*}\cong\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}
(as vector spaces).

Let
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}:T^{*}X\to\mathfrak{g}_{a}^{*},\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:T^{*}X\to\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*}
and
\mu_{\textup{Aff}}:T^{*}X\to\mathfrak{Aff}^{*}
be the moment maps for the
\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
Aff
-actions.

Consider the microlocalization functor

(3.4)

D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(T^{*}(X/\mathbb{G}_{m}))=\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(0)/\mathbb{G}_{m}).

For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)
, we have
SS(\mathcal{F})\subset\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(0)
. Therefore, restricting to the complement of the zero fiber of
\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
induces a functor

(3.5)

\Kir(X)=D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)/D_{\textup{Aff}}(X)\hookrightarrow\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mu_{\textup{Aff}}^{-1}((\mathfrak{g}_{a}^{*}\backslash\{0\})\times\{0\})/\mathbb{G}_{m}).

Note that the coadjoint action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})^{*}
is by scaling, we have an isomorphism

\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}((\mathfrak{g}_{a}^{*}\backslash\{0\})\times\{0\})/\mathbb{G}_{m}\cong\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0).

As an open substack of the Hamiltonian reduction
T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, this also equips
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
with an exact symplectic structure. Combining with (
3.5
), we get a functor

(3.6)

\Kir(X)\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)).

3.3.1 Lemma
.

The inclusion
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)\hookrightarrow\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(1)\subset T^{*}X
induces an isomorphism

(3.7)

\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)\cong T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}.

Proof.

We have
\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(1)=\mu^{-1}(1\times\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*})\subset T^{*}X
. The coadjoint action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
on
1\times\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*}
is by translation on the
\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*}
-factor. Therefore we may identify
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1\times\mathfrak{g}_{m}^{*})/\mathbb{G}_{a}
with
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
.
∎

Combining the isomorphism (
3.7
) with (
3.5
), we get a microlocalization functor

M:\Kir(X)\hookrightarrow\mu\mathrm{Sh}(T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}).

For
\mathcal{F}\in\Kir(X)
that is the image of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
,
SS(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})\cap\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
and depends only on
\mathcal{F}
; we denote it by
SS(\mathcal{F})
, which is a conic subset of
T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

For
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\Kir(X)
that come from objects
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
, we have the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant sheaf
\mu\mathrm{hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})
on
\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(0)
. Restricting
\mu\mathrm{hom}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})
to
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
we get a sheaf on
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)\cong T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}
which is independent of the liftings
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
. We denote this restriction by
\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
, understood as a sheaf on
T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}
. Then we have

\textup{Hom}(M(\mathcal{F}),M(\mathcal{G}))=\textup{H}^{0}({T^{*}X\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})}).

3.3.2 Lemma
.

The functor
M
is conservative.

Proof.

We need to show that if
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X)
and
M(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})=0
then
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant (which is a property rather than a structure, since
\mathbb{G}_{a}
is contractible). Equivalently, we must show
\textup{H}^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is constant along each
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-orbit for all
i
. The microstalk functor at a point
p\in T^{*}X
is a factors through
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\Omega)
for any open
\Omega
containing
p
. Since
M(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})=0
, the microstalks of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
vanish along
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
. Thus the singular support
SS(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant subset of
T^{*}X
that does not intersect
\mu^{-1}_{\textup{Aff}}(1,0)
. By the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariance, we conclude that
SS(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})\subset\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(0)
.

We need to show that
\textup{H}^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is constant along each
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-orbit for all
i
.
Let
a:\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X\to X
be the action map and
p_{X}:\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X\to X
be the projection. The map
da:(T^{*}X)\times_{X,a}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X)\to T^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X)=T^{*}\mathbb{G}_{a}\times T^{*}X
takes the form
(t,x,\xi)\mapsto(t,\mu_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(x,\xi),x,\xi)
where
t\in\mathbb{G}_{a},x\in X
and
\xi\in T_{x}^{*}X
. The condition
SS(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})\subset\mu^{-1}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}(0)
implies that
SS(a^{*}\mathcal{F})
, which lies in the image of
da(SS(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})\times_{X,a}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X))
, is contained in
\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\{0\}\times T^{*}X
, which is the image of
dp_{X}:T^{*}X\times_{X,p_{X}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X)\to T^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times X)
. Now apply
[
32
, Proposition 5.4.5]
to the projection
p_{X}
. We conclude that
\textup{H}^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is locally constant along fibers of
p_{X}
, which implies that
\textup{H}^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
is constant along
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-orbits.
∎

4.
Affine Springer fiber and Hitchin moduli space

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field.

4.1.
Homogeneous affine Springer fiber with slope one

Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
k
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and flag variety
\mathcal{B}
. Let
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}
be a regular semisimple element. Then
T=C_{G}(\psi_{1})
is a maximal torus. Let
\Phi=\Phi(G,T)
be the set of roots of
G
with respect to
T
, and
W
the Weyl group. For
\alpha\in\Phi
, let
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be the corresponding root space.

Let
G(\!(t)\!)
be the loop group of
G
with formal parameter
t
. Let
\mathbf{G}=G[\![t]\!]
be the formal arc group of
G
. In other words,
G(\!(t)\!)(R)=G(R(\!(t)\!))
and
\mathbf{G}(R)=G(R[\![t]\!])
for
k
-algebras
R
. Let
\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{G}
be the affine Grassmannian of
G
.

Let
B\subset G
be a Borel subgroup containing
T
, and
\mathbf{I}\subset\mathbf{G}
the corresponding Iwahori subgroup. Then
\textup{Fl}=G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}
is the affine flag variety of
G
. Let
\Phi^{+}\subset\Phi
be the subset of positive roots defined by
B
.

Write
\psi=t\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)
. This element defines its affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
in the affine flag variety
Fl
and
\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
in the affine Grassmannian
Gr
as in
[
33
]
:

\displaystyle\textup{Fl}_{\psi}=\{g\in\textup{Fl}=G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}_{0}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1})(\psi)\in\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{I}_{0}\};

\displaystyle\textup{Gr}_{\psi}=\{g\in\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{G}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1})(\psi)\in\mathfrak{g}[\![t]\!]\}.

Using the terminology of
[
45
, §3.1]
, the element
\psi
is a homogeneous element of slope
1
in the loop Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)
.

4.1.1.
Geometric properties

By the dimension formula of Kazhdan-Lusztig-Bezrukavnikov
[
33
]
and
[
6
]
, we have

\dim\textup{Fl}_{\psi}=\dim\textup{Gr}_{\psi}=|\Phi^{+}|=\dim\mathcal{B}.

Moreover,
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and
\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
are known to be equidimensional. Now
\mathcal{B}=G/B\subset\textup{Fl}
is contained in
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
, therefore it is an irreducible component of
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.

4.1.2.
Symmetry

Since
\psi
has centralizer
T(\!(t)\!)
in
G(\!(t)\!)
,
T(\!(t)\!)
acts on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and
\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
by left translation. In particular, we get a natural action of the lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and
\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
:
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
acts by left translation by
t^{\lambda}
.

4.1.3.
Hessenberg varieties

Let
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
be the one-dimension torus acting on
G(\!(t)\!),\textup{Fl},\textup{Gr}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\!(t)\!)
by scaling
t
. Then
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
preserves
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
and
\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
. We describe the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-fixed points following
[
45
, §5.4]
.

The fixed points of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
on
Fl
are of the form
Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Let

\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)=\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\cap(Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}).

Note that
Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}\cong G/B_{\lambda}
, and
B_{\lambda}=G\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})\mathbf{I}
is a Borel subgroup of
G
containing
T
, whose roots under
T
are

\{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}|\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\leq 0\}\cup\{\alpha\in\Phi^{-}|\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\leq-1\}.

In particular,
Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}
is isomorphic to the flag variety of
G
. Let

V_{\lambda}^{+}=\mathfrak{t}\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Phi^{+},\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\leq 1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Phi^{-},\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\leq 0}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\right).

This is an
\textup{Ad}(B_{\lambda})
-submodule of
\mathfrak{g}
containing
\mathfrak{b}_{\lambda}=\textup{Lie}\ B_{\lambda}
. Direct calculation shows that
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)\subset G/B_{\lambda}
is the closed subscheme defined by

\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)=\{gB_{\lambda}\in G/B_{\lambda}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1})\psi_{1}\in V_{\lambda}^{+}\}.

These are examples of Hessenberg varieties. In particular,
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(0)=\mathcal{B}
is an irreducible component of
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.

Under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action on the affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
, the attracting locus to
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
is
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\cap(\mathbf{G}t^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I})
, which is an affine space bundle over
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
.

We give some examples of Hessenberg varieties that appear as fixed points of
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.

4.1.4 Example
.

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
satisfies

\lambda
is regular and
\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\neq 1
for all
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}
,

then
V_{\lambda}^{+}=\mathfrak{b}_{\lambda}
, and
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
is canonically isomorphic to the Springer fiber of the regular semisimple element
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{g}
. We conclude that in this case,
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
consists of points
wt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}
, where
w\in W
.

4.1.5 Example
.

Consider the case
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
and
T
the diagonal torus, in which case we can write
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Let
\mu_{1}<\cdots<\mu_{r}
be the different values of
\lambda_{i}
, with
\mu_{1}
appearing
n_{1}
times,
\mu_{2}
appearing
n_{2}
times, etc.

Then under a suitable basis of
k^{n}
,
B_{\lambda}
is the group of upper triangular matrices in
\textup{GL}_{n}
, we may arrange that
V_{\lambda}^{+}
consists of block matrices with blocks of sizes
n_{1},n_{2},\cdots,n_{r}

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}*&*&*&*\\
?&*&*&*\\
0&?&*&*\\
0&0&?&*\end{array}\right)

Here
*
denotes an arbitrary block, while
?
(at the
(i+1,i)
-block) is arbitrary only when
\mu_{i}+1=\mu_{i+1}
; otherwise
?
is the zero block.

4.1.6 Example
.

Consider the case
G=\textup{GL}_{3}
and
\psi_{1}
is a regular diagonal element. Let
\lambda=(-1,0,1)
. In this case,
V_{\lambda}^{+}
takes the form
\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}*&*&*\\
&*&*\\
0&*&*\end{array}\right)
. We have

\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)\cong\{V_{1}\subset V_{2}\subset V=k^{3}|\psi_{1}(V_{1})\subset V_{2}\}.

By convention,
\dim V_{i}=i
. Forgetting
V_{1}
gives a projection

\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)\to\mathbb{P}^{2}=\{V_{2}\subset V\}.

This realizes
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
as the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
at three points
[1:0:0],[0:1:0]
and
[0:0:1]
, which correspond exactly to the three planes in
V
that are stable under
\psi_{1}
.

4.2.
Hitchin moduli space

We review a special case of the construction from
[
9
, §2.5-2.8]
, where we define a symplectic variety
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
containing the affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
as a conic Lagrangian.

4.2.1.
Global setup

Let
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}
with affine coordinate
t
. The two points
0
and
\infty
will play essential roles in the following discussion. To avoid confusion between the point
0\in X
and the number zero, we use
\underline{0}
to denote the point in
X
when necessary.

We again denote by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
the one-dimensional torus acting on
X
by scaling the coordinate
t
.

The loop group
G(\!(t)\!)
, arc group
\mathbf{G}
and Iwahori
\mathbf{I}
are now attached to the formal disk of
X
at
0
, with uniformizer
t
. To emphasize that, we sometimes denote
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{I}
by
\mathbf{G}_{0}
and
\mathbf{I}_{0}
.

4.2.2.
Moduli of bundles

We use
\tau=t^{-1}
as the uniformizer of the local ring of
X
at
\infty
. Let
\mathbf{G}_{\infty}=G[\![\tau]\!]
be the formal arc group of
G
at
\infty\in X
. Let
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\ker(\textup{ev}_{\infty}:\mathbf{G}_{\infty}\to G)
be the first congruence subgroup at
\infty
. Let
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
be the moduli stack of
G
-bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{I}_{0}
level structures
\infty
and
0
respectively. Then
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
classifies
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B})
where
\mathcal{E}
is a
G
-bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
\tau_{\infty}
a trivialization of the fiber
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{0,B}
a
B
-reduction of the fiber
\mathcal{E}_{0}
.

4.2.3.
Moduli of Higgs bundles

Let
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
be the classical cotangent bundle of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. It classifies
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B},\varphi)
where
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B})\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
and
\varphi
is a section of the twisted coadjoint bundle
\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{X}(\infty+\underline{0})
such that
\textup{Res}_{0}\varphi\in\mathcal{E}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{b}^{\bot})
, the vector space associated to the
B
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{0,B}
at
\underline{0}
and the
B
-module
\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Under the Killing form of
\mathfrak{g}
,
\mathfrak{b}^{\bot}
is identified with
\mathfrak{n}
. Since
\omega_{X}(\infty+\underline{0})
has a canonical trivialization by
dt/t
, we may identify
\varphi
with a section of
\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})
whose value at
\underline{0}
lies in
\mathcal{E}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{n})
.

We may view
\psi
as an additive character of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
:

(4.1)

\psi:\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\mod t^{-2}}t^{-1}\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{t}\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\psi_{1}}\mathbb{G}_{a}.

Let
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\subset\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
be the kernel of the above character. Let
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})=T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Then
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
carries an action of
\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
with
\mathbb{G}_{a}
acting as
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
by loop rotation that acts on the tangent space at
\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
by scaling. Let

(4.2)

\mathcal{M}_{\psi}:=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}

be the shifted Hamiltonian reduction of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
classifies
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B},\varphi)
where
\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
,
\varphi\in\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty+\underline{0})
satisfies

•

Near
\infty
,
\varphi
is allowed to have a double pole, so
\varphi\in\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})dt
. Then we require
\varphi\in\psi_{1}dt+\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})dt/t
. To make sense of “the leading term is equal to
\psi_{1}
”, we use the trivialization
\tau_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}
.

•

\textup{Res}_{0}\varphi\in\mathcal{E}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{n})
, i.e., near
0
we have
\varphi\in\mathcal{E}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{n})dt/t+\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})dt
.

Equivalently, we may consider the next congruence subgroup
\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2}=\ker(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\to\tau\mathfrak{g})
, and
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})=T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Then

\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\cong\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{\psi}\mathfrak{g}.

Here we use the natural action of
\mathfrak{g}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty}
on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
.

Since
T=C_{G}(\psi_{1})
, it normalizes
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
. Therefore
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
carries an action of
T
by changing the level structure at
\infty
.

4.3.
The Hitchin map

Let
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}
be a chosen set of homogeneous free generators of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}
. Let
d_{i}=\deg f_{i}
. Let

\mathcal{A}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{H}^{0}({X,\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty+0)^{\otimes d_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-\underline{0})})\cong\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{H}^{0}({X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(d_{i}\cdot\infty-\underline{0})})\cong\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{H}^{0}({X,\mathcal{O}(d_{i}-1)})

Namely, a point
(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})\in\mathcal{A}
consists of a rational poly-differential form
a_{i}=\widetilde{a}_{i}(dt/t)^{d_{i}}
on
X
, where
a_{i}
is a regular function on
X-\{\infty\}
with possibly a pole of order
\leq d_{i}
at
\infty
, and vanishing at
0
. We have a Hitchin map

(4.3)

f:\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})\to\mathcal{A}

sending
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{2\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B},\varphi)
to
(f_{1}(\varphi),\cdots,f_{n}(\varphi))
. Note that
\varphi=\widetilde{\varphi}dt/t
for some
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\textup{H}^{0}({X,\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})(\infty)})
, hence
f_{i}(\varphi)
can be written as
f_{i}(\widetilde{\varphi})(dt/t)^{d_{i}}
where
f_{i}(\widetilde{\varphi})
is a regular function on
X-\{\infty\}
with a pole of order
\leq d_{i}
at
\infty
;
f_{i}(\widetilde{\varphi})
vanishes at
0
because
\widetilde{\varphi}(0)=\textup{Res}_{0}\varphi\in\mathcal{E}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{n})
.

We also have the moment map for the
\mathfrak{g}
-action on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Together with
f
we get

\widetilde{f}=(f,\mu_{\mathfrak{g}}):\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{2},\mathbf{I}_{0})\to\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}.

Here
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{g}^{*}\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}G
. Let
\chi:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to\mathfrak{c}
be the natural map. The map
\textup{ev}_{\infty}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathfrak{c}
is by taking the image of the polar part of
\varphi_{\infty}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}=\textup{ev}_{\infty}^{-1}\chi(\psi_{1})\subset\mathcal{A}
. Then
\widetilde{f}
restricts to a map

f_{\psi}:\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}.

There is a distinguished point
a_{\psi}\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
given as the image of
(\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}},\tau^{\textup{triv}}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}}_{0,B},\psi_{1}dt)\in\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
. Here
(\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}},\tau^{\textup{triv}}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{0,B}^{\textup{triv}})
is the trivial
G
-bundle with its tautological trivialization at
\infty
and
B
-reduction at
0
.

4.3.1 Example
.

Consider the case
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
. In this case
\psi_{1}
is given by a diagonal matrix with entries
(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{n})
. Then the fibers of the Hitchin map
f_{\psi}
can be describe using spectral curves. A point
a=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
(where
a_{i}\in\textup{H}^{0}({X,\mathcal{O}(i)})
and
a_{i}(0)=0
) defines a spectral curve
Y_{a}\subset\textup{Tot}(\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty+\underline{0}))=\textup{Tot}(\mathcal{O}(1))
by the equation
y^{n}-a_{1}y^{n-1}+a_{2}y^{n-2}+\cdots+(-1)^{n}a_{n}=0
, such that over
\infty
it passes exactly through the points
(\lambda_{i},\infty)
for
i=1,2,\cdots,n
, and its preimage over
\underline{0}
is concentrated at
(0,\underline{0})
. (To make sense of this it requires a trivialization of the fiber of
\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty+\underline{0})
at
\infty
, for which we use
dt
).

Then
f_{\psi}^{-1}(a)
is isomorphic to the stack
\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a};\infty)
that classifies rank one torsion-free sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
Y_{a}
together with a trivialization of its fiber at every point over
\infty
. From this we see that
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
acts on
\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a};\infty)
by changing the trivializations, and the quotient
\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a};\infty)/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
is the compactified Picard stack
\overline{\textup{Pic}}(Y_{a})
.

It is easy to see that the arithmetic genus of
Y_{a}
is
(n-1)(n-2)/2
by viewing it as a degree
n
curve in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
(via the identification
\textup{Tot}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}-\{[0,0,1]\}
). Therefore
\dim\overline{\textup{Pic}}(Y_{a})=(n-1)(n-2)/2-1
and
\dim\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a};\infty)=n(n-1)/2
. On the other hand,
\dim\mathcal{A}=1+2+\cdots+n=n(n+1)/2
; since
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\subset\mathcal{A}
is cut out by
n
linear equations,
\dim\mathcal{A}_{\psi}=n(n-1)/2=\dim\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a})
.

For the point
a_{\psi}
, the corresponding
Y_{a_{\psi}}
is defined by
(y-\lambda_{1}t)\cdots(y-\lambda_{n}t)
. It is isomorphic to a union of
n
copies of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
intersecting over a unique point over
\underline{0}
(it is still locally planar at the intersection).

4.3.2.
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action

There is an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
by simultaneously scaling the Higgs field and rotating
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We take the inverse of the second action so that
\psi_{1}dt
is invariant under
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The induced action on
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
is contracting to
a_{\psi}
.

The basic geometric properties of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is summarized below.

4.3.3 Proposition

(
[
9
, Theorem 2.8.1, Lemma 2.15.6]
)
.

(1)

The stack
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is a smooth algebraic space locally of finite type over
k
of dimension
|\Phi|
(the number of roots of
G
).

(2)

The space
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
carries a canonical symplectic structure with weight
1
under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.

(3)

The map
f_{\psi}
is a completely integrable system. In particular, fibers of
f_{\psi}
are Lagrangians in
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
.

(4)

For any
\xi\in\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
, the limit point
\lim_{s\to 0}s\cdot\xi
under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action exists.

4.4.
Central Hitchin fiber

Let
\Lambda_{\psi}=f_{\psi}^{-1}(a_{\psi})\subset\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
be the central fiber of
f_{\psi}
.

4.4.1.
Open subset of
\Lambda_{\psi}
as a Lagrangian in
T^{*}\mathcal{B}

There is an open subset of
\Lambda_{\psi}
that can be identified with a conic Lagrangian in
T^{*}\mathcal{B}
. Let
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{\psi}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
be the open locus where the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}
is trivial. Then the moduli stack of
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty})
with
\mathcal{E}
trivial is a point. Fixing
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty})
to be the trivial bundle with the tautological trivialization at
\infty
, we see that
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{\psi}
classifies pairs
(B^{\prime},\varphi)
where
B^{\prime}\subset G
is a Borel subgroup and
\varphi=\psi_{1}t+A
for
A\in\mathfrak{g}
, such that
A\in\mathfrak{n}_{B^{\prime}}
, the nilpotent radical of
B^{\prime}
. In other words we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{\psi}\cong T^{*}\mathcal{B}
. This is clearly a symplectomorphism.

Consider
\Lambda_{\psi}^{\circ}=\Lambda_{\psi}\cap\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{\psi}
. Under the symplectomorphism
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{\psi}\cong T^{*}\mathcal{B}
,
\Lambda^{\circ}_{\psi}
becomes a Lagrangian in
T^{*}\mathcal{B}
. Explicitly,
\Lambda_{\psi}^{\circ}
consists of pairs
\{(A,B^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{g}\times\mathcal{B}
such that
A\in\mathfrak{n}_{B^{\prime}}
and
\chi(\psi_{1}+sA)=\chi(\psi_{1})\in\mathfrak{c}
for all
s\in\mathbb{C}
, where
\chi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{c}
is the quotient map. Let

\mathcal{N}_{\psi_{1}}=\{A\in\mathfrak{g}|\chi(\psi_{1}+sA)=\chi(\psi_{1})\mbox{ for all $s\in\mathbb{C}$}\}.

In other words
A\in\mathcal{N}_{\psi_{1}}
if and only if the line
\ell_{\psi_{1},A}
through
\psi
in the direction of
A
remains in the adjoint orbit of
\psi_{1}
. Clearly
\mathcal{N}_{\psi_{1}}
is a conical closed subset in the nilpotent cone. Let
\pi:T^{*}\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{N}
be the Springer resolution. Then

\Lambda_{\psi}^{\circ}=\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{\psi_{1}}).

The following is a special case of
[
9
, Theorem 2.8.1(4)]
.

4.4.2 Proposition
.

There is a canonical homeomorphism
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\to\Lambda_{\psi}
.

We briefly indicate how the map
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\to\Lambda_{\psi}
is constructed. We have a base point
(\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}},\tau^{\textup{triv}}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}}_{0,B},\psi_{1}dt)\in\overline{\Lambda}_{\psi}
, where
(\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}},\tau^{\textup{triv}}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}}_{0,B})
is the trivial
G
-bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with the tautological trivialization at
\infty
and Borel reduction at
0
given by
B
itself. A point in
Fl
can be viewed as a triple
(\mathcal{F},\iota,\mathcal{F}_{0,B})
where
\mathcal{F}
is a
G
-torsor over
\textup{Spec}\ \mathbb{C}[\![t]\!]
,
\iota
a trivialization of
\mathcal{F}|_{\textup{Spec}\ \mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)}
and
\mathcal{F}_{0,B}
a
B
-reduction of
\mathcal{F}_{t=0}
. Such a triple lies in
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
if and only if
\psi
, viewed as a section of
\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{F})|_{\textup{Spec}\ \mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)}
, extends to a section of
\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
whose value at
t=0
lies in
\mathcal{F}_{0,B}(\mathfrak{b}^{\bot})
. The map
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\to\Lambda_{\psi}
sends
(\mathcal{F},\iota,\mathcal{F}_{0,B})
to the
G
-Higgs bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
obtained by gluing
(\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}},\tau^{\textup{triv}}_{\infty},\mathcal{E}^{\textup{triv}}_{0,B},\psi_{1}dt)|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}-\{0\}}
with
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0,B},\psi_{1}\frac{dt}{t})
along their trivializations over
\textup{Spec}\ \mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)
.

4.5.
Lattice action on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}

4.5.1 Lemma
.

The natural action of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\cong\Lambda_{\psi}
extends canonically to an action of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
.

Proof.

Following the construction in
[
44
, §2.1]
, let
\mathbb{J}\to[\mathfrak{c}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
be the regular centralizer group scheme for
G
. Let
J\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\times X
be the group scheme whose restriction to
\{a\}\times X
(where
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
) is
a^{*}\mathbb{J}
, where
a
is viewed as a map
X\to\mathfrak{c}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Consider the Picard stack
\mathcal{P}
over
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
whose fiber at
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
is the moduli stack of
J_{a}=a^{*}\mathbb{J}
-torsors over
X
.

For
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
, the leading term of
a
at
\infty
is
\chi(\psi_{1})
, hence the fiber
J_{a}|_{\infty}
is canonically identified with
T=C_{G}(\psi_{1})
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
be the stack whose fiber over
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
is a
J_{a}
-torsor over
X
together with a trivialization at
\infty
, so that
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{P}
is a
T
-torsor. The action of
\mathbb{B}\mathbb{J}
on
[\mathfrak{g}/G]
induces a natural action of
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
over
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
.

Consider the cameral cover
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\times X
that is the pullback of
\mathfrak{t}^{*}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathfrak{c}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
under the evaluation map
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\times X\to[\mathfrak{c}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
. Let
D_{\infty}=\textup{Spec}\ \mathcal{O}_{\infty}
be the formal completion of
X
at
\infty
, and let
\pi_{\infty}:\widetilde{X}^{\wedge}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\widehat{\times}D_{\infty}
be the base change of
\pi
. Since
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\times\{\infty\}\to\mathfrak{c}
is the constant map that lifts to
\psi\in(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{\textup{rs}}
,
\pi_{\infty}
is a
W
-torsor with a trivialization in the special fiber
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\times\{\infty\}
. Therefore the
W
-torsor
\pi_{\infty}
has a canonical trivialization extending the trivialization of the special fiber, i.e., there is a canonical section
s:\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\widehat{\times}D_{\infty}\to\widetilde{X}^{\wedge}_{\infty}
to
\pi_{\infty}
given by
\psi
. Using this section we get a canonical isomorphism

(4.4)

J|_{\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\widehat{\times}D_{\infty}}\cong T\times(\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\widehat{\times}D_{\infty})

of group schemes over
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\widehat{\times}D_{\infty}
. Let
\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{J,\infty}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
be the moduli space whose fiber over
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
classifies
J_{a}
-torsors over
X
with a trivialization over
\infty
and a trivialization over
X-\{\infty\}
. We have a canonical map
\tau:\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{J,\infty}\to\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
over
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
. Using (
4.4
), we can then identify
\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{J,\infty}
with
\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{T}\times\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
where
\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{T}=T(\!(t^{-1})\!)/T[\![t^{-1}]\!]^{1}
, and
T[\![t^{-1}]\!]^{1}=\ker(\textup{ev}_{\infty}:T[\![t^{-1}]\!]\to T)
. Thus we get a canonical homomorphism over
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
.

\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\xrightarrow{\lambda\mapsto t^{\lambda}}\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{T}\times\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\cong\widehat{\textup{Gr}}_{J,\infty}\to\widehat{\mathcal{P}}.

Composing with the action of
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
we get an action of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
that extends the natural action on
\Lambda_{\psi}\cong\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.

∎

4.5.2 Example
.

In the situation of
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
, the action of the standard basis elements
e_{i}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on each Hitchin fiber
\overline{\mathcal{P}}(Y_{a};\infty)
is by sending
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{F}(-y_{i,\infty})
. Here
y_{i,\infty}=(\psi_{i},\infty)\in Y_{a}
are the points over
\infty
. Given a basis of
\mathcal{F}
at
y_{i,\infty}
, we have to specify a basis of
\mathcal{F}(-y_{i,\infty})
at
y_{i,\infty}
. This amounts to exhibiting a canonical basis of
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{a}}(-y_{i,\infty})|_{y_{i,\infty}}
, or a basis of the cotangent line
T^{*}_{y_{i,\infty}}Y_{a}
. However, the projection
p_{a}:Y_{a}\to X
is étale over
\infty
, the cotangent space
T^{*}_{y_{i,\infty}}Y_{a}
is identified with
T^{*}_{\infty}X
via
dp_{a}
, hence carries a basis
dt/t^{2}
.

4.6.
Parahoric version

For any standard parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}\subset G(\!(t)\!)
, we have the moduli stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})
(we use
\mathbf{P}_{0}
to emphasize that the
\mathbf{P}_{0}
-level structure is at
0\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
) and its cotangent bundle
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})
. They have actions of
\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
. Similarly we have
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})
and its cotangent bundle
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})
. They have actions by
\mathfrak{g}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
. We define
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
as

\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}:=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{\psi}\mathfrak{g}.

The Hitchin map

(4.5)

f_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}:\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}

is defined similarly as
f_{\psi}
, but it is in general not surjective. Let
\Lambda_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}=f_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}^{-1}(a_{\psi})
.

In the special case
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{G}=G[\![t]\!]
,
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
consists of triples
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\varphi)
where
\mathcal{E}
is a
G
-bundle over
X
,
\tau_{\infty}
a trivialization of
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}
and
\varphi
is a section of
\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty)\cong\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})
whose leading term at
\infty
is
\psi
. The Hitchin map for
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is the constant map to
a_{\psi}\in\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
because
f_{i}(\varphi)
are constant functions on
X
.

To state an analogue of Proposition
4.4.2
, we need to define affine Spaltenstein fibers for the parahoric
\mathbf{P}
and
\gamma\in\mathfrak{g}(\!(t)\!)
:

\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\gamma}=\{g\in G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{P}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1})\gamma\in\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{P}^{+}\}.

The same argument of Proposition
4.4.2
gives the following.

4.6.1 Proposition
.

There is a canoncial homeomorphism
\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\psi}\cong\Lambda_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
(the fiber of
a_{\psi}
under the parahoric Hitchin map (
4.5
)).

4.6.2.
The case
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{G}

Consider
\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{G}}_{\psi}=\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
we have
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]
. On the other hand, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})=\ker(G[t^{-1}]\to G)\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]
be the point corresponding to the double coset of
t^{\lambda}
. Denote the underlying
G
-bundle of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
by
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
. Note
\psi_{1}dt
defines a global section of
\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\otimes\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty)
, hence giving a point
(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},\psi_{1}dt)\in\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

4.6.3 Lemma
.

We have
\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{G}}_{\psi}=\{t^{\lambda}|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
. In particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is a discrete set consisting of points
\{(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda},\psi_{1}dt)|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
.

Proof.

By definition,
\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{G}}_{\psi}=\{g\in\textup{Gr}|\textup{Ad}(g^{-1})t\psi_{1}\in\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=t\mathfrak{g}[\![t]\!]\}
. Hence
\textup{Fl}^{\mathbf{G}}_{\psi}=\textup{Gr}_{\psi_{1}}
. Since
\psi_{1}
is regular semisimple,
\textup{Gr}_{\psi_{1}}=(\textup{Gr})^{T}=\{t^{\lambda}|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
.
∎

4.7.
Microsheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}

We wish to define a sheaf of categories
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
, in the spirit of Section
3.1.4
. Although
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is a finite dimensional manifold, it is not of finite type, and so this requires some care.

We make
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
into an exact symplectic manifold as follows. Consider the holomorphic symplectic form
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
. As our real symplectic form, we take the real part
\omega=\Re(\omega_{\mathbb{C}})
. Let
V
be the vector field generated by the action of
\mathbb{R}^{*}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
. Then
V
is a Liouville vector field for
\omega
, with associated Liouville form
\lambda
.

We can exhaust
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
by an increasing sequence of open substacks
{}^{\mu_{i}}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
parametrizing bundles of Harder-Narasimhan type bounded by some rational dominant coweight
\mu_{i}
,
i=1,2,\cdots
. Each
{}^{\mu_{i}}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
is a quotient of the smooth finite-dimensional scheme
N_{i}={}^{\mu_{i}}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{n_{i}}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
by the action of
\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{i}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{n_{i}}
for
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large (depending on
\mu_{i}
).

Let
\mathfrak{N}^{i}=[N^{i}/\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{i}]
be the quotient stack. Let
\mathfrak{X}^{i}=T^{*}(\mathfrak{N}^{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
. We apply Definition
3.1.3
to obtain a sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories on
\mathfrak{X}^{i}
.

The action of
\mathbf{K}_{i}/\mathbf{K}_{i}^{\psi}\cong\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}=\mathbb{G}_{a}
admits a moment map
\mu:T^{*}\mathfrak{N}^{i}\to(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbb{G}_{a})^{*}
, and the zero-fiber
\mu^{-1}(0)
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant. Its complement defines a substack
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{i}\subset\mathfrak{X}^{i}
, which embeds in
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
as an open subset. If we view
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{i}
as a (real) symplectic manifold, it is naturally equipped with quotient Maslov data. We can apply Definition
3.1.11
to obtain a sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{i}
, which coincides with the restriction of the previous sheaf to
\mathfrak{X}^{i}
by Proposition
3.1.17
.

We have an exhaustion

\mathcal{M}_{\psi}=\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{i}.

Since the quotient Maslov data on each
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{\psi}
is compatible with the inclusions
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{\psi}\subset\mathcal{M}^{i+1}_{\psi}
, the resulting sheaves are equal after restriction.

4.7.1 Definition
.

Let
\mu\mathrm{Sh}
be the sheaf of stable
\infty
-categories on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
whose restriction to
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{i}
is defined as above.

4.7.2 Proposition
.

Any compactly supported object in
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
is supported on a finite union of irreducible components of the central Hitchin fiber
\Lambda_{\psi}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
.

Proof.

The support of any such object is conical with respect to the Liouville flow. Any compact conical subset must be contained in
\Lambda_{\psi}
. Since the support is coisotropic, it must be a union of irreducible components of
\Lambda_{\psi}
.
∎

For this and other reasons (see the discussion of Fukaya categories below), it is natural to restrict our attention to the subcategory

\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
of microlocal sheaves supported on
\Lambda_{\psi}
. We also sometimes abuse notation and write this as
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\textup{Fl}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
.

4.7.3.
Lattice action

The action of the lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
does not commute with the action of
\mathbb{C}^{*}
, and in particular does not preserve the one-form
\lambda
and does not map conical subsets of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
to conical subsets. For the same reason, the lattice does not
act on the category
\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
. However,
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
does preserve the subset
\Lambda_{\psi}
, and we expect that it acts on
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
by automorphisms. Indeed, for each
\sigma\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, one may pick a homotopy between
\sigma^{*}\lambda
and
\lambda
such that
\Lambda_{\psi}
is fiberwise conical. We expect that these homotopies, composed with the translation action of
\sigma\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
induce an action of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
, following the construction in
[
42
]
. We will not use this action in the present paper. It should be the microlocal counterpart of the action defined in Section
6.2
.

4.8.
Speculations on Fukaya categories

We now turn to the question of associating a Fukaya category to the space
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
. The form
\lambda
does
not
make
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
into a Liouville manifold, because
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is not of finite type. There are various methods available to try and circumvent this problem; we sketch two approaches here.

4.8.1.
Via localization to the core

Recall the Hitchin map
f:\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\to\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
. Fix a linear metric on
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
, and consider the family of balls
B^{k}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
of radius
k>0
centered at
a_{\psi}
. Set
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{k}=f^{-1}(B^{k})\subset\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
. Then the flow of the vector field associated to
\lambda
is outward pointing along
\partial\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{k}
. The core of
(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
may be defined as in Definition
3.2.1
, where we set
X^{k}=\mathcal{M}_{\psi}^{k}
.

4.8.2 Lemma
.

The core of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is the central fiber of the Hitchin fibration:
\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)=\Lambda_{\psi}
.

Proof.

The Liouville vector field dilates
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
, with unique fixed point
a_{\psi}
. Hence
\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)\subset f^{-1}(a_{\psi})=\Lambda_{\psi}
. The opposite containment follows from the fact that
\Lambda_{\psi}
is a union of compact conical subsets.
∎

The fact that the core
\Lambda_{\psi}
is ind-compact is a clue that
(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
in many respects behaves like a finite type Liouville manifold. For instance, the statement of Theorem
3.2.4
should hold for
(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
. We may then seek to
define
the Fukaya category in terms of
\mathfrak{Sh}(X,\lambda):=\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
, converting Theorem
3.2.5
into a definition.

4.8.3.
Via the lattice quotient

A natural approach to producing a (finite type) Liouville manifold from
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is to take a quotient by the action of the lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, which preserves the symplectic form and the subset
\Lambda_{\psi}
. The lattice action is free and discontinuous on
\Lambda_{\psi}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
, and the quotient
\Lambda_{\psi}/\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
is of finite type. The same should be true of a small tubular neighborhood of
\Lambda_{\psi}
.

4.8.4 Definition
.

Fix a small ball
B^{\epsilon}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
centered at
a_{\psi}
, and let
\mathcal{Y}_{\psi}=f^{-1}(B^{\epsilon})/\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

The space
\mathcal{Y}_{\psi}
is a finite type manifold, which we wish to endow with a Liouville structure.
4
4

4

The space
\mathcal{Y}_{\psi}
is a smooth affine complex variety and thus a Stein manifold. It therefore carries a natural family of Weinstein structures. It is difficult, however, to establish a direct relation between these Wenstein structures and the Liouville structure on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
defined above. For the same reason, it is difficult to relate the Fukaya categories defined from these Weinstein structures to the categories considered in this paper, although we expect a close such relation exists.
As mentioned above, the action of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
does not preserve the Liouville form
\lambda
, and hence the latter does not descend to the quotient.

One can instead try to deform
\lambda
to something
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-invariant as follows. Suppose we are given a real-valued function
h:\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\to\mathbb{R}
, such that for all
\sigma\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
we have
\sigma^{*}dh=dh+\theta_{\sigma}
. Here
\theta_{\sigma}=\iota_{V_{\sigma}}\omega
and
V_{\sigma}
is the vector field generating the action of
\mathfrak{Re}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
defined by
\sigma
.

Then
\lambda_{h}=\lambda+df
will be a
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-invariant Liouville form, which descends to
\mathcal{Y}_{\psi}
.
One should further require that the associated isotopy of Liouville vector fields preserves
\Lambda_{\psi}
and dilates
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
. The result would then be a Liouville manifold
(\mathcal{Y}_{\psi},\lambda_{h})
whose Fukaya category can be locally modelled by
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
. We do not at present know how to find such a function
h
. However, its existence would follow from the existence of a suitable hyperkähler structure on
X
by the same construction as in
[
28
]
.

In this case, one could apply Theorem
3.2.5
to identify the category
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\lambda)
with a
\mathbb{X}^{*}(T)
-graded version of the Fukaya category of
\mathcal{Y}_{\psi}
. We leave such questions for future work.

5.
Affine Hecke category with level
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\psi)

In this section we study the structure of versions of the affine Hecke category with deeper level structure
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\psi)
. They will act on the categories
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, the main players in §
6
and §
7
. A more general type of affine Hecke categories were studied by Kamgarpour–Schedler
[
31
]
in the
\ell
-adic context over a positive characteristic base field. Our contribution here is: (1) we work with the Kirillov model so that the results hold in any sheaf-theoretic context; (2) for our purposes we need to work with a completed monodromic version of the affine Hecke category in the spirit of
[
19
, Appendix A]
.

5.1.
The monoidal category
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and its variants

Let
G(\!(\tau)\!)
be the loop group of
G
at
\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with uniformizer
\tau=t^{-1}
. We would like to define a monoidal category that is informally sheaves on the double quotient
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\psi)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\psi)
. More precisely, consider the ind-stack
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
, which carries an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
by loop rotation (scaling
t=\tau^{-1}
) and
\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
by left and translation coming from the fact that
\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
. Our convention is that the left (resp. right) translation by
a\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
is
g\mapsto\widetilde{a}g
(resp.
g\mapsto g(\widetilde{a})^{-1}
) where
\widetilde{a}\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is a lifting of
a
. On the quotient
\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}
by the diagonal
\mathbb{G}_{a}
, there is an action of
\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
where
\mathbb{G}_{a}
acts by the residue symmetry from
(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a})/\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbb{G}_{a}
(via the first projection). We can thus consider the category

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}=\Kir\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}\right).

5.1.1.
Monoidal structure

To explain the monoidal structure on
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, we consider the following general situation. Let
X_{1},X_{2}
and
Y
be stacks with
Aff
-actions. Let
X_{12}
be another stack with an action of
\textup{Aff}^{(2)}:=(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
(diagonal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on both copies of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
), together with maps

\textstyle{X_{12}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{X_{1}}
\textstyle{X_{2}}

such that
p_{i}
is equivariant with respect to the homomorphism
\pi_{i}:\textup{Aff}^{(2)}=(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by
\pi_{i}(a_{1},a_{2},b)=(a_{i},b)
, for
i=1,2
, and
m
is equivariant with respect to the homomorphism
\sigma:\textup{Aff}^{(2)}\to\textup{Aff}
given by
\sigma(a_{1},a_{2},b)=(a_{1}+a_{2},b)
.

We claim that the functor

\displaystyle D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{1})\times D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{2})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(Y)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle m_{!}(p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{1}\otimes p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{2})

induces a functor on the Kirillov categories

(5.1)

\Kir(X_{1})\times\Kir(X_{2})\to\Kir(Y).

Indeed,
p_{i}^{*}
sends
D_{\textup{Aff}}(X_{i})
to
D_{\textup{Aff}^{(2)}}(X_{12})
for
i=1,2
, therefore the functor
(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\mapsto p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{1}\otimes p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{2}
induces a functor

(5.2)

\Kir(X_{1})\times\Kir(X_{2})\to D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{12})/D_{\textup{Aff}^{(2)}}(X_{12}).

Moreover,
m_{!}
sends
D_{\textup{Aff}^{(2)}}(X_{12})
to
D_{\textup{Aff}}(Y)
, therefore it induces a functor

(5.3)

D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{12})/D_{\textup{Aff}^{(2)}}(X_{12})\to\Kir(Y).

The composition of (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) then gives the desired functor (
5.1
).

We now apply the above discussion to the diagram

(5.4)

\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}

Here
m
is induced by the multiplication map of
G(\!(\tau)\!)
, and
p_{1},p_{2}
are the projections to the first and second factors. The
\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})
-quotient in
\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
is given by the action
a\cdot(g_{1},g_{2})=(\widetilde{a}g_{1}\widetilde{a}^{-1},\widetilde{a}g_{2}\widetilde{a}^{-1})
. We equip
\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
with the
Aff
-action explained in the beginning of §
5.1
. We equip
\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
with the
\textup{Aff}^{(2)}
-action where
(a_{1},a_{2})\in\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
acts by
(a_{1},a_{2})(g_{1},g_{2})=(\widetilde{a_{1}}g_{1},g_{2}\widetilde{a_{2}})
while
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
still acts by loop rotation. The discussion in the previous paragraph then gives a functor on the Kirillov categories

\displaystyle(-)\star(-):\mathcal{H}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{H}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\infty}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle m_{!}(p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{1}\otimes p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{2}).

This defines a monoidal structure on
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
: the associativity structure follows from that of the monoidal structure of
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}}(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}})
under the usual convolution.

5.1.2.
Perverse
t
-structure

The
*
-pullback functor

D\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\right)\to D(G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})

is fully faithful. The perverse
t
-structure on
D(G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
restricts to a
t
-structure on
D(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}})
, which induces a
t
-structure on the quotient
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. We call it the
perverse
t
-structure
on
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. Denote the heart of the perverse
t
-structure by
\mathcal{H}^{\heartsuit}_{\infty}
.

5.1.3.
Variants

We will need the following variants of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

(1)

We may impose right
T
-equivariance and consider the category

\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}=\Kir\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}\right).

Similarly we may impose left
T
-equivariance to obtain the category
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. We can also impose
T
-equivariance on both sides to form the monoidal category

{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}=\Kir\left(\dfrac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}\right).

The monoidal structure on
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
is defined using a diagram similar to (
5.4
)

(5.5)

\textstyle{\dfrac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T}}{{\times}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}

For both
\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}
and
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}
, we give them the perverse t-structure by viewing objects therein as sheaves on
G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)
. Denote the hearts of the perverse t-structures by

(\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty})^{\heartsuit},\quad({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}.

(2)

We may consider the full dg subcategory
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
generated by the essential image of the forgetful functor
\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. It will be shown in Lemma
5.3.5
that this is the same as the subcategory generated by the essential image of the forgetful functor
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
is a monoidal full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. We call it the
T
-monodromic subcategory of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
(with unipotent monodromy).

(3)

We may define a completed version
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
by formally adding pro-objects that are universally monodromic along the left or right
T
-orbits. This can be done using the general framework in
[
19
, Appendix A]
.

5.2.
Relevant double cosets

By §
2.4
, we can talk about relevant points in
\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}
under the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action. We shall simply say
g\in G(\!(\tau)\!)
is relevant if its image in
\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta(\mathbb{G}_{a})}
is.

5.2.1 Lemma
.

The relevant locus in
G(\!(\tau)\!)
is the union of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

By definition, a point
x\in G(\!(\tau)\!)
is relevant in this situation if and only if, under the
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
-action by left and right translation, the projection

\textup{Stab}_{\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{(\psi,\psi)}\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\xrightarrow{-}\mathbb{G}_{a}

is trivial (the last map is taking the difference of two coordinates). Here we view
\psi
as the additive character on
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
via (
4.1
). The first projection identifies
\textup{Stab}_{\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}}(g)
with the subgroup
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap x\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}x^{-1}\subset\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
, and the above additive character becomes

\displaystyle\chi:\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap x\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}x^{-1}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{a}

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\psi(g)-\psi(x^{-1}gx).

Therefore
x
is relevant if and only if

(5.6)

\psi(g)=\psi(x^{-1}gx)\mbox{for all $g\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap x\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}x^{-1}$.}

By the Cartan decomposition, we may assume
x\in\mathbf{G}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}_{\infty}
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
(up to
W
). Let
P_{\lambda}
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
containing
T
with roots
\{\alpha\in R|\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\geq 0\}
. Let
L_{\lambda}
be its Levi subgroup containing
T
with roots
\{\alpha\in R|\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle=0\}
, and let
U_{\lambda}
be the unipotent radical of
P_{\lambda}
. Note that
P_{\lambda}=G\cap\tau^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}
. Consider the
G\times G
-action on
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
; the stabilizer of the base point
t^{\lambda}
is the subgroup of
P_{\lambda}\times_{L_{\lambda}}P_{-\lambda}
(fiber product using the common quotient
L_{\lambda}
of
P_{\lambda}
and
P_{-\lambda}
). Therefore, up to left and right translation by
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
, we may write
x=g_{1}\tau^{\lambda}g_{2}^{-1}
, where
(g_{1},g_{2})\in G\times G
is unique up to right translation by the subgroup
P_{\lambda}\times_{L_{\lambda}}P_{-\lambda}
. For this
x
, we have
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap x\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}x^{-1}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap g_{1}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda}g_{1}^{-1}=g_{1}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda})g_{1}^{-1}
. Our goal is to show that the condition (
5.6
) implies that
x\in T\tau^{\lambda^{\prime}}
for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in W\lambda
, up to right multiplying
(g_{1},g_{2})
by
P_{\lambda}\times_{L_{\lambda}}P_{-\lambda}
.

The condition (
5.6
) becomes

(5.7)

\psi^{g_{1}}(y)=\psi^{g_{2}}(\tau^{-\lambda}y\tau^{\lambda})\quad\mbox{ for all $y\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda}$,}

where for
g\in G
,
\psi^{g}\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
denote the linear function
v\mapsto\psi(\textup{Ad}(g)x)
on
\mathfrak{g}
, viewed as a additive character on
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
as in (
4.1
). Now let
y
go through elements in
\exp(\tau^{n}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})
for various affine roots
\alpha+n
in that appear in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda}
, we see that (
5.7
) implies

\psi^{g_{1}}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{\lambda}}=0,\quad\psi^{g_{2}}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{-\lambda}}=0,\quad\psi^{g_{1}}|_{\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}}=\psi^{g_{2}}|_{\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}}.

Here
\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda},\mathfrak{n}_{\lambda}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{-\lambda}
are the Lie algebras of
L_{\lambda},U_{\lambda}
and
U_{-\lambda}
respectively. Using an invariant quadratic form on
\mathfrak{g}
to identify
\mathfrak{g}
with
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, we see that these conditions mean

(5.8)

\psi^{g_{1}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda},\quad\psi^{g_{2}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{-\lambda},\quad\psi^{g_{1}}\textup{ mod }\mathfrak{n}_{\lambda}=\psi^{g_{2}}\textup{ mod }\mathfrak{n}_{-\lambda}\in\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}.

Since
\psi^{g_{1}}
is a semisimple element in
\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}
, there exists
u_{1}\in U_{\lambda}
such that
\psi^{g_{1}u_{1}}\in\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}
. Changing
g_{1}
to
g_{1}u_{1}
, we may assume
\psi^{g_{1}}\in\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}
. Then there exists
\ell_{1}\in L_{\lambda}
such that
\psi^{g_{1}\ell_{1}}\in\mathfrak{t}
. Since
\psi\in\mathfrak{t}
is regular semisimple, we conclude that
g_{1}\ell_{1}\in N_{G}(T)
. So we may write
g_{1}=\dot{w}_{1}\ell_{1}^{-1}
for some
\dot{w}_{1}\in N_{G}(T)
with image
w_{1}\in W
. Similarly, we may assume
g_{2}=\dot{w}_{2}\ell_{2}^{-1}
for some
w_{2}\in W
and
\ell_{2}\in L_{\lambda}
. Now the third condition in (
5.8
) implies
\psi^{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}=\psi
, hence
g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}\in T
since
\psi
is regular semisimple. In other words,
\dot{w}_{1}\ell_{1}^{-1}\ell_{2}\dot{w}_{2}^{-1}\in T
.

Now we conclude

x=g_{1}\tau^{\lambda}g_{2}^{-1}=\dot{w}_{1}\ell_{1}^{-1}\tau^{\lambda}\ell_{2}\dot{w}_{2}=\dot{w}_{1}\ell_{1}^{-1}\ell_{2}\dot{w}_{2}^{-1}\tau^{w_{2}\lambda}\in T\tau^{w_{2}\lambda}.

Here we use that
\tau^{\lambda}
commutes with
L_{\lambda}
.
∎

5.2.2.
Orbit dimension

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we have

(5.9)

d_{\lambda}:=\dim\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\dim\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}=\sum_{\alpha\in R}\max\{-\langle{\alpha,\lambda}\rangle,0\}=\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}|\langle{\alpha,\lambda}\rangle|.

Indeed,
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(\tau^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
. The Lie algebra of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(\tau^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is spanned by the affine root spaces
\tau^{n}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
such that
n>0
and
n+\langle{\alpha,\lambda}\rangle>0
, for each root
\alpha
of
G
. From this we see the tangent space of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(\tau^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
at identity is the direct sum of
\tau^{n}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
for
-\langle{\alpha,\lambda}\rangle\geq n>0
.

In particular, if
\lambda
is dominant, and
2\rho=\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\alpha
, then
d_{\lambda}=\langle{2\rho,\lambda}\rangle
.

The next few lemmas will be used in the next subsection for computing the convolution of standard sheaves.

Choose a pair of opposite Borel subgroups
B,B^{-}\subset G
containing
T
. Let
N
and
N^{-}
be the unipotent radical of
G
. Let

\mathbf{J}_{N}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap N[\![\tau]\!],\quad\mathbf{J}_{T}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap T[\![\tau]\!],\quad\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap N^{-}[\![\tau]\!].

5.2.3 Lemma
.

(1)

Multiplication induces an isomorphism of
k
-schemes

(5.10)

\mathbf{J}_{N}\times\mathbf{J}_{T}\times\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}.

(2)

If
\lambda
is dominant with respect to
B
, then

\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\mathbf{J}_{N}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}.

Proof.

(1) It is well-known that the map
N\times T\times N^{-}\hookrightarrow G
is an open embedding. Part (1) of the lemma follows by taking the based arc spaces of both sides.

(2) Since
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cong\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
, where
{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda}
, we only need to show that the inclusion
\mathbf{J}_{N}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
induces a surjection
\mathbf{J}_{N}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
. Applying conjugation by
\tau^{\lambda}
to the decomposition (
5.10
),
{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
admits a triangular decomposition
{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}\times\mathbf{J}_{T}\times{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}
, where
{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}=\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}\tau^{-\lambda}={}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap N[\![\tau]\!]
, etc. Since
\lambda
is dominant, we have
\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}\subset{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}
. Therefore every coset in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
can be represented by a point in
\mathbf{J}_{N}
by (1).
∎

5.2.4 Lemma
.

For any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
,

(1)

The only relevant points in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
are
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
.

(2)

The only relevant points in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
are
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
.

(3)

The only relevant points in
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
are
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

(1) follows from (2) because
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cdot T
and
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cdot T
.

(2) By Lemma
5.2.1
, all relevant double cosets in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
are of the form
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\nu}h\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
for some
\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
and
h\in T
. We need to show that if
\tau^{\nu}h\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
has non-empty intersection with
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}
, then
\nu=\lambda+\mu
and
h=1
.

Choose a Borel
B\subset G
containing
T
such that
\lambda
is dominant with respect to
B
. We apply Lemma
5.2.3
to write

(5.11)

\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}=\mathbf{J}_{N}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\mathbf{J}_{N}\tau^{\lambda}(\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{J}_{T}\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}})\tau^{\mu}\subset N(\!(\tau)\!)(\tau^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{J}_{T})N^{-}(\!(\tau)\!).

On the other hand, using (
5.10
) again, we have

(5.12)

\tau^{\nu}h\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\subset N(\!(\tau)\!)\cdot(\tau^{\nu}h\mathbf{J}_{T})\cdot N^{-}(\!(\tau)\!).

If the left sides of (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) intersect, so do the right sides, which by the uniqueness of triangular decomposition implies
\tau^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{J}_{T}=\tau^{\nu}h\mathbf{J}_{T}
. This forces
\nu=\lambda+\mu
and
h=1
.

(3) The proof is almost the same as (2), using the triangular decomposition
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}=\mathbf{J}_{N}\times\mathbf{J}^{\psi}_{T}\times\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}
, where
\mathbf{J}_{T}^{\psi}=\mathbf{J}_{T}\cap\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
.

∎

5.2.5 Lemma
.

The fiber of the multiplication map
m^{\lambda,\mu}:\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\to G(\!(\tau)\!)
over
\tau^{\lambda+\mu}
is an affine space of dimension
(d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda+\mu})/2
.

The same is true when
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is replaced by
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

We prove the version for
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
, and argument for the
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
-version is the same. Consider the subscheme
\widetilde{Z}
of
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})^{3}
consisting of triples
(x,y,z)
such that
x\tau^{\lambda}y\tau^{\mu}z=\tau^{\lambda+\mu}
. Rewriting this as
(\tau^{-\lambda}x^{-1}\tau^{\lambda})(\tau^{\mu}z^{-1}\tau^{-\mu})=y
and make a change of variables
x^{\prime}=\tau^{-\lambda}x^{-1}\tau^{\lambda}\in{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
and
z^{\prime}=\tau^{\mu}z^{-1}\tau^{-\mu}\in{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
, we have

\widetilde{Z}\cong\{(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\in{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}|x^{\prime}z^{\prime}\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\}.

There is an action of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
on
\widetilde{Z}
by left translation on the
x^{\prime}
-factor and an action of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
on it by right translation on the
z^{\prime}
-factor. A simple calculation shows that

(5.13)

(m^{\lambda,\mu})^{-1}(\tau^{\lambda+\mu})\cong(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})\backslash\widetilde{Z}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})=:Z.

Now choose a Borel subgroup
B
containing
T
(with
N
,
N^{-}
having the usual meaning) such that
\lambda
is dominant with respect to
B
. Then Lemma
5.2.3
(2) (or rather the same argument using triangular decomposition) shows that
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cong\mathbf{J}_{N}\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}
. We can thus write the right side of (
5.13
) as

\displaystyle Z\cong\{(\xi,\zeta)\in\mathbf{J}_{N}\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}\times{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})|\xi\zeta\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\}.

Write
\zeta\in{}^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
using the triangular decomposition
\zeta=\zeta^{+}\zeta^{0}\zeta^{-}
where
\zeta^{+}\in{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{N},\zeta^{0}\in\mathbf{J}_{T}
and
\zeta^{-}\in{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}
. If
\xi\in{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}
is such that
\xi\zeta=(\xi\zeta^{+})\zeta^{0}\zeta^{-}\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
, we must have
\zeta^{-}\in\mathbf{J}_{N^{-}}
by the uniqueness of the triangular decomposition. This means for any point
(\xi,\zeta)\in Z
we may replace
\zeta
by an element in
{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{N}
. Thus we get

Z\cong\{(\xi,\zeta)\in\mathbf{J}_{N}\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{N}\times{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{N}/(\mathbf{J}_{N}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{N})|\xi\zeta\in\mathbf{J}_{N}\}.

We consider an analog of the right side for each root group
U_{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in R^{+}
): let
\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}=\ker(U_{\alpha}[\![\tau]\!]\to U_{\alpha})=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap U_{\alpha}[\![\tau]\!]
, and consider

\displaystyle Z_{\alpha}=\{(\xi,\zeta)\in\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\times{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}/(\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha})|\xi\zeta\in\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\}.

For
n\leq 0
, let
L_{n}=\tau^{n+1}\mathbb{A}^{1}[\![\tau]\!]/\tau\mathbb{A}^{1}[\![\tau]\!]
, an additive group of dimension
-n
. An isomorphism
U_{\alpha}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}
allows us to identify
\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\backslash{}^{-\lambda}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}
with
L_{\langle{-\lambda,\alpha}\rangle}
. We have two cases:

(1)

If
\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle\geq 0
, then
{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\subset\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}
and
Z_{\alpha}=\textup{pt}
.

(2)

If
\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle<0
, then
{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}/(\mathbf{J}_{\alpha}\cap{}^{\mu}\mathbf{J}_{\alpha})\cong L_{\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle}
. Under these isomorphisms,
Z_{\alpha}
is the graph of the negation of either the natural embedding
L_{\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle}\hookrightarrow L_{\langle{-\lambda,\alpha}\rangle}
or the natural embedding
L_{\langle{-\lambda,\alpha}\rangle}\hookrightarrow L_{\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle}
, depending on whether
\langle{\lambda+\mu,\alpha}\rangle\geq 0
or not. In this case,
\dim Z_{\alpha}=\min\{\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle,\langle{-\mu,\alpha}\rangle\}
.

We can thus write uniformly

(5.14)

\dim Z_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle+|\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle|-|\langle{\lambda+\mu,\alpha}\rangle|\right).

Moreover, using the ordering of positive roots by height,
Z
is an iterated fibration

Z=Z_{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{n}}Z_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{n-1}}\cdots Z_{1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}Z_{0}=\textup{pt}

such that each map
\pi_{i}
is a torsor for the additive group
\prod_{\langle{\rho^{\vee},\alpha}\rangle=i}Z_{\alpha}
. We conclude that
Z
, hence the fiber
(m^{\lambda,\mu})^{-1}(\tau^{\lambda+\mu})
, is isomorphic to an affine space of dimension
\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\dim Z_{\alpha}
. Using (
5.14
) and the definition of
d_{\lambda}
in (
5.9
), we see that

\dim Z=\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\frac{1}{2}\left(\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle+|\langle{\mu,\alpha}\rangle|-|\langle{\lambda+\mu,\alpha}\rangle|\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda+\mu}\right).

∎

5.2.6 Remark
.

The above lemma strengthens and generalizes
[
31
, Proposition 30]
.

5.3.
Standard objects

5.3.1.

Fix
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Let

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)=\Kir\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\right)

Note that
\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}
acts trivially on
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
since we may lift
\mathbb{G}_{a}
to
\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{1}=\ker(T[\![\tau]\!]\to T)
. Similarly define the monodromic and completed versions
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)
, and the left (right)
T
-equivariant versions
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, the
Aff
-equivariant embeddings

i_{\lambda}:\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}

gives a full embedding

i_{\lambda!}:\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\infty}

and induces a full embedding
