\widehat{i}_{\lambda!}:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}.

Similarly define
Aff
-equivariant embedding

i^{T}_{\lambda}:\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}

and its counterparts
{}^{T}i_{\lambda}
when the
T
-quotient is on the left, and
{}^{T}i^{T}_{\lambda}
when the
T
-quotient is on both sides.

5.3.2.
Standard objects in
T
-equivariant variants

For each
\lambda
, the
Aff
-equivariant map

\kappa^{T}_{\lambda}:\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\tau^{\lambda}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)

induces an equivalence on Kirillov categories

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})

where
Aff
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
via affine transformations. The same is true for
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)
. Using the equivalence
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect})
from Example
2.1.5
, we see that

(5.15)

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{b}(\textup{Vect}).

For
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
, we consider
{}^{T}{\kappa}^{T}_{\lambda}:(\textup{pt}/T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\cong T\backslash(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\tau^{\lambda}T)/T\to(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)
, and we have

(5.16)

{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Kir((\textup{pt}/T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D(\textup{pt}/T).

5.3.3 Definition
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

(1)

We define
\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)
to be the object corresponding to the one-dimensional vector space
E\in\textup{Vect}
under the equivalence (
5.15
). Similarly define
{}^{T}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)
. Define
{}^{T}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)
to correspond to the constant sheaf
E\in D(\textup{pt}/T)
under the equivalence (
5.16
).

(2)

We define

\displaystyle\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}:=(i^{T}_{\lambda})_{!}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty},

\displaystyle{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}:=({}^{T}i_{\lambda})_{!}({}^{T}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle\in{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty},

\displaystyle{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}:=({}^{T}i^{T}_{\lambda})_{!}({}^{T}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda})\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle\in{}^{T}\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty},

Since
i^{T}_{\lambda}
and
{}^{T}i^{T}_{\lambda}
are affine morphisms, with our convention on the perverse t-structures, we have

\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\in(\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty})^{\heartsuit},\quad{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\in({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}.

Moreover,
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{0}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}
is the monoidal unit.

5.3.4 Lemma
.

(1)

Consider the map

\iota^{T}_{\lambda}:\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}.

Then
\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
is the image of
\iota^{T}_{\lambda!}E\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

(2)

Consider the map

\kappa^{T,\circ}_{\lambda}:\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}-\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}.

Then
\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
is the image of
\kappa^{T,\circ}_{\lambda!}E\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle[1]
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

Proof.

(1) Under the equivalence
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect})
,
E
corresponds to the skyscraper sheaf
i_{!}E
supported at
0
, therefore under the equivalence (
5.15
),
\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}
corresponds to the image of
(^{\prime}\iota^{T}_{\lambda})_{!}E
where
{}^{\prime}\iota^{T}_{\lambda}
is the embedding
\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
. The statement for
\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
then follows by applying
i^{T}_{\lambda!}
to
\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}\cong(^{\prime}\iota^{T}_{\lambda})_{!}E\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)
.

(2) is proved similarly, using that
E\in\textup{Vect}
also corresponds to
j_{!}E[1]\in\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
, for
j:\mathbb{G}_{a}-\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
the open inclusion .
∎

Let
\omega^{T}:\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and
{}^{T}\omega:{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
be the forgetful functors. Then it is clear that

(5.17)

\omega^{T}(\Delta^{T}_{\lambda})\cong{}^{T}{\omega}({}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}).

We denote both objects by
\Delta_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)
.

5.3.5 Lemma
.

The objects
\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
(resp.
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}
) for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
generate
\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}
(resp.
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
). The essential images of
{\omega}^{T}
and
{}^{T}\omega
are the same, and they both generate
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

First we prove the generation statement for
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
. By the equivalence (
5.15
) and the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}
generates
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda)
. Now by Lemma
5.2.1
and Corollary
2.4.4
,
i^{T}_{\lambda!}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{\lambda}=\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
generate
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

The generation statement for
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
is proved in the same way. Hence the essential image of
\omega^{T}
(resp.
{}^{T}\omega
) is generated by
\{\omega^{T}(\Delta^{T}_{\lambda})|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
(resp.
\{{}^{T}{\omega}({}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda})|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
). By (
5.17
), both essential images are generated by
\{\Delta_{\lambda}|\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
.
∎

5.3.6.
Standard objects in the completed category

Let
Aff
act on
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
by

s\cdot_{\lambda}(h,b)=(s^{-\lambda}h,s^{-1}b),\quad a\cdot(h,b)=(h,b+a),\quad h\in T,b\in\mathbb{G}_{a},(a,s)\in\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}.

We call this the
\lambda
-action. If we want to emphasize the
\lambda
-action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we use
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\lambda)
. Consider the map

\kappa_{\lambda}:T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}=T\times\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\to\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}

sending
(h,a)\mapsto\tau^{\lambda}h\widetilde{a}
for
h\in T,a\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
. This is
Aff
-equivariant for the
\lambda
-action on
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
. The adjoint functors
(\kappa_{\lambda}^{*},\kappa_{\lambda*})
give an equivalence on Kirillov categories

\kappa_{\lambda}^{*}:\mathcal{H}_{\infty}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Kir_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}):\kappa_{\lambda*}.

Here we use subscript
\lambda
in
\Kir_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
to emphasize the
Aff
-action on
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
is the
\lambda
-action. Similarly we get equivalences of the monodromic and completed versions

\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda}^{*}:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}):\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}.

We put the superscript “
T\textup{-mon}
” in
\Kir^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
to emphasize that the objects there are unipotently monodromic along
T
.

By Lemma
2.1.7
, we have an equivalence

J^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\lambda}:D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Kir^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})

Note a priori Lemma
2.1.7
asserts a similar equivalence with the monodromicity condition under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action on
T
via
\lambda
and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-scaling action on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
. Here we are restricting to the full subcategories of
T
-monodromic objects. The equivalence
J^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\lambda}
induces an equivalence of the completed versions

\widehat{J}_{\lambda}:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})

and the equivalence

\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}\widehat{J}_{\lambda}:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\xrightarrow{\widehat{J}_{\lambda}}\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda).

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
be the universal unipotent local system on
T
. As a representation of the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(T)
, it is the completion of
E[\pi_{1}(T)]
along the augmentation ideal.

The following lemma essentially follows from
[
19
, Lemma A.3.6]
.

5.3.7 Lemma
.

The object
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle
has a canonical structure of a monoidal unit in
\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
under
!
-convolution.

5.3.8 Definition
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

(1)

Define

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}:=\widehat{J}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\in\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

(2)

Let
r=\dim T
. Define the corresponding
free-monodromic standard object
in
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
as

\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}:=i_{\lambda,!}\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\langle{d_{\lambda}+2r}\rangle\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}.

Unraveling the definitions, we arrive at the following description of
\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}\widehat{J}_{\lambda}
.

5.3.9 Lemma
.

Consider the maps

\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}-\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{j_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{T\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}-\{0\})=T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{T}

Here
j_{\lambda}
is the open embedding,
q_{\lambda}
takes
g_{1}\tau^{\lambda}hg_{2}
(where
h\in T,g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
) to
(h,\psi(g_{1}g_{2}))
, and
m_{\lambda}
is the homomorphism sending
(h,s)\mapsto hs^{-\lambda}
for
h\in T
and
s\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Then for
\mathcal{F}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
,
\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda*}\widehat{J}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})
is the image of
j_{\lambda!}q_{\lambda}^{*}m_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{F}[1]
in the Kirillov quotient
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)
. In particular,

\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\cong\mbox{image of $i_{\lambda!}j_{\lambda!}q_{\lambda}^{*}m_{\lambda}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{d_{\lambda}+2r}\rangle[1]$ in $\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}$}.

When
\lambda=0
, the equivalence
\widehat{\kappa}_{0*}\widehat{J}_{0}
has a simpler description.

5.3.10 Lemma
.

Let
\iota_{0}:\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
be the closed embedding. Consider
the composition

(5.18)

\iota_{*}:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\cong\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\right)\xrightarrow{\iota_{0*}}\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}}(\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}.

Here,
\iota_{0*}
naturally lifts to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-equivariant category because
\mathcal{F}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}})
, there is a canonical
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-equivariant structure on
\iota_{0*}\mathcal{F}
(for
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
acts trivially on
T
).

Then there is a canonical isomorphism of functors

\iota_{*}\cong i_{0!}\widehat{\kappa}_{0*}\widehat{J}_{0}:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}.

Moreover,
\iota_{*}
has a natural structure of a monoidal functor. In particular,
\widehat{\Delta}_{0}\cong\iota_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle
is a monoidal unit of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

When
\lambda=0
, the map
T\to T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
given by
h\mapsto(h,0)
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant with respect to the trivial action on
T
and the
\lambda=0
-action on
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
(which is trivial on
T
). The above description of
\widehat{J}_{0}
follows from Example
2.1.5
.

The monoidal structure on the functor (
5.18
) is an easy diagram chase. Since
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle
is a monoidal unit in
\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
by Lemma
5.3.7
,
\widehat{\Delta}_{0}
is a monoidal unit of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
.
∎

Let
\pi^{T}:\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\to\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
be the projection map. We have
\pi^{T}_{!}:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

5.3.11 Lemma
.

We have
\pi^{T}_{!}\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\cong\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

Comparing the description of
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
in Lemma
5.3.9
and the description of
\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
in Lemma
5.3.4
(2), the isomorphism follows from the following statement: Let
p:T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the projection map, then

(5.19)

p_{!}m_{\lambda}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle\cong\underline{E},\quad\mbox{(constant sheaf on $\mathbb{G}_{m}$).}

Since the map
(m_{\lambda},p):T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
is an isomorphism, we have
p_{!}m_{\lambda}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(T,\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle)\otimes\underline{E}\cong\underline{E}
. This proves (
5.19
), and (1) follows.
∎

5.4.
Convolution

The goal here is to describe the monoidal category
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
and its equivariant version
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
in terms of
T
. This will be achieved in Theorem
5.4.6
and Proposition
5.4.9
. Such descriptions for deeper levels were obtained by Kamgarpour and Schedler
[
32
]
categorifying Roche’s results on principal series Hecke algebras. Generalization of Proposition
5.4.9
in another direction (replacing
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
with the unipotent radical of an arbitrary parahoric
\mathbf{P}
) is worked out in Jianqiao Xia’s thesis
[
52
]
.

5.4.1 Lemma
.

The following diagram is canonically commutative

\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\times\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{J}_{\lambda}\times\widehat{J}_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\star_{T}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\times\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\mu}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{J}_{\lambda+\mu}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda+\mu}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})}

Here the
\star_{T}
and
\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}
are the
!
-convolutions under the group structures on
T
and
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the version before completion. We will abbreviate all the
T\textup{-mon}
by
\mathrm{mon}
. We also suppress the superscripts
\mathrm{mon}
from
J^{\mathrm{mon}}
and its inverse
\Phi^{\mathrm{mon}}
.

Let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
and let
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=J_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}(\lambda)}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mu}=J_{\mu}(\mathcal{G})\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mu)}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
.
Our goal is to give a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}\cong J_{\lambda+\mu}(\mathcal{F}\star_{T}\mathcal{G})\in\Kir^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\lambda+\mu}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

We can rewrite this in terms of the inverse
\Phi_{\lambda+\mu}
to
J_{\lambda+\mu}
:

(5.20)

\Phi(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{G}_{\mu})\cong\mathcal{F}\star_{T}\mathcal{G}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T).

We will use
F
denotes the Fourier transform
F:D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X\times\check{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
for various stacks
X
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. By definition,

(5.21)

\mathcal{F}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{G}=m_{T!}(p_{13}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}p_{23}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}).

Here
p_{13},p_{23}:T\times T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\to T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
are the projections,
\star_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
denotes the convolution on
T\times T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
in the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-factor, and
m_{T}:T\times T\to T
is the multiplication map. Applying Fourier transform to both sides of (
5.21
), and using that Fourier transform intertwines tensor product and additive convolution, we get

\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\mathcal{G}_{\mu})=\mathrm{FT}(m_{T!}(p_{13}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}p_{23}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\mu}))\cong m_{T!}(p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\otimes p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu})).

By the construction of the inverse
\Phi
to
J
in Lemma
2.1.7
,
p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\lambda)
-equivariant with restriction equal to
\mathcal{F}
over
T\times\{1\}
;
p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu})
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mu)
-equivariant with restriction equal to
\mathcal{G}
over
T\times\{1\}
. Therefore,
p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\otimes p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu})
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant on
T\times T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
with respect to the
(\lambda,\mu)
-action
s\cdot(h_{1},h_{2},a)=(h_{1}s^{-\lambda},h_{2}s^{-\mu},s^{-1}a)
, and its restriction to to
T\times T\times\{1\}
is
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}
. In other words we have a canonical isomorphism

(5.22)

\Phi(p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\otimes p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}))\cong\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T\times T).

Applying
m_{T!}
to both sides of (
5.22
), using the functoriality of the equivalences
J
and
\Phi
(see Remark
2.1.8
), we get a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi(m_{T!}(p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\otimes p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu})))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle m_{T!}\Phi(p_{13}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\otimes p_{23}^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{G}_{\mu}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle m_{T!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})=\mathcal{F}\star_{T}\mathcal{G}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T).

Combined with (
5.21
) we get (
5.20
).
∎

5.4.2 Proposition
.

For
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{c}_{\lambda,\mu}:\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda+\mu}.

Proof.

We restrict the diagram (
5.4
) to the part relevant for the calculation of
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}
:

\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{\lambda,\mu}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}

We have
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}=m_{\lambda,\mu!}(p^{*}_{1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\otimes p_{2}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+4r}\rangle
.

By Lemma
5.2.4
, the only relevant points in the image of
m_{\lambda,\mu}
are in the double cosets
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
. By Lemma
5.2.1
and Corollary
2.4.4
,
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}
is a successive extension of
i_{\nu!}\mathcal{F}_{\nu}
for
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\nu)
. Since the fiber of
m_{\lambda,\mu}
over
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\nu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is empty for
\nu\neq\lambda+\mu
, we must have

(5.23)

\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}=i_{\lambda+\mu!}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu},

where
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}=i_{\lambda+\mu}^{*}(\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu})\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda+\mu)
.

To compute
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}
, we restrict
m_{\lambda,\mu}
over
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
. Using the translation actions of
T
and
\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
, we can write

(5.24)

\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cong(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\times(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})\times(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

Similarly, using left translation by
T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
, we may write

(5.25)

\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cong T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\times(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}).

Let
F_{\lambda,\mu}
be the fiber of the multiplication map

m_{\lambda,\mu}^{\prime}:\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}

over
\tau^{\lambda+\mu}
. Then the isomorphisms (
5.24
) and (
5.25
) imply the following diagram is Cartesian

\textstyle{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\times F_{\lambda,\mu}\times T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\lambda,\mu}}
\scriptstyle{m_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\circ\textup{pr}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{\lambda,\mu}}
\textstyle{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\lambda+\mu}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}

Here
\alpha_{\lambda,\mu}(h_{1},a_{1},x,t_{2},a_{2})=h_{1}\widetilde{a}_{1}xh_{2}\widetilde{a}_{2}
(where
h_{i}\in T
,
x\in F_{\lambda,\mu}
,
a_{i}\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
, and
\widetilde{a}_{1},\widetilde{a}_{2}
are liftings of
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
to
\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{1}
), and
\alpha_{\lambda+\mu}=i_{\lambda+\mu}\kappa_{\lambda+\mu}
, so that
\alpha_{\lambda+\mu}(h,a)=\tau^{\lambda+\mu}h\widetilde{a}
. The left vertical arrow is the composition of the projection
\textup{pr}:T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\times F_{\lambda,\mu}\times T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\to(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})^{2}
with the group law
m_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}:(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})^{2}\to T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
. Therefore by proper base change

\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda+\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}=\alpha^{*}_{\lambda+\mu}(\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu})\cong m_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a},!}\textup{pr}_{!}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\underline{E}\boxtimes\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+4r}\rangle.

By Lemma
5.2.5
,
F_{\lambda,\mu}
is an affine space of dimension
(d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda+\mu})/2
. The fundamental cycle of
F_{\lambda,\mu}
gives a canonical isomorphism

R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda,\mu},E)\cong E\langle{-d_{\lambda}-d_{\mu}+d_{\lambda+\mu}}\rangle.

Therefore we have a canonical isomorphism

\textup{pr}_{!}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\underline{E}\boxtimes\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+4r}\rangle\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu}\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+4r}\rangle\otimes R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda,\mu},E)\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\boxtimes\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu}\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu}+4r}\rangle.

Therefore

(5.26)

\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda+\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu}\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu}+4r}\rangle\in\widehat{\Kir}^{T\textup{-mon}}_{\lambda+\mu}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

By Lemma
5.4.1
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu}=\widehat{J}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\widehat{J}_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\cong\widehat{J}_{\lambda+\mu}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\star_{T}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}).

By Lemma
5.3.7
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\star_{T}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{-2r}\rangle

we get

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\star_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu}\cong\widehat{J}_{\lambda+\mu}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\langle{-2r}\rangle\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda+\mu}\langle{-2r}\rangle.

Combined with (
5.26
) we get a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\kappa}_{\lambda+\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda+\mu}\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu}+2r}\rangle\quad\mbox{ or }\quad\mathcal{F}_{\lambda+\mu}\cong\kappa_{\lambda+\mu*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda+\mu}\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu}+2r}\rangle.

Combined with (
5.23
), we conclude with a canonical isomorphism
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda+\mu}
.

∎

Similar argument applied to the convolution in the equivariant version
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
shows the following.

5.4.3 Proposition
.

For
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

c_{\lambda,\mu}:{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\star{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\cong{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda+\mu}.

Let
T^{\vee}
be the torus over
E
dual to
T
, i.e.,
\mathbb{X}^{*}(T^{\vee})=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. We have a monoidal equivalence
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\cong\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
(
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-graded finite-dimensional vector spaces over
E
).

5.4.4 Lemma
.

The functor

{}^{T}\phi^{T}:\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}

sending
E(\lambda)
(the one-dimensional representation of
T^{\vee}
with weight
\lambda
) to
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
has a canonical structure of a monoidal functor.

Proof.

Using the isomorphisms constructed in Proposition
5.4.3
, it remains to check that these isomorphisms satisfy associativity: for
\lambda,\mu,\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we need to show that
\alpha:=c_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}\circ(c_{\lambda,\mu}\star\textup{id}_{\nu})
and
\beta:=c_{\lambda,\mu+\nu}\circ(\textup{id}_{\lambda}\star c_{\mu,\nu})
are the same isomorphisms between
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\star{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\nu}
and
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda+\mu+\nu}
. These two isomorphisms differ only by a scalar, therefore it suffices to show that
\alpha
and
\beta
agree after taking stalks at
\tau^{\lambda+\mu+\nu}
.

As in the proof of Proposition
5.4.2
, we use
F_{\lambda,\mu}
to denote the fiber over
\tau^{\lambda+\mu}
of the convolution map of the
\tau^{\lambda}
and
\tau^{\mu}
double cosets. Let
F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
be the fiber over
\tau^{\lambda+\mu+\nu}
of the triple convolution map

m_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}:\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\times^{T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\times^{T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\nu}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\to G(\!(\tau)\!).

Using the realization of
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
as
\iota^{T}_{\lambda!}E\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle
as in Lemma
5.3.4
, the object
(i^{T}_{\lambda+\mu+\nu})^{*}({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\star{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\nu})\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda+\mu+\nu)\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect})
is the stalk of the direct image
m_{\lambda,\mu,\nu!}\underline{E}\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+d_{\nu}}\rangle
. This stalk is
R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+d_{\nu}}\rangle
. The stalks of both

\alpha
and
\beta
at
\tau^{\lambda+\mu+\nu}
give isomorphisms

z_{\alpha},z_{\beta}:R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+d_{\nu}}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}E\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu+\nu}}\rangle.

It suffices to show that
z_{\alpha}=z_{\beta}
.

Let
d=\frac{1}{2}(d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+d_{\nu}-d_{\lambda+\mu+\nu})
. Then the above isomorphisms show that
\dim F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}=d
and it has only one
d
-dimensional irreducible component. The isomorphisms
z_{\alpha}
and
z_{\beta}
each give an
E
-basis of
\textup{H}_{c}^{2d}({F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}})(d)
. All we need to show is that the two bases are the same.

Now let
m_{12}:F_{\lambda,\mu\,\nu}\to G(\!(\tau)\!)/(T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
be the multiplication of the first two factors. Let
F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}=m_{12}^{-1}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda+\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/T\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. Then
m_{12}
restricts to a surjective map
F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}\to F_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}
whose fibers are isomorphic to
F_{\lambda,\mu}
. We see
\dim F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}=\dim F_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}+\dim F_{\lambda,\mu}=d
, and
R\Gamma_{c}(F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu})\cong E\langle{-2d}\rangle
. In particular, the inclusion
F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}\hookrightarrow F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
induces an isomorphism on
R\Gamma_{c}
, which implied
F^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}=F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
. By construction,
z_{\alpha}\in\textup{H}_{c}^{2d}({F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}})(d)
then corresponds to the composition

R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu})\langle{d_{\lambda}+d_{\mu}+d_{\nu}}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}R\Gamma_{c}(F_{\lambda+\mu,\nu})\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu}+d_{\nu}}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}E\langle{d_{\lambda+\mu+\nu}}\rangle.

Here the first map is capping with the relative fundamental class of
m_{12}:F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}\to F_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}
and the second one is capping with the fundamental class of
F_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}
. Altogether we see that
z_{\alpha}
is capping with the fundamental class of
F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
.

Similarly, by considering the multiplication of the second and third factors in
F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
we get a map
m_{23}:F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}\to F_{\lambda,\mu+\nu}
whose fibers are isomorphic to
F_{\mu,\nu}
. The map
z_{\beta}
can be similarly written as a composition, from which we see that it is also given by capping with the fundamental class of
F_{\lambda,\mu,\nu}
. This shows that
z_{\alpha}=z_{\beta}
and finishes the proof.
∎

5.4.5 Corollary
.

Consider the additive functor

(5.27)

\phi:\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}

sending
E(\lambda)
to
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
. Then
\phi
has a canonical structure of a monoidal functor.

Proof.

The image of
\phi
lies in the abelian category
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon},\heartsuit}_{\infty}[r]
, therefore it suffices to check that the canonical isomorphisms
\widehat{c}_{\lambda,\mu}
constructed in Proposition
5.4.2
satisfy associativity: for
\lambda,\mu,\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, the two isomorphisms
\widehat{\alpha}:=\widehat{c}_{\lambda+\mu,\nu}\circ(\widehat{c}_{\lambda,\mu}\star\textup{id}_{\nu})
and
\widehat{\beta}:=\widehat{c}_{\lambda,\mu+\nu}\circ(\textup{id}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{c}_{\mu,\nu})
between
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\nu}
and
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda+\mu+\nu}
are the same. Let
\widehat{\gamma}:=\widehat{\beta}\circ\widehat{\alpha}^{-1}\in\textup{Aut}(\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda+\mu+\nu})\cong\textup{Aut}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})
. By the usual spreading-out argument, we may reduced to the case where the base field
k
is a finite field, and the relevant sheaves all carry canonical Weil structures. The canonical maps
\widehat{c}_{\lambda,\mu}
also respect Weil structures. In particular,
\widehat{\gamma}
respects the Weil structure of the universal unipotently monodromic local system
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
on
T
. By
[
19
, Lemma A.4.3]
, we have

\textup{End}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\cong\widehat{\textup{Sym}}_{E}(\textup{H}_{1}({T,E}))

Here
\widehat{\textup{Sym}}
means completion with respect to the augmentation ideal. Since
\textup{H}_{1}({T,E})
has weight
-2
, we see that the only endomorphisms of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
preserving Weil structures are scalars in
E
. Since
\widehat{\gamma}
is a scalar, it is the same scalar as its
T
-equivariant version, which is shown to be
1
in Lemma
5.4.4
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Recall the monoidal full embedding
\iota_{*}:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
from Lemma
5.3.10
.

We denote by
\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
the category of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-graded objects in
D(T)
. This is a monoidal category.

5.4.6 Theorem
.

Consider the additive functor

\Phi:\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}

sending
\mathcal{F}(\lambda)
(for
\mathcal{F}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
put in degree
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
) to
\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
. Then
\Phi
has a canonical monoidal structure making it a monoidal equivalence.

Proof.

Monoidal structure of
\Phi
comes from the monoidal structure of
\iota_{*}
and
\phi
(see Corollary
5.4.5
). It remains to show that
\Phi
is an equivalence of the underlying categories.

We show
\Phi
is essentially surjective. Let
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
be the essential image of the full embedding
i_{\lambda!}:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
. From the convolution diagram we see that convolving with
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
gives an equivalence

(-)\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{S}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}.

Since
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
are clearly in the image of
\Phi
,
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
is in the essential image of
\Phi
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. By Lemma
5.2.1
and Corollary
2.4.4
,
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
is generated by
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, therefore
\Phi
is essentially surjective..

To show
\Phi
is fully faithful, it suffices to show for
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
and
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)

\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}}(\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda},\iota_{*}\mathcal{G}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu})=\begin{cases}\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})&\lambda=\mu,\\
0&\lambda\neq\mu.\end{cases}

If
\lambda=\mu
, using that
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
is invertible, we get

\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}}(\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda},\iota_{*}\mathcal{G}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda})\cong\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}(\iota_{*}\mathcal{F},\iota_{*}\mathcal{G})=\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

because
\iota_{*}
is fully faithful.

If
\lambda\neq\mu
, we may convolve both objects by
\widehat{\Delta}_{-\mu}
and get

\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}}(\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda},\iota_{*}\mathcal{G}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu})\cong\textup{Hom}_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}}((\iota_{*}\mathcal{F})\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda-\mu},\iota_{*}\mathcal{G}).

Now
\iota_{*}\mathcal{G}
and
(\iota_{*}\mathcal{F})\star\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda-\mu}
are both
!
-extensions from disjoint strata
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda-\mu}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
of
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
, their Hom space is zero. This finishes the proof that
\Phi
is an equivalence.

∎

5.4.7 Corollary
.

The natural transformation of functors
i_{\lambda!}\to i_{\lambda*}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda),\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We use the notation
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
from the proof of Theorem
5.4.6
, which is the essential image of
i_{\lambda!}
. Theorem
5.4.6
implies that
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
is the direct sum of
\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. In particular, for
i_{\lambda!}\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}
and
i_{\mu!}\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}
, and
\lambda\neq\mu
, we have
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}(i_{\mu!}\mathcal{G},i_{\lambda!}\mathcal{F})=0
. This implies
i_{\lambda!}\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{\lambda*}\mathcal{F}
for any
\mathcal{F}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}(\lambda)
.
∎

5.4.8.
T
-equivariant version

We briefly record the description of the bi-
T
-equivariant monoidal category
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}
parallel to the discussions on
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
. The proofs are similar to the monodromic case, which we omit.

The map
{}^{T}\iota^{T}:\textup{pt}/T\to\frac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}\hookrightarrow\frac{(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}
induces a monoidal full subcategory
{}^{T}\iota^{T}_{*}:D(\textup{pt}/T)\cong{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(0)\hookrightarrow{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

5.4.9 Proposition
.

(1)

The natural transformation of functors
{}^{T}i^{T}_{\lambda!}\to{}^{T}i^{T}_{\lambda*}\in\mathrm{Fun}({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}(\lambda),{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The functor

{}^{T}\Phi^{T}:D(\textup{pt}/T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}

sending
\mathcal{F}(\lambda)
to
\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\star({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda})
has a canonical monoidal structure making it into a monoidal equivalence.

(3)

The functor
{}^{T}\Phi^{T}
restricts to a monoidal equivalence

(5.28)

\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}

sending
E(\lambda)
to
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

6.
The category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}

In this section we study the category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
that carries commuting actions of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and the Satake category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
. We determine the structure of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
as module categories under both. The actions on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
give a monoidal functor
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
which we interpret as taking microstalks. As a byproduct, we construct a fiber functor on the Satake category using microstalks, categorifying an old result of Evans and Mirkovic
[
22
]
.

Some of the proofs in §
6.4
are postponed to the next section.

6.1.
Definitions

Recall
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Consider the moduli stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi})
. Using uniformization at
\infty
, we may write

(6.1)

\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi})=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t].

The additive group
\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
acts on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
by left translation under the above double coset description. On the other hand,
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
acts on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
by rotation on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
These actions combine to give an action of
\textup{Aff}=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. Consider the corresponding Kirillov category

\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi})).

The heart of the perverse t-structure of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
(inherited from
D(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi}))
) is denoted
\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

6.1.1.
Relevant points

Recall from §
4.6
that
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a}
. By definition, the relevant points for
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
are the images of the projection
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, let
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
be the point corresponding to the double coset of
t^{\lambda}
under the isomorphism (
6.1
); its image in
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
is the point
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
defined in §
4.6.2
. The
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-orbits of
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
are exactly the relevant points of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
under the
Aff
-action.

Let
A_{\lambda}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})G[t]=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})G[t]
be the automorphism group of
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
, which is also the automorphism group of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
. We have the locally closed substack

\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\cong\textup{pt}/A_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})

consisting of one point
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\}
. Taking its preimage in
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
we get the locally closed substack which is the
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-orbits of
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}

(6.2)

\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}:\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\cong(\textup{pt}/A_{\lambda})\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}).

For varying
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, these are exactly the relevant strata for
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. The map
\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}
sends
a\in\mathbb{G}_{a}=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
to
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\widetilde{a}t^{\lambda}G[t]
in the double coset description (for any lifting
\widetilde{a}
of
a
in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
). Then
\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}
is
Aff
-equivariant with respect to the tautological action on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
, and the loop rotation action on
A_{\lambda}
.

6.1.2 Lemma
.

\dim A_{\lambda}=d_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

The Lie algebra of
A_{\lambda}
is spanned by affine root spaces in
\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
that are not in
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
. Hence it has the same dimension as
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cong\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
. Comparing with (
5.9
) we get
\dim A_{\lambda}=d_{\lambda}
.
∎

6.1.3.
Standard objects

The embedding
\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}
gives functors

\displaystyle\beta_{\lambda,!}:\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}}\Kir((\textup{pt}/A_{\lambda})\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\overline{\beta}_{\lambda!}}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}},

\displaystyle\beta_{\lambda,*}:\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}}\Kir((\textup{pt}/A_{\lambda})\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\overline{\beta}_{\lambda*}}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}.

Here the pullback
\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}
is an equivalence because
A_{\lambda}
is a unipotent group.

Let
\delta\in\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
be the skyscraper sheaf at
\{0\}
. We define the standard objects in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
:

\displaystyle\delta_{\lambda}=\beta_{\lambda,!}\delta\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle.

Since the embedding
\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}
is affine, we have
\delta_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
. By Corollary
2.4.4
,
\{\delta_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
generate
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

6.2.
Hecke action at
\infty

From the double coset description (
6.1
), we see there is a left action of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
using the
Aff
-equivariant diagram

(6.3)

\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]}
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}{\Delta\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]}

We denote this action by

(-)\star_{\infty}(-):\mathcal{H}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}.

6.2.1 Lemma
.

(1)

Restricting the
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
-action to
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
, the action extends to an action of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

(2)

Moreover, for
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\delta_{\lambda-\mu}.

Proof.

Since
\{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
generate
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
and
\{\delta_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
generate
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
, (2) implies (1). We now prove (2).

First we prove the special case where
\mu=\lambda
, i.e.,

(6.4)

\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\delta_{0}.

Restricting to the relevant part of the convolution diagram (
6.3
):

m_{\lambda,\lambda}:\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\to\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t].

Consider the subgroups
\mathbf{K}_{+}:=\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})G[t]\cap\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}=\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})G[t]\cap\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
and
\mathbf{K}_{-}:=\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
of
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
(the first one is finite-dimensional, the second finite-codimensional). Then the multiplication map
\mathbf{K}_{+}\times\mathbf{K}_{-}\to\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is an isomorphism. This can be seen by checking at the level of Lie algebras (using that
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
is pro-unipotent). This implies
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{-\lambda}G[t]=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{K}_{+}\mathbf{K}_{-}\tau^{-\lambda}G[t]=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T\textup{Ad}(\tau^{\lambda})\mathbf{K}_{+}\textup{Ad}(\tau^{\lambda})\mathbf{K}^{-}G[t]=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}G[t]
. Moreover, identifying
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]
with
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{K}_{+}
(as
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
-homogeneous spaces), and identifying
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
with
\mathbf{K}_{-}^{\psi}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times T
(as
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\times T
homogeneous spaces, where
\mathbf{K}_{-}^{\psi}=\mathbf{K}_{-}\cap\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
), we can fit
m_{\lambda,\lambda}
into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{\lambda,\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}T\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{-}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}}{{\times}}\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{K}_{+})\times T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}G[t]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{K}_{-}^{\psi}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{K}_{+})\times T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{a}\times T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{a}}

Using this diagram, we see that

(6.5)

\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\beta_{0!}(\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},!}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\langle{2r}\rangle))

where
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\in\widehat{\Kir}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\lambda}(T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
corresponds to the universal local system
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{mon}}(T)
under the equivalence
\widehat{J}_{\lambda}
. Note that the shift-twists
\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle
in
\delta_{\lambda}
and
\langle{d_{\lambda}}\rangle
in
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}
cancel each other. By the functoriality in Remark
2.1.8
,
\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},!}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\langle{2r}\rangle)
corresponds to
R\Gamma_{c}(T,\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\langle{2r}\rangle)=E
under the equivalence
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong D(\textup{Vect})
, which implies that
\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a},!}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\langle{2r}\rangle)
is the image of
\delta\in D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{G}_{a})
in
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
. Hence the right side of (
6.5
) is canonically isomorphic to
\beta_{0!}\delta=\delta_{0}
. This proves (
6.4
).

Applying
\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}
to both sides of (
6.4
), and using that
\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}
is the monoidal unit, we get

(6.6)

\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{0}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\delta_{\lambda}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{0}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\delta_{\lambda}.

For general
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, by Proposition
5.4.2
we have a canonical isomorphism
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda+\mu}
. Hence we get canonical isomorphisms

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{0}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{0}\cong\delta_{\lambda-\mu}.

where we use (
6.6
) twice, first for
\lambda
and then for
\lambda-\mu
.
∎

Recall from Corollary
5.4.5
the monoidal functor
\phi:\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
, which is conservative but not fully faithful. The action of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
makes
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
into a
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-module category, hence also a
D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
-module category.

6.2.2 Proposition
.

(1)

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, the natural transformation
\beta_{\lambda,!}\to\beta_{\lambda,*}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a}),\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The functor

\oplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\beta_{\lambda!}:\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}D^{b}(\textup{Vect})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}

is an equivalence.

(3)

We have a canonical t-exact equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\cong D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit})
.

Proof.

(1) Since
\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})
is generated by
\delta
, equivalently we need to show

(6.7)

\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\delta_{\lambda},\delta_{\mu})=0,\quad\forall\lambda\neq\mu.

Since
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
acts on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
and
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
is invertible, it suffices to show

\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda},\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda})=0.

By Lemma
6.2.1
, the above is
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}(\delta_{0},\delta_{\mu-\lambda})
, which by adjunction is
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})}(\delta,\overline{\beta}_{0}^{!}\delta_{\mu-\lambda})
. Now
\overline{\beta}_{0}
is an open embedding
\mathbb{G}_{a}\cong\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}G[t]/G[t]\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
and
\delta_{\mu-\lambda}
is supported on the complement of the open stratum
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}G[t]/G[t]
, we have
\overline{\beta}_{0}^{!}\delta_{\mu-\lambda}=\overline{\beta}_{0}^{*}\delta_{\mu-\lambda}=0
and the desired vanishing follows.

(2) Each
\beta_{\lambda!}
is fully faithful. By (
6.7
), the functor
\oplus\beta_{\lambda!}
is also fully faithful. By Corollary
2.4.4
,
\oplus\beta_{\lambda!}
is essentially surjective.

(3) follows from (2).
∎

6.2.3 Corollary
.

(1)

As a
D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
-module category,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one, and
\delta_{\lambda}
is a generator for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

(2)

The action of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
perserves the heart
\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. As a
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-module category,
\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one, and
\delta_{\lambda}
is a generator for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

(1) follows directly from Proposition
6.2.2
(2). (2) follows from Lemma
6.2.1
(2).
∎

6.3.
T
-equivariant version

Consider the variant of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
by further imposing
T
-equivariance at
\infty

{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}:=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)).

The relevant strata for
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
are

{}^{T}\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}:\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)=(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\cong\textup{pt}/(A_{\lambda}T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a}\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T).

We also have the
T
-equivariant version of
\beta_{\lambda,!}
and
\beta_{\lambda,*}
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,!}:\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}}\Kir(\textup{pt}/(A_{\lambda}T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\overline{\beta}_{\lambda!}}{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}},

(6.9)

\displaystyle{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,*}:\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}}\Kir(\textup{pt}/(A_{\lambda}T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\overline{\beta}_{\lambda*}}{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}.

If we denote by
{}^{T}\delta\in\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
the skyscraper sheaf at
\textup{pt}/T\times\{0\}
, then

{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}={}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,!}({}^{T}\delta)\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle

is a canonical
T
-equivariant lifting of
\delta_{\lambda}
.

The monoidal category
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
acts on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. We still denote this action by
\star_{\infty}
.

There is a
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-action on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
via the monoidal functor
\phi:\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
in Corollary
5.4.5
, where
E(\lambda)
acts by
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\star_{\infty}(-)
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. On the other hand, Proposition
5.4.9
(3) gives an equivalence
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
, which in particular gives an action of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

6.3.1 Lemma
.

The forgetful functor
\omega:{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
has a canonical structure of a functor of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-module categories.

Proof.

Recall the functor
\pi^{T}_{!}:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}\to\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
. We have the convolution
\mathcal{H}^{T}_{\infty}\times{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. By Lemma
5.3.11
,
\pi^{T}_{!}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\cong\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
and any
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(6.10)

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\omega(\mathcal{F})\cong(\pi^{T}_{!}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu})\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}\cong\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}.

Note that
\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
is the image of
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
under the forgetful functor
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
. Therefore

\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}\cong\omega({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}).

Combined with (
6.10
) we get a canonical isomorphism functorial in
\mathcal{F}

(6.11)

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\omega(\mathcal{F})\cong\omega({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}).

It is easy to check that this isomorphism is compatible with convolutions of the
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}
’s and the
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
’s. This proves that
\omega
has a canonical structure of a functor of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-modules.
∎

We give
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
the perverse t-structure inherited from the forgetful functor to
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

6.3.2 Lemma
.

The forgetful functor
\omega^{\heartsuit}:{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
T
is connected,
\omega^{\heartsuit}
is fully faithful. Since any object in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is a direct sum of
\{\delta_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
, and each
\delta_{\lambda}
is the image of
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}
under
\omega^{\heartsuit}
, we conclude that
\omega^{\heartsuit}
is also essentially surjective, hence an equivalence.
∎

6.3.3 Corollary
.

For
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

(6.12)

{}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}^{T}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}\cong{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda-\mu}.

Proof.

Lemma
6.3.1
applied to the actions of
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}
and
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
implies a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\delta_{\lambda}\cong\omega({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda})

By Lemma
6.2.1
, the left side above is canonically isomorphic to
\delta_{\lambda-\mu}
. In particular both sides are in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
, and we have a canonical isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}

(6.13)

\omega^{\heartsuit}({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda})\cong\delta_{\lambda-\mu}\cong\omega^{\heartsuit}({}^{T}\delta_{\lambda-\mu}).

Since
\omega^{\heartsuit}
is an equivalence by Lemma
6.3.2
, (
6.13
) comes from a unique isomorphism
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}\cong{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda-\mu}
, as desired.

∎

6.3.4 Corollary
.

The action of
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}\cong\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
preserves
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
. The forgetful functor
\omega^{\heartsuit}:{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is an equivalence of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-module categories.

Proof.

The fact that
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
preservves
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
follows from Corollary
6.3.3
and the fact that
\{{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
generate
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
. The rest follows from Lemma
6.3.1
and Lemma
6.3.2
.
∎

6.3.5 Corollary
.

(1)

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, the natural transformation
{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,!}\to{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,*}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Kir((\textup{pt}/T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a}),{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The functor

\oplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda!}:\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\Kir((\textup{pt}/T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}D(\textup{pt}/T)\to{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}

is an equivalence.

(3)

As a
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
-module category,
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one, and
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}
is a generator for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

(4)

As a
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
-module category,
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is free of rank one, and
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}
is a generator for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

(1) follows from the non-
T
-equivariant version Proposition
6.2.2
(1), because the forgetful functor
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})
is conservative.

(2) follows from (1) as in the proof of Proposition
6.2.2
(2).

(3) Proposition
5.4.9
(2) identifies
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
with
D(\textup{pt}/T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
and part (2) above identifies
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
also with
D(\textup{pt}/T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
. By
Corollary
6.3.3
, the action functor
(-)\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{0}:{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
can be identified with the auto-equivalence of
D(\textup{pt}/T)\otimes\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
given by the identity on
D(\textup{pt}/T)
and the inversion map on
T^{\vee}
. This shows that
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one over
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
with generator
{}^{T}\delta_{0}
. For general
\lambda
,
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}\cong{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{0}
by Corollary
6.3.3
, and
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}
is invertible, hence
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}
is also a free generator for
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

(4) The action map
(-)\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}:{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is an equivalence by (3) and is t-exact by Corollary
6.3.3
. Therefore
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is free of rank one over
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
, with generator
{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}
.

∎

6.4.
Spherical Hecke action

Consider the affine Grassmannian
\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]
with the action of
G[\![t]\!]\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
(where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
acts on
G[\![t]\!]
by scaling
t
) by left translation and loop rotation.
Let
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
be the category of
G[\![t]\!]
-equivariant constructible complexes on
Gr
supported on finitely many
G[\![t]\!]
-orbits (the subscript
0
in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
emphasizes that
t
is the local coordinate at
0\in X
). Note that all objects in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
are automatically
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-monodromic. Let
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
be the heart of the perverse t-structure (as sheaves on
Gr
). The geometric Satake equivalence of Lusztig, Ginzburg, Beilinson-Drinfeld and Mirkovic-Vilonen gives a canonical symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
(extending the monoidal structure given by the convolution product) and an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories

\mathrm{Sat}:\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\cong\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee}).

Via modification of bundles at
0\in X
, we get an action of
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. More precisely, consider the diagram

(6.14)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Hk}_{G,0}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{h_{2}}
\textstyle{G[\![t]\!]\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})}

where
\mathrm{Hk}_{G,0}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
classifies a pair of
G
-bundles on
X
with
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
level structures
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
and an isomorphism
\tau:\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{X\backslash\{0\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{X\backslash\{0\}}
. The map
h_{i}
records
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, and
\textup{ev}_{0}
records the relative position of
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
on the formal disk around
0
. The maps
h_{1},h_{2}
are
Aff
-equivariant. The map
\textup{ev}_{0}
is
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-invariant, and it descends to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-equivariant map

\overline{\textup{ev}}_{0}:\mathrm{Hk}_{G,0}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})\to G[\![t]\!]\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!].

The construction of integral transform on Kirillov categories §
2.3.9
gives a functor

D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}\textup{-mon}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{G,0}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}))\to\textup{End}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}).

Precomposing it with
\overline{\textup{ev}}_{0}^{*}
we get a functor

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\overline{\textup{ev}}_{0}^{*}}D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}\textup{-mon}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{G,0}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}))\to\textup{End}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}).

This defines an action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. We denote the action of
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
by
(-)\star_{0}\mathcal{K}
, which is given by the formula
(-)\star_{0}\mathcal{K}=h_{2!}(h_{1}^{*}(-)\otimes\textup{ev}^{*}_{0}\mathcal{K})
.

Using the coset descriptions, we can rewrite the diagram (
6.14
) as

(6.15)

\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G[t]}}{{\times}}G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{G[t]\backslash G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G[\![t]\!]\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]}

Here
\textup{pr}_{1},\textup{pr}_{2}
are projections to the first and second factors, and
m
is the multiplication map on
G(\!(\tau)\!)
.

Similarly, for the
T
-equivariant version, we have an action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
, still denoted by
\star_{0}
.

6.4.1.
The monoidal functor
\rho

The action of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
commutes with the action of
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
since they are defined by modifications at different points. The spherical action thus gives a monoidal functor

(6.16)

\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to\textup{End}_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})^{\textup{rev}}\to\textup{End}_{D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})^{\textup{rev}}.

Here
(-)^{\textup{rev}}
means reversing the order of the monoidal operation.
By Corollary
6.2.3
,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one under
D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
, with generator
\delta_{0}
. Therefore we get a canonical monoidal equivalence

(6.17)

\textup{End}_{D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})^{\textup{rev}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))

The equivalence (
6.17
) sends
F:\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
to the unique object
V=\oplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}V(\lambda)\in D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
defined by

V(\lambda)=\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\delta_{\lambda},F(\delta_{0}))\in D^{b}(\textup{Vect}).

Composing (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) we get a monoidal functor
\rho

(6.18)

\rho:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})).

Unraveling definitions, for
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
, we have

\rho(\mathcal{K})=\oplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})\in D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})),

where
\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})\in D^{b}(\textup{Vect})
is defined by

(6.19)

\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})=\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\delta_{\lambda},\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K}).

6.4.2.
Equivariant version of
\rho

For the
T
-equivariant version, the commuting action of
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
gives a monoidal functor

(6.20)

{}^{T}\rho:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to\textup{End}_{{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}}({}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})^{\textup{rev}}\cong{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}.

Here we use the fact that
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is free of rank one over
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
by Corollary
6.3.5
(
3
). Writing
{}^{T}\rho=\oplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}
where
{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D(\textup{pt}/T)
, we have

(6.21)

\displaystyle{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}=\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}_{{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}({}^{T}\delta_{\lambda},{}^{T}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K})\in D(\textup{pt}/T).

Thus
\rho
is naturally isomorphic to the composition

(6.22)

\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\rho}{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}\cong D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))\otimes D(\textup{pt}/T)\xrightarrow{p^{*}}D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))

where the last functor is the pullback along
p:\textup{pt}\to\textup{pt}/T
.

6.4.3 Theorem
.

(1)

The action of
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
preserves the heart
\mathcal{D}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. In particular,
\rho|_{\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}}
gives a monoidal functor

\rho^{\heartsuit}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\cong\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\to\textup{End}_{\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})}(\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit})^{\textup{rev}}\cong\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}).

Here the last equivalence uses Corollary
6.2.3
(2).

(2)

There is a canonical isomorphism of monoidal functors

\rho^{\heartsuit}\cong\textup{inv}\circ\textup{Res}^{G^{\vee}}_{T^{\vee}}:\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\textup{Res}^{G^{\vee}}_{T^{\vee}}}\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\textup{inv}}\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}).

Here
\textup{inv}:\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
is the tensor equivalence induced by the inversion on
T^{\vee}
.

The proof will be given in §
7.3
, after some preparation on the Iwahori-level analogue of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. Another possible approach to Theorem
6.4.3
(2) is to prove that
\rho^{\heartsuit}
is compatible with the commutativity constraints of
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
and
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
using Hecke action at multiple moving points on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

6.5.
Microlocalization of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}

In this subsection we work over
k=\mathbb{C}
and the analytic topology.

By the discussion in §
3
, we have a microlocalization functor

(6.23)

M_{\mathbf{G}}:\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}_{1}\mathbb{G}_{a})=\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}).

By Lemma
4.6.3
,
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is a discrete set indexed by
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Let
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})\subset\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})
be the full subcategory of objects supported on finitely many points. Then we have a canonical equivalence

\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}).

Here
\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
is the category of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-graded vector spaces. Composing with this equivalence, we can rewrite the microlocalization functor
M_{\mathbf{G}}
as

M_{\mathbf{G}}:\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}).

6.5.1.
Thick Grassmannian

To study the microlocalization functor, it will be convenient to work with a uniformization of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
by a pro-smooth scheme.

Let
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
be the moduli space of pairs
(\mathcal{E},\sigma)
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a
G
-bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\sigma:\mathcal{E}|_{D_{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{triv}}|_{D_{\infty}}
is a trivialization of
\mathcal{E}
over the formal disk
D_{\infty}
at
\infty
. We may identify
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}(k)
with cosets

\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\cong G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t].

The scheme
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
has an open covering by projective limits of smooth varieties with smooth transition maps.

There is a left action of the loop group
G(\!(\tau)\!)
on
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
, whose quotient by
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
(resp.
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
) is canonical identified with
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
(resp.
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
). The quotient map
\pi_{\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}}:\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
is a
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
-torsor.
Recall we have the locally closed substack
\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})=[\{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\}/A_{\lambda}]
, which corresponds to the double coset
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]
. Let

\Sigma_{\lambda}=\pi^{-1}_{\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}}(\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}))=\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\subset\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}.

Then
\Sigma_{\lambda}
is pro-smooth. Similarly define

\Sigma^{\psi}_{\lambda}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]/G[t]\subset\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}

which is the preimage of
[\{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}\}/A_{\lambda}]\subset\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
.

The proof of the following result uses ideas from the work of Evans–Mirkovic
[
22
]
that shows the vanishing of microstalks with the help of torus action.

6.5.2 Theorem
.

(1)

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
,
M_{\mathbf{G}}(\delta_{\lambda})
is supported at
(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\psi_{1}dt)\in\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
with one-dimensional microstalk in degree zero.

(2)

The microlocalization functor
M_{\mathbf{G}}
in (
6.23
) is an equivalence

M_{\mathbf{G}}:\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Vect}_{\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}).

Proof.

In view of Proposition
6.2.2
, (1) implies (2).

We work with the uniformized pro-smooth moduli space
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
