-equivariant complexes on it. The microstalk of
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
at
(\mathcal{E}_{\nu},\psi=\psi_{1}dt)
is computed as follows.
We have the microlocalization functor along
\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}
:

\mu_{\nu}:=\mu_{\Sigma_{\nu}^{\psi}/\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}}:D_{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}})\to D_{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}}(T^{*}_{\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}).

Viewing
\psi=\psi_{1}dt
as an element in the conormal fiber of
T^{*}_{\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
at
t^{\nu}\in\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}
, the microstalk
M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{F})_{\nu}
is the stalk of
\mu_{\nu}(\mathcal{F})
at
(t^{\nu},\psi)
.

By Lemma
A.4.1
, due to the
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
-equivariance, the restriction of
\mu_{\nu}
to
t^{\nu}
can be calculated by restricting to a transversal slice
\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
to
\Sigma_{\nu}^{\psi}
through
t^{\nu}
and microlocalizing along the point
t^{\nu}
. A transversal slice to
\Sigma_{\nu}
at
t^{\nu}
is given by

S_{\nu}=U_{\nu}[t]t^{\nu}G[t]/G[t]\subset\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}.

Here
U_{\nu}\subset G
is the unipotent subgroup whose Lie algebra is the direct sum of root spaces
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
such that
\langle{\alpha,\nu}\rangle>0
. The fact that
S_{\nu}
is a transversal slice to
\Sigma_{\nu}
at
t^{\nu}
can be checked by computing the tangent spaces. To get a transversal slice to
\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}
, we choose a line
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathfrak{t}=\textup{Lie}\ T
such that
\psi|_{\mathfrak{a}}:\mathfrak{a}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is an isomorphism. Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\subset\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{1}
be the subgroup consisting of
1+a\tau
for
a\in\mathfrak{a}
. Then

\widetilde{S}_{\nu}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot S_{\nu}\cong\mathfrak{a}\times S_{\nu}

is a transversal slice to
\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}
at
t^{\nu}
. The covector
\psi_{1}dt
on
(N_{\Sigma^{\psi}_{\nu}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}})_{t^{\nu}}
, after identifying the latter with
T_{t^{\nu}}\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
, is proportional to the projection to the first factor
\textup{pr}_{\mathfrak{a}}:T_{t^{\nu}}\widetilde{S}_{\nu}\to T_{0}\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}
. Therefore we have

(6.24)

M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{F})_{\nu}=\mathrm{FT}(\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F}))|_{\psi_{1}dt}=\phi(\textup{pr}_{\mathfrak{a}*}\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F}))

where
\phi(-)
denotes the monodromic vanishing cycles on the line
\mathfrak{a}
towards zero.

Let
\mathbb{G}_{m}(-\nu)=\mathbb{G}_{m}
, with the action on
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
by right translation (equivalently adjoint action) via
-\nu:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T
. This action stabilizes
\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
and contracts
\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
to
\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}:=t^{\nu}\cdot\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\cong\mathfrak{a}
. Then
\mathbb{G}_{m}(-\nu)
also acts on
T_{t^{\nu}}\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
and contracts it to
T_{t^{\nu}}(t^{\nu}\cdot\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})\cong T_{0}\mathfrak{a}
. Let
k:\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
be the inclusion, whose tangent map at
t^{\nu}\in\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}
is
k_{0}:T_{t^{\nu}}(\mathfrak{a}_{\nu})\cong T_{0}\mathfrak{a}\hookrightarrow T_{t^{\nu}}\widetilde{S}_{\nu}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{F}
is
T
-equivariant, hence
\mathbb{G}_{m}(-\nu)
-equivariant, and so is
\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F})
. By the contraction principle, we have a canonical isomorphism

(6.25)

\textup{pr}_{\mathfrak{a}*}\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F})\cong k_{0}^{*}\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F}).

By Lemma
A.1.3
, we have

(6.26)

k_{0}^{*}\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\widetilde{S}_{\nu}}(\mathcal{F})\cong\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}}(k^{*}\mathcal{F}).

The deformation from
\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}
to its tangent space
T_{t^{\nu}}\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}
can be trivialized using the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action on
\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}\cong\mathfrak{a}
by scaling. Using that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-equivariant, we can identify
\textup{Sp}_{t^{\nu}/\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}}(k^{*}\mathcal{F})
with
k^{*}\mathcal{F}
, viewed as an object in
D_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}}(\mathfrak{a})
. Combining (
6.24
), (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), we have

(6.27)

M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{F})_{\nu}\cong\phi(k^{*}\mathcal{F})=\phi(\mathcal{F}|_{\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}}).

When
\mathcal{F}=\delta_{\nu}
,
\mathcal{F}|_{\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}}
is the skyscraper sheaf at

0
by definition, therefore
M_{\mathbf{G}}(\delta_{\nu})_{\nu}
is one-dimensional.

Now consider the case
\mathcal{F}=\delta_{\lambda}
where
\lambda\neq\nu
. Since
\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}=t^{\nu}\cdot\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}
does not intersect
\Sigma^{\psi}_{\lambda}
, which is the support of
\mathcal{F}
, we see that
\delta_{\lambda}|_{\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}}=0
. We conclude from (
6.27
) that
M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{F})_{\nu}=0
.
∎

6.6.
Microlocal fiber functor for the Satake category

The focus of this subsection is the Satake category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
itself. We give an interpretation of the functor (
6.18
) as a microstalk functor on the affine Grassmannian. The main result in this subsection are not used elsewhere in the paper but we believe it is of independent interest.

Since the affine Grassmannian cannot be written as an increasing union of smooth varieties in general, we will need to embed it into something smooth in order to make sense of microstalks.

6.6.1.
Embedding
Gr
into the thick Grassmannian

Viewing the affine Grassmannian
\textup{Gr}=G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]
as classifying
G
-bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with trivialization on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\backslash\{0\}
, we get an ind-closed embedding into the thick Grassmannian introduced in §
6.5.1

(6.28)

i_{\textup{Gr}}:\textup{Gr}\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}.

Using the coset descriptions,
i_{\textup{Gr}}
can be written as

G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]\cong G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\hookrightarrow G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]

corresponding to the inclusion
G[t,t^{-1}]\subset G(\!(\tau)\!)
. For an object
\mathcal{K}\in D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr})=\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
,
i_{\textup{Gr},*}\mathcal{K}\in D(\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}})
, and it makes sense to talk about microstalks of
i_{\textup{Gr},*}\mathcal{K}
since
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
is pro-smooth.

Recall the subscheme
\Sigma_{\lambda}\subset\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
from §
6.5.1
. A simple calculation of tangent spaces shows:

6.6.2 Lemma
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
,
\Sigma_{\lambda}
is a transversal slice to
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
at
t^{\lambda}
, both viewed as subschemes of the pro-smooth scheme
\ \textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
.

Let
\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda}=\Sigma_{\lambda}\cap\textup{Gr}
and
\sigma_{\lambda}:\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow\textup{Gr}
be the locally closed embedding. The linear character
\psi
on
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}
induces a function

\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda}:\Sigma_{\lambda}\cong\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/A_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathbb{G}_{a},

and restricts to a function

\psi_{\lambda}:\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda}\subset\Sigma_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda}}\mathbb{G}_{a}.

The function
\psi_{\lambda}
is
T
-invariant with respect to the left translation action on
\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda}
.

Recall the functors
\rho_{\lambda}
and its equivariant version
{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}
defined in §
6.4.1
and §
6.4.2
.

6.6.3 Lemma
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of functors

(6.29)

{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}\langle{2d_{\lambda}}\rangle\cong\phi(\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}(-)):\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D(\textup{pt}/T)

Here
\phi(-)
denotes the monodromic vanishing cycles functor on
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
.

In particular, there is a canonical isomorphism for the non-equivariant version

\rho_{\lambda}\langle{2d_{\lambda}}\rangle\cong\phi(\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}(-)):\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D^{b}(\textup{Vect})

Proof.

We use (
6.21
) to compute
{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})
for
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
.

We first compute
{{}^{T}}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K}
. By definition,
{}^{T}\delta_{0}
is the extension by zero from the base point of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. We restrict the diagram (
6.15
) defining the spherical Hecke action to the locus where the first factor
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)
is replaced by
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}G[t]
, the support of
{}^{T}\delta_{0}
:

(6.30)

\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}G[t]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G[t]}}{{\times}}G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{2}}
\textstyle{G[\![t]\!]\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]}

Now using the isomorphism
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}G[t]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G[t]}}{{\times}}G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\cong G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\cong\textup{Gr}
, the map
m
becomes the composition of the embedding
i_{\textup{Gr}}
followed by the quotient map
\pi:\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
:

\textup{Gr}=G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]\xrightarrow{i_{\textup{Gr}}}G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]=\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/G[t]=\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}).

The map
\textup{pr}_{2}
in (
6.30
) becomes the projection
\textup{pr}:G[t,t^{-1}]/G[t]=\textup{Gr}\to G[\![t]\!]\backslash\textup{Gr}
. Therefore

(6.31)

{}^{T}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K}\cong(\pi\circ i_{\textup{Gr}})_{!}\mathcal{K}.

Here we view
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
as a
T
-equivariant complex of sheaves on
Gr
.

To compute
{}^{T}\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})=\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}({}^{T}\delta_{\lambda},{}^{T}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K})
, we need to restrict
{}^{T}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K}
via the functor

{}^{T}b^{*}_{\lambda}:{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T))\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}}\Kir(\textup{pt}/(A_{\lambda}T)\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\textup{pr}^{*}}\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})

Consider the following diagram where all squares are Cartesian

(6.32)

\textstyle{\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{i_{\Sigma_{\lambda}}}
Gr
\scriptstyle{i_{\textup{Gr}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\psi}_{\lambda}=\textup{pr}\circ\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
pr
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{a}\times(\textup{pt}/A_{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\beta}_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})}

By proper base change

{}^{T}\beta^{*}_{\lambda}(\pi\circ i_{\textup{Gr}})_{!}(\mathcal{K})\cong\textup{pr}^{*}\overline{\psi}_{\lambda!}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\lambda}^{*}i_{\textup{Gr}!}\mathcal{K}\cong\textup{pr}^{*}\textup{pr}_{!}\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{K}\in\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

Since
pr
is the quotient map by the trivial action of the unipotent group
A_{\lambda}
, we have
\textup{pr}^{*}\textup{pr}_{!}\cong\textup{id}\otimes\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({A_{\lambda}})=\textup{id}\langle{-2d_{\lambda}}\rangle
. Combining with (
6.31
) we get

{}^{T}\beta^{*}_{\lambda}({}^{T}\delta_{0}\star_{0}\mathcal{K})\cong{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}(\pi\circ i_{\textup{Gr}})_{!}(\mathcal{K})\cong\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{K}\langle{-2d_{\lambda}}\rangle\in\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a}).

Finally, we apply
\phi
extracts the multiplicity of
\delta\in\Kir(\textup{pt}/T\times\mathbb{G}_{a})
in the above expression to get the desired formula (
6.29
).
∎

6.6.4.
Renormalized microstalks

The cotangent bundle
T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
classifies triples
(\mathcal{E},\sigma,\varphi)
where
(\mathcal{E},\sigma)\in\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
and
\varphi\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{1},\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})
. Expanding
\varphi
at
\infty
using the trivialization
\sigma
of
\mathcal{E}
near
\infty
, we get a canonical map

T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\to\mathfrak{g}(\!(\tau)\!)d\tau

which is the moment map for the
G(\!(\tau)\!)
-action on
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
.

The union of conormals

\Lambda_{\textup{Gr}}:=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)/W}T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\subset T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}

can be described as follows. A triple
(\mathcal{E},\sigma,\varphi)\in T^{*}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
lies in
\Lambda_{\textup{Gr}}
if and only if
\sigma
extends to an isomorphism
\widetilde{\sigma}:\mathcal{E}|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\backslash\{0\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{triv}}|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\backslash\{0\}}

(which means
(\mathcal{E},\tau)\in\textup{Gr}
), and that
\varphi
, viewed as a section of
\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{triv}})\otimes\omega|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\backslash\{0\}}
via the isomorphism
\widetilde{\sigma}
, extends to a section of
\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{triv}})\otimes\omega|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
.

In particular, for the point
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
, the Higgs field
\psi=\psi_{1}dt
gives a point
(t^{\lambda},\psi)\in\Lambda_{\textup{Gr}}
, which lies in the conormal fiber of
T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
at
t^{\lambda}
.

We would like to apply the microlocalization functor (see §
A.4
) for the subvartiety
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
of
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
. Since
\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
is infinite-dimensional, we need to renormalize the shift as follows.

Let
\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}
be the moduli stack of
G
-bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with a trivialization on the
(N-1)
-th infinitesimal neighborhood of
\infty
(so that
\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}=\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})
). Let
\pi_{N}:\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\to\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}
be the projection. Each
\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}
contains a maximal schematic (open) locus which we denote by
X_{N}
. Note that

(6.33)

\bigcup_{N\geq 1}\pi_{N}^{-1}(X_{N})=\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}.

Let
i_{\textup{Gr},N}:\textup{Gr}\xrightarrow{i_{\textup{Gr}}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{N}}\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}
.

Fix
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. For each
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
, its support is contained in
\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
for some dominant coweight
\nu
. We enlarge if necessarily such that
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
. For sufficiently large
N
(depending on
\nu
hence on
\mathcal{K}
),
i_{\textup{Gr}}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\subset\pi_{N}^{-1}(X_{N})
by (
6.33
), hence
i_{\textup{Gr},N}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\subset X_{N}
. Moreover for sufficiently large
N
we can arrange that
i_{\textup{Gr},N}|_{\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}}:\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}\to X_{N}
is an embedding.

We may then apply the microlocalization functor to the sheaf
\mathcal{K}_{N}=i_{\textup{Gr},\mathcal{K},!}\mathcal{K}\in D(X_{N})
on
X_{N}
with respect to its subscheme
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
(embedded using
i_{\textup{Gr},N}
):

\mu_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}:D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\to D(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}).

We will use a shifted version of microlocalization: it amounts to removing the shift in the Fourier transform step. In particular we consider

\displaystyle\mu^{\natural}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}:=\mu_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}\circ i_{\textup{Gr},N,!}[-\textup{codim}_{X_{N}}\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}]:D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\to D(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}).

The conormal bundles
T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}
form an increasing union of vector bundles over
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
as
N
increases, whose union is
T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
. Concretely, the conormal fiber of
T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}
at
(\mathcal{E},\tau:\mathcal{E}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\cong G\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\in\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
is
\textup{H}^{0}({\mathbb{P}^{1},\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(N\infty)})
. For
N<N^{\prime}
let

(6.34)

\iota_{N,N^{\prime}}:T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}\hookrightarrow T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N^{\prime}}.

be the natural embeddings of conormal bundles over
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
.

6.6.5 Lemma
.

Fix a dominant coweight
\nu
such that
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
. The functors
\{\mu^{\natural}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}\}
are compatible with pullbacks along the embeddings of the conormal bundles in (
6.34
), i.e., there are canonical isomorphisms of functors for
N<N^{\prime}

\iota_{N,N^{\prime}}^{*}\circ\mu^{\natural}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N^{\prime}}}\cong\mu^{\natural}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}:D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\to D(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N})

(whenever
N
are large enough so that
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
embeds to
X_{N}
), compatible with pullbacks in multiple steps.

Proof.

Let
X=X_{N}
. Although
X_{N^{\prime}}
does not necessarily map to
X_{N}
, let
Y\subset X_{N^{\prime}}
be the preimage of
X_{N}
. Let
\pi:Y\to X
be the projection, which is a torsor for an affine algebraic group, hence is smooth.

Let
S=\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
with an embedding
i_{Y}:S\hookrightarrow Y
(given by
i_{\textup{Gr},N^{\prime}}
) such that
i_{X}=\pi\circ i_{Y}:S\to X
is also an embedding. Let
Z=\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
be embedded into
Y
and
X
by the restrictions of
i_{Y}
and
i_{X}
.
Let
\iota:T^{*}_{Z}X\hookrightarrow T^{*}_{Z}Y
be the map induced by
d\pi
.

The lemma asks for a canonical isomorphism of functors

(6.35)

\mu_{Z/X}i_{X,!}[-\textup{codim}_{X}Z]\cong\iota^{*}\circ\mu_{Z/Y}i_{Y,!}[-\textup{codim}_{Y}Z]:D(S)\to D(T^{*}_{Z}X).

Let
\tau:N_{Z}Y\to N_{Z}X
be the dual of
\iota
. We first claim that the specializations to the normal bundles satisfy

(6.36)

\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}\circ i_{X,!}\cong\tau_{!}\circ\textup{Sp}_{Z/Y}\circ i_{Y,!}:D(S)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}X).

Indeed, let
M_{Z}S
be the total space of the deformation to the normal cone of
Z
in
S
. Then we have embeddings
k_{Y}:M_{Z}S\hookrightarrow M_{Z}Y
and
k_{X}:M_{Z}S\hookrightarrow M_{Z}X
compatible with the natural map
M_{Z}Y\to M_{Z}X
. Now both sides of (
6.36
) can be identified with
k_{X,0,!}\textup{Sp}_{Z/S}
, where
k_{X,0}:N_{Z}S\to N_{Z}X
is the special fiber of
k_{X}
.

The isomorphism (
6.35
) then follows by applying the monodromic Fourier transform to both sides of (
6.36
) and applying Lemma
A.3.5
to the pair of maps
(\tau,\iota=\tau^{\vee})
between normal and conormal bundles.
∎

By Lemma
6.6.5
, for fixed
\nu
, the functors
\{\mu^{\natural}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}\}
together with a functor

\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}:D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\to\varprojlim_{N}D(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}):=D^{*}(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}).

As we enlarge
\nu
to
\nu^{\prime}
, the functor
\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}
above is compatible with the embeddings
D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})\hookrightarrow D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu^{\prime}})
. Passing to the colimit for
\nu
, we get the renormalized microlocalization functor for the Satake category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
along
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
, still denoted by the same notation

\displaystyle\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D^{*}(T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}).

In particular, it makes sense to evaluate the
*
-stalk of
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})
(
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
) at any point of
\xi\in T^{*}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}\textup{Bun}_{G,\widehat{\infty}}
, and call it the renormalized microstalk of
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
at
\xi
. We are interested in the case
\xi=(t^{\lambda},\psi)
.

6.6.6 Proposition
.

For
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
, there is a canonical isomorphism functorial in
\mathcal{K}

\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})\cong\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\langle{-2d_{\lambda}}\rangle.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
6.6.3
, it remains to give a canonical isomorphism

(6.37)

\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\cong\phi(\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{K})

for
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}
. Below we shall freely use notations from the proof of Lemma
6.6.3
.

Suppose
\textup{Supp}(\mathcal{K})\subset\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
and
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
. By definition of the renormalized microlocalization functor in §
6.6.4
, we have

(6.38)

\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}=\mu_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}(i_{\textup{Gr},N!}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}[-\textup{codim}_{X_{N}}\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}]

for sufficiently large
N
.

Let
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
be the image of
\Sigma_{\lambda}
in
\textup{Bun}_{G,N\infty}
. We have
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}\cong\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{N}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/A_{\lambda}
(where
\mathbf{G}^{N}_{\infty}
is the
N
th congruence subgroup of
G[\![\tau]\!]
). Hence for
N
sufficiently large
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
is a scheme therefore
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}\subset X_{N}
. For
N\geq 2
, the map
\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda}:\Sigma_{\lambda}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}
factors through a map
\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda,N}:\Sigma_{\lambda,N}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}
. We thus have an analog of the diagram (
6.32
), of which we only record a part

(6.39)

\textstyle{\Sigma^{\textup{Gr}}_{\lambda,N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\lambda,N}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\lambda,N}}
\scriptstyle{i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}}
\textstyle{\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\textup{Gr},N}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\lambda,N}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda,N}}
\textstyle{X_{N}}

Since
\mathcal{K}
is supported on
\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
, we have

(6.40)

\phi(\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{K})\cong\phi(\psi_{\lambda,N,!}\sigma_{\lambda,N}^{*}\mathcal{K}).

We then apply Proposition
A.4.3
to the smooth scheme
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
equipped with the inverse of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action. The inverse of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action contracts
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
to
t^{\lambda}
, and the function
\psi_{\lambda,N}:\Sigma_{\lambda,N}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
has weight
1
under the inverse of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action, which is the smallest among the weights on
T^{*}_{t^{\lambda}}\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
. Proposition
A.4.3
is thus applicable, and it gives a canonical isomorphism for
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}\textup{-mon}}(\Sigma_{\lambda,N})
.

\mu_{\{t^{\lambda}\}/\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}(\mathcal{F})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\cong\phi(\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda,N,!}\mathcal{F})[\dim\Sigma_{\lambda,N}].

We apply this to
\mathcal{F}=i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K}
to get

\mu_{\{t^{\lambda}\}/\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}(i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\cong\phi(\widetilde{\psi}_{\lambda,N,!}i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K})[\dim\Sigma_{\lambda,N}]\cong\phi(\psi_{\lambda,N,!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K})[\dim\Sigma_{\lambda,N}].

Combined with (
6.40
) we get

(6.41)

\phi(\psi_{\lambda!}\sigma_{\lambda}^{*}\mathcal{K})\cong\mu_{\{t^{\lambda}\}/\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}(i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}[-\dim\Sigma_{\lambda,N}].

By Lemma
6.6.2
,
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
is a transversal slice to
\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}
in
\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
, hence

(6.42)

\dim\Sigma_{\lambda,N}=\textup{codim}_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}}X_{N}.

Using (
6.38
), (
6.41
) and the identity (
6.42
), to give (
6.37
), it reduces to give a canonical isomorphism

(6.43)

\mu_{\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}/X_{N}}(i_{\textup{Gr},N!}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mu_{\{t^{\lambda}\}/\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}(i_{\Sigma_{\lambda,N}!}\sigma^{*}_{\lambda,N}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\cong\mu_{\{t^{\lambda}\}/\Sigma_{\lambda,N}}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\lambda,N}^{*}i_{\textup{Gr},N!}\mathcal{K})|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}

for any
\mathcal{K}\in D_{G[\![t]\!]}(\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu})
. This follows by applying Lemma
A.4.1
to
X=X_{N}
,
Y=\textup{Gr}_{\leq\nu}
with the action of a finite-dimensional quotient
H
of
G[\![t]\!]
by left translation, the
H
-orbit
Z=\textup{Gr}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow Y
through
z=t^{\lambda}
, with transversal slice
S=\Sigma_{\lambda,N}
.
∎

Combining Proposition
6.6.6
and Theorem
6.4.3
, we conclude that the microstalks functor on the Satake category gives a fiber functor. More precisely,

6.6.7 Corollary
.

The functor

\omega_{\psi}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}\mu^{\natural}_{\lambda}|_{(t^{\lambda},\psi)}\langle{-2d_{\lambda}}\rangle:\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph}}_{0}\to D^{b}(\textup{Vect})

has a canonical monoidal structure and is t-exact. Moreover, its restriction to
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
can be equipped with the structure of a fiber functor for the Tannakian category
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
.

7.
The category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}

We introduce and study the main player of this paper, a conventional sheaf category on the affine flag variety that turns out to be equivalent to microsheaves on the affine Springer fiber
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
with finite type supports.

7.1.
Definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}

Consider the stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
with the canonical
Aff
-action. Define

\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}:=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})).

By Proposition
2.2.1
, when
\textup{char}(k)=p
, we may identify
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
with the category of
(\mathbb{G}_{a},\psi)
-equivariant complexes on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
, or equivalently
(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
-equivariant complexes on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{2}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
.

7.1.1.
Relevant points for
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}

By Definition
2.4.1
, the relevant locus for the Kirillov category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
is the image of the forgetful map
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
.

To describe the relevant locus of
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
, consider the map

(7.1)

\widetilde{\beta}_{\lambda}:Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{I}_{0}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})=G[\tau]^{1}\backslash\textup{Fl}.

We identify the source of the above map as a closed variety of
Fl
, which is a fixed point component under
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
. Recall the Hessenberg variety
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)\subset Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{I}_{0}
introduced in §
4.1.3
.

7.1.2 Lemma
.

The relevant locus for
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
is the union of
\widetilde{\beta}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda))
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

Using the uniformization

G[\tau]^{1}\backslash\textup{Fl}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0}),

the relevant
k
-points for
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
are the image of those
g\mathbf{I}_{0}\in\textup{Fl}
such that the composition

(7.2)

G[\tau]^{1}\cap\textup{Ad}(g)\mathbf{I}_{0}\subset G[\tau]^{1}\twoheadrightarrow t^{-1}\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{t}\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\langle{\psi_{1},-}\rangle}k

is zero. Up to left translation by
G[\tau]^{1}
we may assume
g\mathbf{I}_{0}\in Gt^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}
. Then a root-theoretic calculation shows that (
7.2
) is trivial if and only if
g\mathbf{I}_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
. The lemma follows.

∎

7.1.3.
The category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}

For any standard parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}\subset G(\!(t)\!)
we may consider
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi},\mathbf{P}_{0})
and the corresponding Kirillov category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
. In particular, for
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{G}=G[\![t]\!]
we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
defined in §
6.1
.

The projection
\pi_{\mathbf{P}}:\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi},\mathbf{I}_{0})\to\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{\psi},\mathbf{P}_{0})
induces functors

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathbf{P}*}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{P}}}

For
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{G}
, we denote
\pi_{\mathbf{G}}
by
\pi
.

We give
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}
the perverse t-structure inherited from the middle perversity on the stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{0})
. Denote its heart by
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\heartsuit}
.

7.1.4.
Affine Hecke action

Let
\mathcal{H}_{0}=D(\mathbf{I}_{0}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}_{0})
be the affine Hecke category of
G
. Again all objects in
\mathcal{H}_{0}
are automatically
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-monodromic with unipotent monodromy, since all
\mathbf{I}_{0}
-double cosets in
G(\!(t)\!)
are stable under loop rotation. Similar to the spherical action discussed in §
6.4
, there is a right action of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
by Hecke modification at
0
, using the construction of integral transforms in §
2.3.9
. For
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we write its action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
by
(-)\star_{0}\mathcal{K}
. It will be convenient to use the double coset description (uniformization at
0
)

\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})\cong G[\tau]^{\psi}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}_{0}=G[\tau]^{\psi}\backslash\textup{Fl}

when working with the action of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Here
G[\tau]^{\psi}=G[\tau]\cap\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\subset G[\tau]
.
We also introduce the notation

G[t^{-1}]^{1}=G[\tau]^{1}:=\ker(\textup{ev}_{\infty}:G[\tau]\to G).

For
w\in\widetilde{W}
, let
\textup{IC}_{w}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
be the middle extension of the constant sheaf
E\langle{\ell(w)}\rangle
from the Schubert cell indexed by
w
.

7.1.5 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
be the full subcategory generated under the
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-action by the image of
\pi^{*}:\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
.

Recall the objects
\delta_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Let

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
is generated by
\{\delta_{\lambda}\}
,
\{\mathcal{F}_{\lambda};\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\}
generate
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
as a
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-module.

7.1.6 Remark
.

Note that by Theorem
6.4.3
, the action of central sheaves in
\mathcal{H}_{0}
on
\mathcal{F}_{0}
generates all
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
after idempotent completion. Thus
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is generated under the
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-action by only the object
\mathcal{F}_{0}
after idempotent completion.

7.1.7.
The category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}

For general
\mathbf{P}
, let
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
such that
\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
. Equivalently,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
is generated by the summands of the image of
\pi_{\mathbf{P}*}:\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
. When
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{G}
, this is consistent with the earlier definition of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
.

7.1.8.
Hecke action at
\infty

As in the case of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
, the monoidal category
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
by modifications of bundles at
\infty
. We denote the action of
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
by
\mathcal{K}\star_{\infty}(-)
. This action commutes with the action of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
, and therefore the
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
-action preserves the subcategory
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
. It will be convenient to use the double coset description

\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})\cong\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash G(\!(\tau)\!)/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}

when working with the action of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
. Here
I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\subset G[t]
is the preimage of
B
under the evaluation map
\textup{ev}_{t=0}:G[t]\to G
.

By Lemma
6.2.1
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\lambda-\mu},\quad\forall\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T).

In particular, the action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
extends to an action of the completion
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
.

7.1.9.
T
-equivariant versions

We can similarly consider the stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
with the action of
Aff
. We then can define

{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}:=\Kir(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})).

We have adjoint functors

\textstyle{{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{T}\pi}
\textstyle{{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{T}\pi^{*}}

The category
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
still carries an action of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
of the Hecke category at
0
, and an action of
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
at infinity (see §
5.1.3
).

We give
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
the perverse t-structure inherited from the forgetful functor to
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
, which in turn comes from the middle perversity on the stack
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. We denote its heart by
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi}
.

By Proposition
5.4.9
, we have a monoidal equivalence of abelian categories
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\cong({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
, under which the character
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}^{*}(T^{\vee})=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
corresponds to the standard sheaf
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\in({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
.

7.1.10 Lemma
.

For any
\mathcal{K}\in({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
, the endofunctor
\mathcal{K}\star_{\infty}(-):{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\to{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
is t-exact for the perverse t-structure on
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
.

Proof.

We first show that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, the endofunctor
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\star_{\infty}(-)
of
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
is t-left-exact. Indeed,
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\star_{\infty}(-)\cong p_{\lambda!}[d_{\lambda}]
, where
p
is the following map induced by multiplication

p_{\lambda}:(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T\times^{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T}\textup{Fl}\cong((\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T))\backslash\textup{Fl}\to(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\textup{Fl}.

Since
p_{\lambda}
is affine with relative dimension
d_{\lambda}
by (
5.9
), we conclude that
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\star_{\infty}(-)
is t-left-exact.

Now
{}^{T}\Delta_{-\lambda}^{T}\star{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\cong{}^{T}\Delta_{0}^{T}
by Proposition
5.4.9
, which acts by identity on
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
. This forces
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\star_{\infty}(-)
to be t-exact. Finally, since
\{{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
generate the abelian category
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
by Lemma
5.2.1
,
\mathcal{K}\star_{\infty}(-)
is t-exact for any
\mathcal{K}\in({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
.

∎

7.2.
The category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
and action by Wakimoto sheaves

Consider the embedding

{}^{T}\overline{\beta}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda}:\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0}):=(G[\tau]^{\psi}T)\backslash G[t]^{1}t^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{I}_{0}=(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0}).

analogous to the embedding in
6.2
. Note that here these are not all the relevant strata in
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Similar to the calculations in §
6.1.1
, we have

\textup{Bun}^{\lambda}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})=(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\backslash\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\cong(\textup{pt}/\widetilde{A}_{\lambda})\times\mathbb{G}_{a}

where
\widetilde{A}_{\lambda}=(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. The embedding
{}^{T}\overline{\beta}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda}
gives a functor

\displaystyle{}^{T}\beta^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda,!}:\Kir(\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}^{*}}\Kir((\textup{pt}/\widetilde{A}_{\lambda})\times\mathbb{G}_{a})\xrightarrow{{}^{T}\overline{\beta}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda,!}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}

Let

\mathfrak{d}_{0}={}^{T}\beta^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{0,!}\delta,\quad\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}={}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{0},\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T).

By Lemma
7.1.10
, we see that

\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\in{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi},\quad\mbox{for all }\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T).

7.2.1 Definition
.

Let
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}\subset{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi}
be the full subcategory whose objects are finite successive extensions of
\{\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
. Further denote by
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}

the full subcategory of
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
whose objects are finite direct sums of
\{\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
.

7.2.2 Remark
.

The category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
is not a subcategory of
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
. We will mention in §
8.3.3
how this category fits into the coherent description of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

7.2.3 Lemma
.

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
we have a canonical isomorphism

{}^{T}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda})\cong{}^{T}\delta_{\lambda}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}.

Proof.

For
\lambda=0
it follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{d}_{0}
and
\delta_{0}
because the projection
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})\to\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T)
is an isomorphism. Now we apply convolution by
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}
to the isomorphism
\mathfrak{d}_{0}\cong{}^{T}\delta_{0}
and use Lemma
6.12
to conclude for general
\lambda
.
∎

7.2.4 Lemma
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
we have
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\cong{}^{T}\beta^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda,!}\delta\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle
.

Proof.

We show that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
there is a canonical isomorphism

(7.3)

{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}\star_{\infty}{}^{T}\beta^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda,!}\delta\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle\cong{}^{T}\beta_{0!}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\delta=\mathfrak{d}_{0}.

The result then follows by convolving with
{}^{T}\Delta_{-\lambda}^{T}
, which is the inverse of
{}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}^{T}
.

To see (
7.3
), we only need to note that the multiplication map gives an isomorphism

\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T\times^{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}T}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\cong\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}},

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. Indeed, we can identified the left side with
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\times^{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
, so it suffices to show that

(7.4)

\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\times^{\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}

is a point. This has essentially been proved in Lemma
6.2.1
(1). We observe that

\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}/I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})
. For
\lambda
dominant, both
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})I_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
and
\mathbf{K}_{+}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})G[t]
are equal to
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})N[t]
(where
N
is the unipotent radical of
B
). We also have
\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\tau^{\lambda}\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}=\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{-}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
, where
\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{-}=\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}\cap\textup{Ad}(t^{\lambda})\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
. Therefore (
7.4
) can be identified with
\mathbf{K}_{-}^{\psi}\backslash\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}/\mathbf{K}_{+}
. Finally we observe that the multiplication map
\mathbf{K}^{\psi}_{-}\times\mathbf{K}_{+}\to\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty}
is an isomorphism, which proves that (
7.4
) is isomorphic to a point.
∎

7.2.5 Lemma
.

(1)

The category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is a semisimple abelian category with simple objects
\{\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
.

(2)

As a
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
-module category,
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is free of rank one.

Proof.

(1) By definition,
\mathfrak{d}_{0}
is a simple object in
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi}
. Since the action by
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}(-)
is an auto-equivalence of
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi}
for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we see that
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}={}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{0}
is also a simple object in
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\heartsuit}_{\psi}
. This shows that
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is a semisimple abelian category. To show (1), we only need to show that for
\lambda\neq\lambda^{\prime}
,
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
is not isomorphic to
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
. Translate by
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\mu}
for
\mu
sufficiently dominant, we may arrange that
\lambda+\mu
and
\lambda^{\prime}+\mu
are both dominant. In this case, we have
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}\not\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda^{\prime}+\mu}
by Lemma
7.2.4
. Translating back by
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\not\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.

(2) Since
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
is semisimple with simple objects
\{{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
, (2) now follows from (1) and the definition of
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
.
∎

7.2.6 Lemma
.

The functor
{}^{T}\pi_{*}:{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
restricts to an equivalence of
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
-module categories

{}^{T}\pi_{*}|_{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}}:{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}.

Proof.

The functor
{}^{T}\pi_{*}
is clearly equivariant under
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
. By Lemma
7.2.3
,
{}^{T}\pi_{*}
sends simple objects to simple objects. By Lemma
7.2.5
and Corollary
6.3.5
(4),
{}^{T}\pi_{*}
induces a bijection between isomorphisms classes of simple objects (both are indexed by
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
). The statement follows.
∎

7.2.7 Lemma
.

(1)

Each
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
has a unique filtration
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
(by subobjects in
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
) indexed by the poset
(\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)),\leq)
, such that the associated graded pieces
\textup{gr}_{\lambda}\mathcal{G}=F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}/F_{<\lambda}\mathcal{G}
is a direct sum of copies of
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
.

(2)

All morphisms in
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
preserve the filtration above. Passing to the associated graded gives a functor

\textup{gr}:{}^{T}\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}.

(3)

For any
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, we have

F_{\leq\lambda}(^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G})=^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}F_{\leq\lambda+\mu}\mathcal{G}

as subobjects of
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

Existence part of (1). We call a filtration
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
indexed by the poset
(\mathbb{X}_{*}(T),\leq)

admissible
if it satisfies the condition specified in the lemma. We first remark that
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
is a filtration of
\mathcal{G}
satisfying the required condition, then for any
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
,
F_{\leq\lambda}({}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}):={}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}F_{\leq\lambda+\mu}\mathcal{G}
is an admissible filtration for
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}
.

Now we construct an admissible filtration on any
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
. By the definition of
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, each object
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
has a finite filtration with associated graded in
\{\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\}
. We first consider the case where all the associated graded of
\mathcal{G}
are
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. We will define a filtration
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
indexed by
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
by truncation of the support as follows.

We have a stratification
\textup{Bun}_{G}=\cup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}}\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}
where
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}
has only one point corresponding to the double coset
\mathbf{G}_{\infty}t^{\lambda}G[t]
. Note that for
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
,
\textup{Bun}^{[\lambda^{\prime}]}_{G}
is in the closure of
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}
if and only if
\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}
. In particular,
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\leq[\lambda]}:=\cup_{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda}\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda^{\prime}]}
is open in
\textup{Bun}_{G}
. Let
j^{\leq[\lambda]}:\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\leq[\lambda]}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
be the preimage of
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\leq[\lambda]}
. Let
j^{[\lambda]}:\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
be the preimage of
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}
. Define
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}=j^{\leq[\lambda]}_{!}j^{\leq[\lambda],*}\mathcal{G}
. The associated graded
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}/F_{<\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
is then isomorphic to
j^{[\lambda]}_{!}j^{[\lambda]*}\mathcal{G}
, which is isomorphic to
{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda,!}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}!}{}^{T}\beta_{\lambda}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}*}\mathcal{G}
because
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\lambda}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
is the only relevant stratum in
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{[\lambda]}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
in the support of
\mathcal{G}
. By Lemma
7.2.4
,
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\cong{}^{T}\beta^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}_{\lambda,!}\delta\langle{-d_{\lambda}}\rangle
, therefore
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}/F_{<\lambda}\mathcal{G}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
. This gives an admissible filtration on
\mathcal{G}
.

For general
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, by convolving with some
{}^{T}\Delta_{-\mu}^{T}
where
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
is dominant enough, we can arrange that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}={}^{T}\Delta_{-\mu}^{T}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}
is a successive extension of
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. Let
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the filtration constructed in the previous paragraph. Then
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}:={}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}^{T}\star_{\infty}F_{\leq\lambda+\mu}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is an admissible filtration on
\mathcal{G}
, by the remark in the first paragraph.

(2) We need to show that if
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
are equipped with admissible filtrations
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{2}
, and
\varphi:\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a morphism in
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, then induces a canonical map
\varphi_{\leq\lambda}:F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1}\to F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{2}
compatible with
\varphi
and compatible with the filtrations in the obvious sense.

By convolving both
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
with
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\mu}
for sufficiently dominant
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
, we may assume that
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}
are both successive extensions of
\{\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}}
. Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. By Lemma
7.2.4
,
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1}
is the
!
-extension from the open substack
\textup{Bun}^{\leq[\lambda]}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
, and
\mathrm{Cone}(F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{2})
is supported on the closed complement of
\textup{Bun}^{\leq[\lambda]}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}T,\mathbf{I}_{0})
. Therefore
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}(F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathrm{Cone}(F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}))=0
. This implies that
\varphi|_{F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1}}
has a canonical factorization as
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{\leq\lambda}}F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

The uniqueness of the admissible filtration on
\mathcal{G}
in (1) follows by applying the functoriality in (2) to the identity map of
\mathcal{G}
.

It remains to show (3). Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}={}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}
. Define another filtration
F^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, also indexed by
(\mathbb{X}_{*}(T),\leq)
, by

F^{\prime}_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}:={}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}\star_{\infty}F_{\lambda+\mu}\mathcal{G}.

Note that the right side is a subobject of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}={}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{G}
because convolution with
{}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}\star_{\infty}
is t-exact. The associated graded of the filtration
F^{\prime}
are
\textup{gr}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cong{}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\textup{gr}_{\lambda+\mu}\mathcal{G}
which is isomorphic to copies of
{}^{T}\Delta_{\mu}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
. Therefore
F^{\prime}
is also an admissible filtration on
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. By the uniqueness of the admissible filtration, we have
F^{\prime}_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, which proves (3).
∎

7.2.8.
Wakimoto sheaves

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, denote by
J_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
the Wakimoto sheaf defined in
[
1
, Corollary 1]
. Recall that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
, the dominant cone,
J_{-\lambda}\cong j_{-\lambda!}E\langle{\ell(\lambda)}\rangle
, where
j_{-\lambda}:\mathbf{I}t^{-\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}\hookrightarrow\textup{Fl}
is the embedding.

Denote by
\mathrm{Wak}\subset\mathcal{H}_{0}
the full subcategory whose objects are finite successive extensions of
\{J_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
. Further denote by
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
consisting of finite direct sums of
\{J_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)}
. By
[
1
, Corollary 1]
, both
\mathrm{Wak}
and
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
are monoidal subcategories of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Moreover, we have a canonical monoidal equivalence

(7.5)

\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}

sending the one-dimensional representation
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}^{*}(T^{\vee})=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
to
J_{\lambda}
.

Consider the partial order of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
given by
\lambda\leq\mu
if
\mu-\lambda
a sum of positive coroots. By
[
1
, 3.6.5]
, each object
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Wak}
has a
canonical
filtration
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{F}
such that
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{F}/F_{<\lambda}\mathcal{F}
is a direct sum of copies of
J_{\lambda}
. Moreover, by
[
1
, Proposition 6(a)]
, the associated graded functor

\textup{gr}:\mathrm{Wak}\rightarrow\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\cong\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})

is a monoidal functor.

7.2.9 Lemma
.

Let
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
be the set of coweights
\lambda
such that
\langle{\lambda,\alpha}\rangle\geq 1
for each positive root
\alpha
. Then for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
, we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}\star_{0}J_{-\mu}
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the multiplication map gives an isomorphism

(7.6)

G[\tau]^{\psi}t^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\times^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathbf{I}_{0}t^{-\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{I}_{0}\cong G[\tau]^{\psi}t^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{I}_{0}

for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. Below we use abbreviated notation: for a subgroup
\mathbf{J}\subset G(\!(t)\!)
and
\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we write

{}^{\nu}\mathbf{J}:=\textup{Ad}(t^{\nu})\mathbf{J}.

To prove (
7.6
), observe that its left side is
G[\tau]^{\psi}
-equivariantly isomorphic to

(7.7)

G[\tau]^{\psi}\times^{G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}}({}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0})/({}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}).

To show (
7.6
), we only need to two things

(a)

(
7.7
) is homogeneous under
G[\tau]^{\psi}
;

(b)

the stabilizer of the identity coset in
{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}/({}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0})
in (
7.7
) is
G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}
.

Now (a) holds if and only if

{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}=(G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0})({}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0})

Conjugating by
t^{-\lambda-\mu}
the above becomes

(7.8)

\mathbf{I}_{0}=({}^{-\lambda-\mu}G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap\mathbf{I}_{0})(\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{-\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}).

Let
x
be the barycenter of the fundamental alcove of the building of
G(\!(t)\!)
corresponding to
\mathbf{I}_{0}
. Comparing affine roots of both sides of (
7.8
), we see it is equivalent to that for any real affine roots
\alpha
such that
\alpha(x)>0
, we have either
\alpha(x+\mu)>0
or
\alpha(\lambda+\mu)<0
. In other words, to show (a), we only need to show that

(7.9)

No real affine root
\alpha
satisfies
\alpha(x)>0,\alpha(x+\mu)<0
and
\alpha(\lambda+\mu)\geq 0
.

On the other hand, (b) is equivalent to

G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{\lambda+\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}=G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{0}

or equivalently

{}^{-\lambda-\mu}G[\tau]^{\psi}\cap{}^{-\mu}\mathbf{I}_{0}\subset\mathbf{I}_{0}.

Comparing affine roots on both sides, it is further equivalent to

(7.10)

No real affine root
\alpha
satisfies
\alpha(x)<0,\alpha(x+\mu)>0
and
\alpha(\lambda+\mu)<0
.

It remains to show (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) for
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
. The two statements are clearly equivalent, so we only need to show (
7.9
). Write
\alpha=\overline{\alpha}+n
as an affine function on the standard apartment, where
\overline{\alpha}
is a finite root. The stated properties of
\alpha
imply that

(7.11)

\langle{\mu,\overline{\alpha}}\rangle<0,\mbox{ and }\langle{\lambda-x,\overline{\alpha}}\rangle>0.

Since
\mu
is dominant, we see
\overline{\alpha}<0
. Since
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
,
\lambda-x
still lies in the interior of the dominant cone, hence
\langle{\lambda-x,\overline{\alpha}}\rangle<0
, which contradicts the second inequality in (
7.11
). This proves (
7.9
), which implies (a) and (b).
∎

7.2.10 Proposition
.

(1)

The monoidal category
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
acts on the category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
.

(2)

There are canonical isomorphisms

c_{\lambda,\mu}:\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}

for all
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
satisfying the following compatibilities

(a)

For
\lambda,\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
,
c_{\lambda,\mu+\mu^{\prime}}
is the composition

\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu+\mu^{\prime}}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\star_{0}J_{\mu^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{c_{\lambda,\mu}\star\textup{id}(J_{\mu^{\prime}})}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}\star_{0}J_{\mu^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{c_{\lambda+\mu,\mu^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu+\mu^{\prime}}.

(b)

For
\lambda,\mu,\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
,
c_{\nu+\lambda,\mu}
is equal to the composition

\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}={{}^{T}}\Delta^{T}_{-\nu}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\xrightarrow{\textup{id}({{}^{T}}\Delta^{T}_{-\nu})\star_{\infty}c_{\lambda,\mu}}{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\nu}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}=\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda+\mu}.

(3)

The
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
-module category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is free of rank one.

Proof.

(1) will follow from the existence of the isomorphisms
c_{\lambda,\mu}
in (2).

(2) For
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
(anti-dominant) and
\lambda+\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
, we let
c_{\lambda,\mu}:\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}
be the isomorphism constructed in Lemma
7.2.9
. The compatibility in (a) holds whenever
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
and
\lambda+\mu+\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
. Indeed, the equation to be checked holds up to a nonzero scalar in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
, and it is enough to check it for the maps between stalks at
t^{\lambda+\mu+\mu^{\prime}}
, for which the equality follows from the isomorphism (
7.6
) that gives the isomorphisms in Lemma
7.2.9
. Similarly, (b) holds whenever
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
,
\lambda+\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
, as can be checked on the stalks at
t^{\nu+\lambda+\mu}
.

Now we define
c_{\lambda,\mu}
for
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
and general
\lambda
. Choose
\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
such that
\nu+\lambda+\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{++}
. Now
c_{\nu+\lambda,\mu}:\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda+\mu}
is already defined. Acting on both sides by
{}^{T}\Delta_{\nu}^{T}
we denote the resulting isomorphism
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}={}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\nu}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\cong{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\nu}\star_{\infty}\mathfrak{d}_{\nu+\lambda+\mu}=\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}
by
c_{\lambda,\mu}
. The compatibility (b) for
c_{\lambda,\mu}
in the previous paragraph shows that
c_{\lambda,\mu}
thus defined is independent of the choice of
\nu
. Moreover, the
c_{\lambda,\mu}
defined so far satisfies the compatibility (a) whenever the terms are defined.

Next we define
c_{\lambda,\mu}
for general
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Choose
\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
such that
\mu+\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{-}
. Now
c_{\lambda,\mu+\mu^{\prime}}
and
c_{\lambda+\mu,\mu^{\prime}}
are both defined in the previous paragraph, and they induce an isomorphism

\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\star_{0}J_{\mu^{\prime}}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu+\mu^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{c_{\lambda,\mu+\mu^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu+\mu^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{c_{\lambda+\mu,\mu^{\prime}}^{-1}}\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}\star_{0}J_{\mu^{\prime}}.

Applying
\star_{0}J_{-\mu^{\prime}}
to both sides gives the desired isomorphism
c_{\lambda,\mu}
. The partial compatibility (a) shows that
c_{\lambda,\mu}
is independent of the choice of
\mu^{\prime}
. Finally one can show that the fully defined
\{c_{\lambda,\mu}\}
satisfy the compatibilities (a) and (b), by reducing to the partial compatibilities (a) and (b) we checked in the beginning.

(3) The functor
\mathfrak{d}_{0}\star_{0}(-):\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
sends simple objects
J_{\lambda}
of
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
to simple objects
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
. Since both
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
and
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
are semisimple, this functor is an equivalence, proving that
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is free of rank one as a
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
-module category.
∎

7.2.11 Corollary
.

(1)

The actions of
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
and
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
induce equivalences of monoidal categories

a_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}:\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}).

(2)

Using the free generator
\mathfrak{d}_{0}
to identify
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
with
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
as a left
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
-module, we have a monoidal equivalence
\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})\cong({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
. Under the equivalences (
7.5
) and (
5.28
), there is a canonical isomorphism of monoidal functors making the following diagram commutative

\textstyle{\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}}
\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
inv
\textstyle{\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})}

Here
inv
is the auto-equivalence of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
induced from the inversion on
T^{\vee}
, i.e., the functor that sends
E(\mu)
to
E(-\mu)
.

Proof.

(1) The category
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
carries commuting left action by
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
and right action by
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
, and it is free of rank one over both by Lemma
7.2.5
and Proposition
7.2.10
.

(2) It suffices to give canonical isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mu}:a^{ss}_{\mathrm{Wak}}(J_{\mu})\cong{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{-\mu}
for all
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
compatible with convolutions. By definition, such an isomorphism
\gamma_{\mu}
for an individual
\mu
amounts to the same thing as a collection of isomorphisms
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda+\mu}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
compatible with the left action by
{}^{T}\Delta^{T}_{\nu}
for any
\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Such a collection is given by
c_{\lambda,\mu}
, and the required compatibility boils down to Proposition
7.2.10
(2)(a). This defines
\gamma_{\mu}
for each
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. The fact that these
\gamma_{\mu}
are compatible with convolutions follows from Proposition
7.2.10
(2)(b).

∎

Let
\textup{End}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
be the category of triples
(\Phi,\Phi_{\textup{gr}},\alpha)
where
\Phi:{}^{T}\mathcal{C}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
is an endo-functor of
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
,
\Phi_{\textup{gr}}:{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}\to{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
is an endo-functor of
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
, and
\alpha:\textup{gr}\circ\Phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\Rightarrow}}\Phi_{\textup{gr}}\circ\textup{gr}
is a natural isomorphism of functors making the following diagram commutative

\textstyle{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
gr
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
gr
\textstyle{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\textup{gr}}}
\textstyle{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

A morphism two such triples
(\Phi,\Phi_{\textup{gr}},\alpha)\to(\Phi^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime}_{\textup{gr}},\alpha^{\prime})
is a pair of natural transformations
\beta:\Phi\Rightarrow\Phi^{\prime}
,
\beta_{\textup{gr}}:\Phi_{\textup{gr}}\Rightarrow\Phi^{\prime}_{\textup{gr}}
such that
\textup{gr}\circ\beta\in\textup{End}(\textup{gr}\circ\Phi)
and
\beta_{\textup{gr}}\circ\textup{gr}\in\textup{End}(\Phi_{\textup{gr}}\circ\textup{gr})
are intertwined by the isomorphism
\alpha
. Then
\textup{End}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
has an obvious monoidal structure under composition of functors.

Let
\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})\subset\textup{End}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
be monoidal subcategory with further compatibility data with the action of
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
and on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
(any morphisms are required to be compatible with the
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
-action as well). We have forgetful functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.31061pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.31061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 62.31061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.79173pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\omega}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.31061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.8018pt\raise 5.49306pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\omega_{\textup{gr}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 174.62628pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 174.62628pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

7.2.12 Proposition
.

The category
\mathrm{Wak}
acts on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
compatibly with the action of
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
via the canonical grading functors on both categories. In other words, the action functor
\mathrm{Wak}\to\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C})
has a canonical lift to a monoidal functor

a_{\mathrm{Wak}}:\mathrm{Wak}\to\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr}).

Proof.

The action of
\mathrm{Wak}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
follows immediately from the action of
\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}
.

We show that for any
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
and any
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, convolution with
J_{\mu}
satisfies

(7.12)

(F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G})\star_{0}J_{\mu}=F_{\leq\lambda+\mu}(\mathcal{G}\star_{0}J_{\mu})

as subobjects of
\mathcal{G}\star_{0}J_{\mu}
.

To show (
7.12
), the argument is the same as the proof of Lemma
7.2.7
(3): define another filtration
F^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}\star_{0}J_{\mu}
by
F^{\prime}_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=F_{\leq\lambda-\mu}\mathcal{G}\star_{0}J_{\mu}
. We check that
F^{\prime}
is also an admissible filtration because its associated graded are
\textup{gr}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cong\textup{gr}_{\lambda-\mu}\mathcal{G}\star_{0}J_{\mu}
, which is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda-\mu}\star_{0}J_{\mu}\cong\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
by Proposition
7.2.10
(2). Hence
F^{\prime}
has to be equal to the filtration
F
on
\mathcal{G}
by the uniqueness of the admissible filtration, proving (
7.12
).

Now consider any objects
\mathcal{K}\in\mathrm{Wak}
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, each equipped with canonical filtrations
F_{\leq\mu}\mathcal{K}
(whose associated graded are direct sums of
J_{\mu}
) and
F_{\leq\lambda}\mathcal{G}
. Then (
7.12
) implies that

F_{\leq\lambda}(\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\mathcal{K})=\sum_{\lambda^{\prime}+\mu\leq\lambda}(F_{\leq\lambda^{\prime}}\mathcal{G})\star_{0}(F_{\leq\mu}\mathcal{K})

as subobjects of
\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\mathcal{K}
. Passing to the associated graded we get a canonical functorial isomorphism

(\textup{gr}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\mathcal{G})\star_{0}(\textup{gr}_{\mu}\mathcal{K})\cong\textup{gr}_{\lambda^{\prime}+\mu}(\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\mathcal{K})

which gives a lifting of
\mathcal{K}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\in\textup{End}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
. Moreover, the compatibility of the canonical filtrations on objects in
{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
with convolution by
({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}
as in Lemma
7.2.7
(3) shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
indeed lands in the subcategory
\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
. One easily checks that the assignment
\mathcal{K}\mapsto\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
extends to a monoidal functor
a_{\mathrm{Wak}}:\mathrm{Wak}\to\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})
, as desired.

∎

7.3.
Proof of Theorem
6.4.3

7.3.1.
Reduction to equivariant version

Since
\rho
can be factored as in (
6.22
) as the composition of
{}^{T}\rho
and the forgetful functor
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}\to D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
, it suffices to prove the version of Theorem
6.4.3
for
{}^{T}\rho
. Below we will work systematically with the
T
-equivariant versions
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
and
{}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
, with the actions of
{}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T}
.

7.3.2.
Central functor

Recall Gaitsgory’s central functor
[
25
]

\mathcal{Z}:\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}\to\mathcal{H}_{0}.

By
[
1
, Proposition 5(b)]
, we have
\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{K})\in\mathrm{Wak}
for
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
. This way we get a monoidal functor
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}:\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\to\mathrm{Wak}
. By passing to associated graded, we get a monoidal functor with a canonical central structure

\mathcal{Z}^{ss}_{\mathrm{Wak}}:\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}}\mathrm{Wak}\xrightarrow{\textup{gr}}\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}.

By
[
1
, Theorem 6]
, after identifying both sides above with
\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})
(via
\mathrm{Sat}
) and
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
(see (
7.5
)), there is a natural isomorphism of monoidal functors

(7.13)

\mathcal{Z}^{ss}_{\mathrm{Wak}}\cong\textup{Res}^{G^{\vee}}_{T^{\vee}}:\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}).

Now consider the commutative diagram

(7.14)

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{ss}_{\mathrm{Wak}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Wak}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathrm{Wak}}}
gr
\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C};\textup{gr})^{\textup{rev}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\textup{gr}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a^{ss}_{\mathrm{Wak}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})^{\textup{rev}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{End}({}^{T}\pi_{*}|{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit})^{\textup{rev}}}

Here the bottom right equivalence is induced by
{}^{T}\pi_{*}|_{{}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss}}
from Lemma
7.2.6
.

7.3.3 Lemma
.

The composition of the arrows through the top row of (
7.14
) down to
\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit})^{\textup{rev}}
is naturally isomorphic to the monoidal functor defined using the action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph}}_{0}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\textup{Gr}}
in (
6.20
).

Proof.

For
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}
and
\mathcal{G}\in{}^{T}\mathcal{C}
, we need to give a functorial isomorphism

{}^{T}\pi_{*}(\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}(\mathcal{K}))\cong({}^{T}\pi_{*}\mathcal{G})\star_{0}\mathcal{K}.

We have

\pi_{*}(\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}(\mathcal{K}))\cong\mathcal{G}\star_{0}\pi_{\textup{Gr},*}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}(\mathcal{K})\cong\mathcal{G}\star_{0}({}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K}).

Here
\pi_{\textup{Gr},*}
is the direct image functor
\mathcal{H}_{0}\to D^{b}(\mathbf{I}_{0}\backslash\textup{Gr})
;
{}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K}\in D^{b}(\mathbf{I}_{0}\backslash\textup{Gr})
is the same as
\mathcal{K}
but with the left
\mathbf{G}
-equivariance forgotten down to
\mathbf{I}_{0}
-equivariance. We are using here that
\pi_{*}\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{K})\cong{}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K}
, which follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{Z}
by nearby cycles
[
25
]
. Since
{}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K}
comes from the
\mathbf{G}
-equivariant object
\mathcal{K}
, we have

\mathcal{G}\star_{0}({}^{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\mathcal{K})\cong({}^{T}\pi_{*}\mathcal{G})\star_{0}\mathcal{K}

where on the right side the convolution contracts
\mathbf{G}
-equivariance while on the left it contracts
\mathbf{I}_{0}
-equivariance.
∎

7.3.4.
Proof of Theorem
6.4.3
(1)

Lemma
7.3.3
in particular shows that the action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
on
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
preserves the heart
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
. This also implies the action of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
preserves the heart
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
, because the forgetful functor
{}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{\heartsuit}
is essentially surjective by Lemma
6.3.2
. This proves part (1) of Theorem
6.4.3
.

7.3.5.
Proof of Theorem
6.4.3
(2)

Lemma
7.3.3
together with the commutative diagram (
7.14
) gives a factorization of
\rho^{\heartsuit}
as the composition

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}}\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\xrightarrow{a_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}}\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})^{\textup{rev}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}.

By (
7.13
) and Corollary
7.2.11
, the above diagram fits into a commutative diagram with canonical isomorphisms of monoidal functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Wak}^{ss}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathrm{Wak}}^{ss}}
\textstyle{\textup{End}_{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}}({}^{T}\mathcal{C}^{ss})^{\textup{rev}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{({}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{T})^{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{Res}^{G^{\vee}}_{T^{\vee}}}
\textstyle{\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
inv
\textstyle{\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})}

This finishes the proof of Theorem
6.4.3
. ∎

7.4.
Examples

We describe some objects in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
when
G
is of type
A
. We ignore Tate twists here. We will identify the Kirillov category on the open
G/B\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
with sheaves on
G/B
and similarly for other parahoric level structures at
0
.

7.4.1 Example

(
\textup{SL}_{n}
)
.

We consider the pushforward of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
to
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
for certain parahorics
\mathbf{P}
and consider some irreducible summands of these when
G=\textup{SL}_{n}
.

The only cases of interest are when
s_{0}\in W_{\mathbf{P}}
. Let
\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
be the parahoric whose Weyl group is given by
W\cap W_{\mathbf{P}}
, and let
P\subset G
be the corresponding parabolic subgroup. Then the map
\pi_{\mathbf{P}}
factors through
\pi_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
. Thus we can simply compute the pushforward along the map
\pi_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime},\mathbf{P}}
.

Assume that
W_{\mathbf{P}}
includes
s_{i}
for
i<k
and
i>j
and does not include
s_{j}
and
s_{k}
. Then the relevant orbits in
G/P\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P})
are given by

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{P}}(0)=\{(V_{i})\in G/P|\psi V_{k}\subset V_{j}\}.

This is a Hessenberg variety in
G/P
, generalizing the variety
\mathcal{H}(0)
from Section
4.1.3
. Our category thus contains
\pi_{\mathbf{P}}^{*}E_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{P}}(0)}[\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{P}}(0)+\dim(P/B)]
, the pullback of the constant sheaf on the Hessenberg variety
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{P}}(0)
, as one of the irreducible objects.

7.4.2 Example

(
\textup{SL}_{4}
)
