.

For
\textup{SL}_{3}
the previous construction essentially gives all irreducibles. Here we consider the category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
in the case of
\textup{SL}_{4}
, where some more interesting irreducibles appear. Of particular interest will be some non-trivial local systems appearing in the microlocalization of certain irreducible objects.

To construct these objects we will act with
\mathcal{H}_{0}
on some of the objects of Example
7.4
. Recall that there is an open inclusion
G/B\hookrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
. We will describe the restriction of some irreducibles to this
G/B
. From Example
7.4
we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\textup{IC}(G/B)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\textup{IC}(\{(V_{i})\in G/B|\psi V_{1}\subset V_{3}\})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\textup{IC}(\{(V_{i})\in G/B|\psi V_{2}\subset V_{3}\})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\textup{IC}(\{(V_{i})\in G/B|\psi V_{1}\subset V_{2}\}).

Denote by
\alpha_{0},\dots,\alpha_{3}
the simple roots, where
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{3}
denote the finite simple roots. For a simple root
\alpha_{i}
we denote by
\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}=\mathbf{P}_{i}
the parahoric generated by the Iwahori
\mathbf{I}
and the simple reflection
s_{\alpha_{i}}=s_{i}
. Finally denote
\pi_{i}=\pi_{\mathbf{P}_{i}}
. Then we have

\mathcal{F}_{4}:=\mathcal{F}_{3}\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s_{1}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{2}\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s_{3}}=\textup{IC}(\{(V_{i})\in G/B|\psi V_{2}\cap V_{2}\neq\{0\}\}).

Finally we compute
\mathcal{F}_{4}\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s_{2}}=\pi_{2}^{*}(\pi_{2})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{4}[1]
. Here note that when projecting
\{(V_{i})\in G/B|\psi V_{2}\cap V_{2}\neq\{0\}\}
to the partial flag variety, the fibers are cut out by a quadratic equation on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
determined by
\psi V_{2}\cap V_{2}\neq\{0\}
. The support of
\mathcal{F}_{4}
is irreducible and over the partial flag variety
G/P_{2}
is a branched double cover. It follows that on some open
U\subset G/P_{2}
,
(\pi_{2})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{4}\cong\mathcal{L}[5]\oplus E_{U}[5]
for some non-trivial local system
\mathcal{L}
. We thus get our final irreducible

\mathcal{F}_{5}:=\textup{IC}(\pi_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{L})).

The microlocalisation of this object to
G/B
produces a non-trivial local system on an open subset of the latter. It also gives rise to a trivializable local system on a different irreducible component of
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
.

The above irreducibles are in fact all the irreducibles up to the extended action of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and the length zero elements in
\mathcal{H}_{0}
of
\textup{GL}_{4}
.

7.5.
Full faithfulness of microlocalization functor

7.5.1.
Singular support

By the discussion of §
3
, we have a microlocalization functor

\widetilde{M}:\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\to\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi}).

Here we are using (
4.2
).

7.5.2 Lemma
.

For any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
,
\widetilde{M}(\mathcal{F})
is supported on finitely many components of the Lagrangian
\Lambda_{\psi}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is generated under
\mathcal{H}_{0}
by
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
, and
\mathcal{H}_{0}
is generated by
\textup{IC}_{w}
for
\ell(w)=0
or
1
as a monoidal category, we need to check

(1)

SS(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\subset\Lambda_{\psi}
and has finite-type support;

(2)

If
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
has finite-type support in
\Lambda_{\psi}
, so does
\mathcal{F}\star\textup{IC}_{w}
for any
\ell(w)=0
or
1
,
w\in\widetilde{W}
.

Consider the Lagrangian correspondence attached to
\pi=\pi_{\mathbf{G}}

(7.15)

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\psi}\cong\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d\pi}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\natural}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\cong\textup{Gr}_{\psi}}

(1) Use the formula of singular support under smooth pullback
\pi^{*}
, we have

SS(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})=SS(\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda})=d\pi\pi_{\natural}^{-1}(SS(\delta_{\lambda})).

From the diagram (
7.15
) we see
SS(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})\subset\Lambda_{\psi}
. More precisely, by Theorem
6.5.2
(1),
SS(\delta_{\lambda})
is the point
t^{\lambda}\in\textup{Gr}_{\psi}
. Therefore
SS(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})=d\pi\pi_{\natural}^{-1}(t^{\lambda})
, which is equal to
t^{\lambda}G[\![t]\!]/\mathbf{I}_{0}\subset\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
, and is isomorphic to the flag variety of
G
.

(2) The case
\ell(w)=0
is clear since
w
induces an automorphism of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
that preserves the Hitchin map hence preserves
\Lambda_{\psi}
.

We check the case
w=s
is a simple reflection. Let
\mathbf{P}_{s}\supset\mathbf{I}
be the standard parahoric subgroup corresponding to
s
, and
\pi_{s}=\pi_{\mathbf{P}_{s}}
. We have
\mathcal{F}\star\textup{IC}_{s}\cong\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}\langle{1}\rangle
. Consider the Lagrangian correspondence attached to
\pi_{s}

(7.16)

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s,0})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d\pi_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{s,\natural}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}}

Since
\pi_{s}
is smooth and proper, we have

SS(\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F})\subset d\pi_{s}\pi_{s,\natural}^{-1}\pi_{s,\natural}(d\pi_{s})^{-1}(SS(\mathcal{F})).

Now the diagram (
7.16
) is compatible with the Hitchin maps
f_{\psi}
and its analogue for
\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}
. Therefore
f_{\psi}SS(\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F})
is contained in
f_{\psi}SS(\mathcal{F})
, which is the point
a_{\psi}
. Hence
SS(\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F})\subset\Lambda_{\psi}
. Moreover,
d\pi_{s}
is a closed embedding and
\pi_{s,\natural}
is a fibration with fibers isomorphic to the flag variety of
L_{\mathbf{P}}
, both pushforward and pullback under
\pi_{s,\natural}
and
d\pi_{s}
send finite-type algebraic cycles to finite-type algebraic cycles. Therefore
SS(\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F})
is of finite type. This finishes the proof.
∎

7.5.3 Definition
.

Let
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})\subset\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
be the full subcategory consisting of objects supported on finitely many irreducible components of
\Lambda_{\psi}
such that the microstalks are finite dimensional bounded complexes of vector spaces.

Lemma
7.5.2
then shows that
\widetilde{M}|_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}
lands in
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
.

7.5.4 Theorem
.

For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
and
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
, the natural map

(7.17)

\textup{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\to\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})})=\textup{Hom}_{\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})}(\widetilde{M}(\mathcal{F}),\widetilde{M}(\mathcal{G}))

is an isomorphism. In particular,
\widetilde{M}
restricts to a fully faithful functor

M:\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\hookrightarrow\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi},fs}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi}).

Proof.

Let
w\in\widetilde{W}
be either of length
1
or length
0
. We have the adjunction

(7.18)

\textup{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{F}\star\textup{IC}_{w},\mathcal{G})\cong\textup{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\star\textup{IC}_{w^{-1}}).

We claim that there is a similar adjunction

(7.19)

\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F}\star\textup{IC}_{w},\mathcal{G})})\cong\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\star\textup{IC}_{w^{-1}})})

that is compatible with (
7.18
) under the map (
7.17
). Indeed, when
\ell(w)=0
this is clear since
\star\textup{IC}_{w}
is induced by an automorphism of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{I}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty})
. When
w=s
is a simple reflection, then
\star\textup{IC}_{s}=\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\langle{1}\rangle
. The desired adjunction follows from

\displaystyle\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}[1],\mathcal{G})})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\pi_{s*}\mathcal{F}[1],\pi_{s*}\mathcal{G})})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F}[1],\pi_{s}^{!}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{G})[-1]})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F},\pi_{s}^{*}\pi_{s*}\mathcal{G}[1])}).

Here the first and second isomorphisms follow by taking sections of the isomorphisms in
[
32
, Proposition 4.4.6 (iii)(iv)]
over
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
( using that
\pi_{s}
is smooth and proper).

Since
\{\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\star\textup{IC}_{w_{1}}\star\cdots\star\textup{IC}_{w_{n}};\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T),\ell(w_{i})\leq 1,n\in\mathbb{N}\}
generate
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
by definition, it suffices to check (
7.17
) for
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
in this collection. By the adjunctions (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), we reduce to the case
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{G}
arbitrary. In this case, using adjunctions analogous to (
7.18
) and (
7.19
) for
\pi=\pi_{\mathbf{G}}
instead of
\pi_{s}
, we get

\textup{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda},\mathcal{G})=\textup{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}}(\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda},\mathcal{G})\cong\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\delta_{\lambda},\pi_{*}\mathcal{G})

and

\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda},\mathcal{G})})=\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda},\mathcal{G})})\cong\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\delta_{\lambda},\pi_{*}\mathcal{G})}).

Therefore we reduce to showing that the natural map

\textup{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\to\textup{H}^{0}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}},\mu\mathrm{hom}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})})=\textup{Hom}_{\mu\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}})}(M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}),M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism for
\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
. This follows from Theorem
6.5.2
, which says that
M_{\mathbf{G}}
is an equivalence.
∎

7.6.
Singular support characterization of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}

In this subsection we show the converse to Lemma
7.5.2
, characterizing

\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
as a full subcategory in the Kirillov category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
specified by a condition on the singular support.

We will need an auxiliary construction. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
let

(G/B)_{\lambda}=t^{\lambda}G[\![t]\!]/\mathbf{I}_{0}\subset\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
be the corresponding irreducible component isomorphic to the flag variety
G/B
. For an object
\mathcal{F}
in the Kirillov category
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
we set
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})=i_{\psi}^{*}\mu_{(G/B)_{\lambda}}(\mathcal{F})
where
\mu
denotes microlocalization
and
i_{\psi}
is the section of the conormal bundle to
(G/B)_{\lambda}
defined by
\psi
.

7.6.1 Lemma
.

For any
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, we have:

(1)

\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})\cong\mu\mathrm{hom}(\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda},\mathcal{F})
.

(2)

The functors
\mu_{\lambda}
are compatible with the Hecke action at infinity in the sense that there are canonical isomorphisms

\mu_{\lambda+\nu}(\widehat{\Delta}_{\nu}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F})\cong\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F}),\quad\forall\nu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T).

Proof.

(1) follows from the definition.

(2) is a consequence of Proposition
A.4.3
. To see this, note that it is enough to prove the case of
\nu=-\lambda
, as
\widehat{\Delta}_{\nu}\star_{\infty}\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\cong\widehat{\Delta}_{\nu+\lambda}
.

Consider the
G[\tau]=G[t^{-1}]
-orbit
U_{0}=G[\tau]I_{0}/I_{0}\subset\textup{Fl}
. This is an open neighbourhood of
(G/B)_{0}
and the character
\psi
induces a map
\psi_{0}:U_{0}\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times(G/B)_{0}
. We can translate this open by multipliying with
t^{\lambda}
to obtain
U_{\lambda}=t^{\lambda}U_{0}
and a map
\psi_{\lambda}:U_{\lambda}\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times(G/B)_{\lambda}
. We have

\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F}|_{U_{0}}\cong\psi^{*}_{0}(\psi_{\lambda})_{!}(\mathcal{F}|_{U_{\lambda}})\langle{d_{-\lambda}}\rangle.

As
\psi_{0}
is a smooth map, we can rewrite
\mu_{0}(\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda}\star_{\infty}\mathcal{F})\cong\phi((\psi_{\lambda})_{!}\mathcal{F})
, where
\phi
is taking nearby cycles along the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-factor in
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times(G/B)_{\lambda}
. This is precisely the right hand side of the isomorphism in
A.4.3
. The left hand side is by construction
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})
and we thus obtain the required isomorphism.
∎

For an object
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
, we define its
combinatorial
support
to be the set of
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
for which
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
.

7.6.2 Proposition
.

The following properties are equivalent for an object
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
:

a)
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

b)
SS(\mathcal{F})
consists of finitely many irreducible components of
\Lambda_{\psi}
.

c)
\mathcal{F}
is
T
-monodromic and the combinatorial support of
\mathcal{F}
is finite.

Proof.

We check that a)
\Rightarrow
b)
\Rightarrow
c)
\Rightarrow
a).

Here a)
\Rightarrow
b) is the content of Lemma
7.5.2
. The implication b)
\Rightarrow
c) follows from Lemma
7.6.1
a) since support of
\mu\mathrm{hom}
between
two constructible complexes is contained in the intersection of their singular supports and
SS(\mathcal{F})\subset\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
implies
\mathcal{F}
is
T
-monodromic.
It remains to check that c)
\Rightarrow
a).

We first relate the support and the combinatorial support for a
T
-monodromic sheaf
\mathcal{F}
. Consider the stratification of
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{\psi}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
by preimages of points in
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}_{0})
which are in bijection with
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
the set of relevant points are
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
by Lemma
7.1.2
. We show that:

(7.20)

If
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
contains an open substack in the support of
\mathcal{F}
, then
\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in W\cdot\lambda
.

It follows that if the combinatorial support vanishes for a
W
invariant set
S\subset\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
such that
\bigcup_{\lambda\in S}G[\tau]t^{\lambda}
is open, then
\mathcal{F}
vanishes in this open.

To show (
7.20
), consider
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}
as in the proof of Lemma
7.6.1
. Let
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
the intersection of
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
and
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, which is an open in
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
. The intersection of
(G/B)_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
is a Levi flag variety
P/B\subset\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
. We can describe
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
as the attracting locus of this Levi flag variety
P/B
under the action of some cocharacter of the maximal torus
T
. It follows that the
G[\tau]^{1}
-orbit of
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
is contained in the attracting cell of this
P/B
under the cocharacter
\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{\Delta}\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
and thus contained in the open
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, as this is an open
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
stable neighbourhood of
P/B
.

As
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}
an affine bundle over
(G/B)_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and the
G[\tau]^{1}
-orbit of
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
is contained in
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, we have that the
G[\tau]^{1}
-orbit of
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
lives over some locally closed subvariety
V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}\subset(G/B)_{\lambda^{\prime}}
. The result follows from
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathcal{F})
inducing a conservative functor
D^{b}(U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})\rightarrow D^{b}(V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})
. To see this, note that the by Proposition
7.6.1
this functor can be written as the following composition

(7.21)

D^{b}(U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{(j_{U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}})_{!}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\xrightarrow{\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda^{\prime}}\star_{\infty}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\xrightarrow{j_{V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}}D^{b}(V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}).

By construction the composition
D^{b}(U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{(j_{U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}})_{!}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}\xrightarrow{\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda^{\prime}}\star_{\infty}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
produces sheaves that are
!
extended from
V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
. It follows that the last functor,
j_{V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}
is conservative on the image of the previous functors. The action
\widehat{\Delta}_{-\lambda^{\prime}}\star_{\infty}
is an invertible functor with inverse given by
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\star_{\infty}
and so the composition in equation (
7.21
) is conservative.

Now let
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
with
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
containing an open substack in its support. Then there exists
\lambda^{\prime}
such that
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
contains an open substack in the support of
\mathcal{F}
. We show
j_{V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
. To see this, restrict the bundle
U_{l^{\prime}}
over
V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
and note that this intersects the support of
\mathcal{F}
only at the
G[\tau]^{1}
-orbit of
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
. It follows that
j_{V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
is the above conservative functor
D^{b}(U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})\rightarrow D^{b}(V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}})
applied to
j_{U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}\mathcal{F}
. But by assumption
U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}
contains an open part in the support of
\mathcal{F}
and thus
j_{U_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}\mathcal{F}\neq 0
. It follows that
j_{V_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}}^{!}\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
and thus
\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
.

Assume
\mathcal{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\psi}
has finite combinatorial support.
Lemma
7.6.1
(1) shows that the functor
\mu_{\lambda}
is exact in the perverse
t
-structure, so we can assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a perverse sheaf.
It suffices to show that
\mathcal{F}
has a subquotient in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, then the claim follows by induction on the length of
\mathcal{F}
. Using Lemma
7.6.1
(2) we can use Hecke action at infinity to reduce to
the case when
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-fixed points in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
are isolated for all
\lambda

in the combinatorial support of
\mathcal{F}
; notice that this action is
t
-exact, so perversity and length of
\mathcal{F}
are preserved. It follows that the support of
\mathcal{F}
contains one of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-fixed points in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
as an open substack in its support. For an isolated fixed point
x
in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
the IC extension of the unique irreducible sheaf in the Kirillov category of its
G[\tau]^{1}
orbit
G[\tau]^{1}\cdot x
is in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
. ∎

7.6.3 Lemma
.

For an isolated
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-fixed point
x
in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
the IC extension of the unique irreducible sheaf in
\Kir(G[\tau]^{\psi}\backslash G[\tau]^{1}x)
(where
G[\tau]^{\psi}=\ker(G[\tau]^{1}\to\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{\psi_{1}}\mathbb{G}_{a})
) is in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we will denote by
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}w)
the IC extension of the unique irreducible sheaf in
\Kir(G[\tau]^{\psi}\backslash G[\tau]^{1}w)
.

For
s
a simple reflection, let
w
and
ws
be both isolated fixed point in some
(G/B)_{\lambda}
, such that
ws>w
in the Bruhat order. We have that
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}w)
is a summand of
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}ws)*\textup{IC}_{s}
as the restriction of
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}ws)*\textup{IC}_{s}
to the orbit of
w
, which is open in its support, is the constant local system. It follows that if
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}ws)\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, then we also have
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}w)\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

Further note that for
\lambda
such that
(G/B)_{\lambda}
has isolated fixed points
\pi^{*}\delta_{\lambda}
is indeed the IC-sheaf on the orbit of
wt^{\lambda}
of minimal length for
w\in W
.

We just need to show that for each isolated fixed point
x
in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
, there exists
w>x
such that
\textup{IC}(G[\tau]^{1}w)\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
and a chain
w=x_{0},\dots x=x_{k}
such that
x_{i}
isolated fixed points and
x_{i+1}=x_{i}s_{i}
. This follows after noting that
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-fixed points in
(G/B)_{\lambda}
are isolated precisely when
\lambda
pairs non-zero with every root and so this corresponds to some set of alcoves strictly contained in the
W
-cones. Thus for any
x
isolated, there exist
\lambda
far enough in the corresponding cone, such that the minimal element in
Wt^{\lambda}
is bigger than
x
. These two elements are in the same cone and so connected by a chain
w=x_{0},\dots x=x_{k}
such that
x_{i}
isolated fixed points and
x_{i+1}=x_{i}s_{i}
.
∎

8.
The equivalence

We prove the main equivalence (Theorem
1.1.3
) and give its application to small quantum groups.

8.1.
Recollections

Recall
\mathbf{G}=G[\![t]\!]\subset G(\!(t)\!)
and
\mathbf{I}\subset\mathbf{G}
is an Iwahori subgroup.
Let
G^{\vee}
be the Langlands dual group of
G
defined over
E
(the coefficient field of sheaves in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, assumed to be of characteristic zero). Let
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
be the flag variety of
G^{\vee}
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}=T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
its cotangent bundle.

8.1.1 Theorem
.

a) We have monoidal equivalences

(8.1)

D(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{G})\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\textup{pt})\longleftrightarrow D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}),

(8.2)

D(\mathbf{I}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})\longleftrightarrow D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}),

where
\longleftrightarrow
denotes linear Koszul duality
[
39
]
.

b) We have an equivalence

(8.3)

D(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})\longleftrightarrow D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}})

intertwining the convolution action of the two monoidal categories in a).

Proof.

The equivalence (
8.1
) was constructed in
[
11
]
, the equivalence
(
8.2
)
in
[
8
, Theorem 1(4)]
. A special case of
[
15
, Theorem 6.1]
5
5

5

An alternative
reference is
[
2
]
, we neither prove nor use the fact that the equivalences given by the two constructions coincide.
yields a monodromic version
of (
8.3
)

(8.4)

D(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}^{0})\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}})\longleftrightarrow D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}).

The equivalence in (
8.3
) follows from (
8.4
) by the argument of

[
8
, §9.3]
. Compatibility of (
8.4
) with the action of monodromic affine Hecke category
is established in
[
15
]
, compatibility of the equivalence in (
8.3
) with (
8.2
)
follows by inspecting the constructions. Since linear Koszul duality is compatible with the convolution action, we get that Koszul duality in (
8.3
), (
8.2
) is also compatible with the action.
It remains to check compatibility
with (
8.1
).

Recall the tautological endomorphism
\tau
of identity in
\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
:
it is characterized by the property that for
\mathcal{F}\in\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})

the action of
\tau
on the fiber
\mathcal{F}_{x}
equals the action of
x
coming from the equivariant
structure. We use the same notation for the similar endomorphism of the identity functor of

\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}})
.
We first check that the restriction of the composed Koszul duality in (
8.3
) to pure complexes carries multiplication
\bf{c}
by the first Chern class of the determinant bundle to
\tau
.

For a weight
\lambda
let
L_{\lambda}
be the corresponding line bundle
Fl
, and let
h_{\lambda}
be the function on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}
pulled back from the corresponding linear function on
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
.
We claim that the composed Koszul duality in (
8.3
) sends
multiplication by the first Chern class of
L_{\lambda}

to multiplication by
h_{\lambda}
. To see this, one can interpret
c_{1}(L_{\lambda})
as the deformation
class corresponding to the deformation of the category
D(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})
to the category of monodromic sheaves on the punctured total space of
L_{\lambda}
. Likewise, it is easy to
see that
h_{\lambda}
is Koszul dual to the deformation class of the deformation
of
\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}
to

\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}_{\lambda}
where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}_{\lambda}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\times_{\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}}\{E\cdot\lambda\}
. Thus compatibility follows from (
8.4
).

Recall the Wakimoto object
J_{\lambda}\in D^{b}(\mathbf{I}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})
, let
j_{\lambda}
be its direct
image to
D^{b}(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})
. It is easy to see that the action
\bf{c}
on
j_{\lambda}
coincides with the action of
c_{1}(L_{\kappa(\lambda)})
,
where
\kappa
is the map from weights of
G^{\vee}
to weights of
G
coming from the quadratic form used to define the determinant bundle. The image of
j_{\lambda}

under the composed functor in (
8.3
) is the direct image of
\mathcal{O}(\lambda)
under the map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\to\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
, the parallel compatibility between the tautological
endomorphism and the action of
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
is also clear for that object. Thus we get the compability between
\bf{c}
and
\tau
for
j_{\lambda}
. An irreducible
\textup{IC}_{\lambda}\in D(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})

pulled back from the Satake
category admits a ”filtration” (a collection of distinguished triangles) by
j_{\lambda}
and
\textup{Ext}(\textup{IC}_{\lambda},\textup{IC}_{\lambda})

maps injectively to the endomorphisms of the associated graded of that filtration, so compatibility between
\bf{c}
and
\tau
holds for
\textup{IC}_{\lambda}
. Now we see it holds for
every pure object since push-forward from
\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}
to the Satake category is faithful on pure complexes.

Now compatibility of (
8.1
), (
8.3
) with the action of the category of pure complexes in the Satake category on pure complexes in
\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I}
follows
from compatibility between
\bf{c}
and
\tau
in view of the Tannakian formalism,
see
[
1
, §3.4]
. Finally, compatibility for pure complexes implies the desired compatibility by the standard
formality from weights argument, see e.g.
[
11
, §6.5]
. ∎

Next, by Corollary
6.2.3
, we have an equivalence

(8.5)

\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\cong D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))=D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}).

This equivalence intertwines the action of
\widehat{\Delta}_{\lambda}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
on the left and tensoring with the one-dimensional representation
E(\lambda)
on the right.

By Theorem
6.4.3
, the spherical action of
\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\cong\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sph},\heartsuit}_{0}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
intertwines with the action by tensoring on
D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\textup{pt})
via

\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\textup{Res}}\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\textup{inv}}\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}).

Then
T^{\vee}
naturally acts on
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
and hence on
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}
. Our goal in this section is to prove Theorem
8.3.1
establishing an equivalence

(8.6)

\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}),

where
\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})\subset\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})
is the full subcategory of sheaves set-theoretically supported on the zero section

\mathcal{B}^{\vee}\subset T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}
.

8.1.2 Remark
.

Heuristically, (
8.6
) follows from the above equivalences in view of the full embeddings:

\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}^{ho}\otimes_{D^{ho}(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{G})}D^{ho}(\mathbf{G}\backslash G(\!(t)\!)/\mathbf{I})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{ho}_{\psi}

(8.7)

D^{ho}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\textup{pt})\otimes_{D^{ho}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\textup{pt})}D^{ho}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{pt}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})\hookrightarrow D^{ho}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}),

whose existence follows from first principles, where the superscript
ho
refers to the appropriate homotopy lift of the triangulated categories.

However, making it into a rigorous argument
involves some subtleties of homotopical algebra, including a homotopy version of compatibilities of (
8.1
), (
8.3
) with the action of the monoidal categories on the module categories and so forth.
The argument below follows that general idea, but instead of considering the tensor product of homotopy categories we reduce
the construction to tensor product of additive categories of projective objects in relevant abelian categories which is quite close
to the familiar tensor product of rings over a given commutative ring. The reduction is achieved by invoking Koszul duality; as a byproduct of this approach
we get a stronger statement involving an equivalence of abelian categories.

8.2.
Quantum groups and noncommutative Springer resolution

Recall
[
16
]
that the
noncommutative Springer resolution
is the ring
A=\textup{End}_{\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}})}(\mathcal{E})^{op}
where
\mathcal{E}
is a
G^{\vee}
-equivariant vector bundle, the projective generator of the unique exotic
t
-structure
[
16
, §1.4.1, Theorem 1.5.1]
on
D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}))
. Let
\bar{A}=A\otimes_{E[\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}]}E=\textup{End}(\mathcal{E}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}})^{op}
.
We have equivalences
D^{b}(A\mathrm{-mod})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}))
,
D^{b}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
, where
\mathrm{-mod}
stands for the category of finitely
generated modules.

Let
U_{q}
be the Kac-De Concini quantum group at a root of unity
\zeta
of order
\ell
. We require
\ell
to be odd and larger than the Coxeter number. If
\mathfrak{g}
has a factor of type
G_{2}
we also require that
\ell
be coprime to
3
.

By
[
30
]
the center of
U_{q}
contains the Harish-Chandra center
Z_{HC}\cong E[T^{\vee}]^{W}
and the
\ell
-center
Z_{\ell}\cong E[G^{\vee}_{0}]
where
G^{\vee}_{0}=B^{\vee}_{-}B^{\vee}_{+}

is the open Bruhat cell. Thus the quotient of
U_{q}
by the trivial
\ell
-central character
U_{q}\otimes_{Z_{\ell}}E=u_{q}
is the small quantum group. We let
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}
be the summand
of
u_{q}
corresponding to a regular block.

We record the relation between these categories following from the literature.

8.2.1 Proposition
.

(1)

The algebra
\bar{A}
can be equipped with a grading making it a Koszul quadratic algebra.
The Koszul dual algebra
\bar{A}^{!}
is Morita equivalent to
A_{0}:=A\otimes_{E[\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}]}E
; the equivalence can be chosen
to be compatible with the
G^{\vee}
action.

(2)

We have compatible equivalences
A_{0}\mathrm{-mod}\cong u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}
,
A_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}}\cong u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}

where
\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}}
denotes the category of
T^{\vee}
-equivariant
A_{0}
-modules and
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}
denotes the
category of
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}
modules graded by the weight lattice
\Lambda
in a way compatible with the
\Lambda/\ell\Lambda

action coming from the action of the Cartan generators
K_{i}\in u_{q}
.

(3)

We have:

A_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}}\cong\textup{Perv}_{\mathbf{I}^{0}}(\textup{Gr})\cong U_{q}^{Lus}\mathrm{-mod}_{0},

where
U_{q}^{Lus}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}
is
a regular block in the category of modules over Lusztig’s big quantum group.

Proof.

The first statement in part (1) is a special case of
[
16
, Proposition 5.5]
. The
G^{\vee}
-equivariant Morita equivalence of the Koszul
dual algebra
\bar{A}^{!}
with
A_{0}
follows from the arguments of
[
46
]
. Namely, by
[
39
]
we have a Koszul type equivalence
(i.e. an equivalence sending homological shift to the composition of the homological shift and twist by the tautological character of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
)

D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}))

where the fiber product in the right hand side is understood
to be derived. Recall the equivalences

D^{b}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))\text{ and }D^{b}(A_{0}\mathrm{-mod})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\})),

where the latter follows from the equivalence
D^{b}(A\mathrm{-mod})\cong D^{b}(\textup{Coh}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}))
by base change, along with their
\mathbb{G}_{m}
equivariant versions. It remains
to show that projective graded (
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant)

A_{0}
modules are sent into semisimple complexes of
\bar{A}
-modules (i.e. a sum of simple modules and their homological shifts).
One such projective module
P_{0}
corresponds to the structure sheaf of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}
. Its image under Koszul duality is

i_{*}(\mathcal{O})
where
i:\mathcal{B}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
is the zero section. This object corresponds to an irreducible
\bar{A}
-module. Other projective modules

P_{0}
are obtained from
P_{0}
by
geometric reflection functors

[
16
]
, thus we are reduced to checking that the Koszul dual
functors in the sense of
[
39
]
correspond to semi-simple
endofunctors of
D^{b}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
.
For a base field of large positive characteristic the latter is shown in

[
46
]
. The present case of a characteristic zero base field follows.

The first equivalence in part (3) is
[
15
, Corollary 6.3]
, while the second one is the main result of Lusztig’s program. See overview in the introduction to
[
2
]
; the main result of
[
2
]
provides an alternative proof.

The composed equivalence between the first and the third category in part (3) is easily checked to be
compatible with the natural
\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})
action. Using the description of modules over the small quantum group as
\textup{Rep}(G^{\vee})
eigenobjects in
representations of
U_{q}^{Lus}

[
3
]
we get compatible equivalences in part (2). ∎

8.2.2 Remark
.

Let
U_{q}^{0}
be the quotient of
U_{q}
by a regular integral character of
Z_{HC}
. Let
U_{q}^{0}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}
be the category of (finitely generated) modules
where
Z_{\ell}
acts with the trivial generalized central character.
Using a result of
[
48
]
, one can construct compatible equivalences
\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}\cong U_{q}^{0}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}
,

\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}^{T^{\vee}}\cong U_{q}^{0}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}^{gr}
where
U_{q}^{0}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}^{gr}

denotes the
category of
U_{q}^{0}
modules graded by the weight lattice
\Lambda
in a way compatible with the
\Lambda/\ell\Lambda

grading by generalized eigenspaces of the Cartan generators
K_{i}\in u_{q}
.
This gives an alternative way to establish the above equivalences between derived
categories of coherent sheaves and quantum group modules, bypassing
[
2
]
. See the discussion
in
[
14
]
.

8.2.3 Corollary
.

Consider the dga
\bar{A}_{0}=A_{0}\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{E[\mathfrak{t}]}E=\bar{A}\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{E[\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}]}E
.
We have a
t
-exact equivalence
D^{b}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})\cong D_{\mathbf{I}}(\textup{Gr})
.

Proof.

This follows from the first equivalence in Proposition
8.2.1
c) by base change, cf.
[
8
, §9.3.1]
. ∎

8.2.4 Remark
.

The proof of Koszul duality between
\bar{A}^{!}
and
A_{0}
can be adapted to show Koszul duality between the dga
\bar{A}_{0}
and the ring
A
:
again we have a Koszul type equivalence
D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}))\cong D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\})
by
[
39
]
. This functor sends the structure
sheaf of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}
to
i_{*}(\mathcal{O})
where
i:\mathcal{B}^{\vee}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}
. The two objects correspond, respectively, to a projective
A
-module and an irreducible
\bar{A}_{0}
-module. Furthermore, the geometric reflection functors commute with semisimple functors on
D(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod})
which yields the statement.

8.3.
The equivalence

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}=\mathcal{D}_{\psi}^{\heartsuit}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
be the subcategory of perverse sheaves.

8.3.1 Theorem
.

(1)

We have a canonical equivalence

\Theta:\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\simeq D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}).

It intertwines the action of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
coming from Corollary

5.4.5
with the action of the affine Hecke category (
8.2
).

(2)

The equivalence intertwines the exotic
t
-structure on
D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
with the

t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
induced by the perverse
t
-structure on constructible sheaves.
Thus it induces an equivalence

\Theta^{\heartsuit}:\mathcal{P}_{\psi}\cong\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}^{T^{\vee}}.

Proof.

Consider the subcategory
\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}}
of semisimple complexes (i.e. of sums of irreducible perverse sheaves and their homological shifts) in
D(\mathbf{G}\backslash LG/\mathbf{I})
.
Let
L_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
be the irreducible object with full support.
We have the functor
\varphi_{0}:D_{\mathbf{I}}(\textup{Gr})\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
sending
\mathcal{F}
to the convolution
L_{0}\star_{\mathbf{G}}\mathcal{F}
. Its essential image generates
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.
Using Corollary
8.2.3
we can identify
\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}}
with the full subcategory
\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})
of semisimple
complexes in
D^{b}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})
. We have a functor
\varphi_{1}:D^{b}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})\to D^{b}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})
given by pull back and restricting the equivariance. By inspection, the equivalences

D^{b}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})\cong D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}),

D^{b}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})\cong D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})

intertwine
\varphi_{1}
with the natural
functor between the coherent sheaves categories. Hence, the essential image of
\varphi_{1}
generates
D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}})
.
It thus suffices to identify the essential images
\varphi_{0}(\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}})
and
\varphi_{1}(\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}}))
and show formality of the corresponding
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}
algebras.

By Proposition
8.2.1
(1),
\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})
is equivalent to the category of
G^{\vee}
-equivariant projective graded
A
-modules.
By (
8.1
) the category
\textup{Irr}_{S}
of semisimple objects in the Satake category
D(\mathbf{G}\backslash LG/\mathbf{G})
is equivalent to the category
\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{fr}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
of projective
objects in
\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
.

By Theorem
6.4.3
(2),
\varphi_{0}|_{\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}}}
intertwines the action of
\textup{Irr}_{S}\cong\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{fr}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})

on
\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}}
with an action on its target factoring through the functor

\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{fr}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\to\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
given by pull back to
0\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
followed by restriction of equivariance from

G^{\vee}
to
T^{\vee}
. It follows that
\varphi_{0}|_{\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}}}
factors canonically through a functor from
T^{\vee}
-equivariant projective graded modules
over
A/\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}A=A_{0}
, which by Koszul duality is identified with
\textup{Irr}((\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}}))
,
to
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.
We now argue that the resulting functor

\varphi:\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is fully faithful.

By Theorem
8.1.1
(b) the functor
\varphi_{0}
is compatible
with the action of the affine Hecke category. It follows that

\varphi
is compatible with the action of
the category of semisimple complexes in the affine Hecke category
\textup{Irr}(\mathbf{I}\backslash LG/\mathbf{I})
.
This allows one to reduce checking that the map
\textup{Hom}(A,B)\to\textup{Hom}(\varphi(A),\varphi(B))
is an isomorphism to the case
when
A=\mathcal{F}_{0}=\pi_{\mathbf{G}}^{*}(\delta_{0})
.

One also checks that
\varphi
fits in commutative diagrams with the equivalence (
8.5
):

\textstyle{\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
8.5
)
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}}

\textstyle{D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
(
8.5
)
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{\psi}}

where
\alpha
and
\beta
come from the functors

p_{*}\iota^{*}:D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))\to D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
and

\iota_{*}p^{*}:D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))\to D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
as follows. We have
\alpha=p_{*}\iota^{*}|_{\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})}
, where we identify

\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})
with the image of its full embedding in
D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
). On the other hand,
\beta
post-composed with the full embedding

\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})\hookrightarrow D^{b}(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}))
is identified with
\iota_{*}p^{*}
.
Here
\iota:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}
is the embedding and
p:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\times^{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\{0\}\to\{0\}

is the projection. The commutativity follows from similar compatibilities between (
8.1
), (
8.3
) mentioned after (
8.3
).

It follows that the essential image of
D^{b}(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
in
\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}\mathrm{-mod}^{T^{\vee}})
is equivalent to the essential image of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}
in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

This yields an equivalence between the essential images
\varphi_{0}(\textup{Irr}_{\textup{Gr}})
and
\varphi_{1}(\textup{Irr}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}}))
.

Finally, formality in both cases is implied by coincidence of the inner (dilation) grading with the homological grading on the corresponding
Ext
spaces, cf.

[
11
, Proposition 6]
. For the essential image of
\varphi_{1}
this coincidence follows from Koszul duality. Also, the identification between the essential images of
\varphi_{1}

and
\varphi_{0}
carries the inner grading into the weight grading, since the same is true for the equivalence
D^{b}(\bar{A}_{0}\mathrm{-mod}^{G^{\vee}})\cong D_{\mathbf{I}}(\textup{Gr})
, this yields the
grading compatibility for the essential image of
\varphi_{0}
. This proves the equivalence in part (1). Compatibility with the action of
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
and of

\textup{Irr}(\mathbf{I}\backslash LG/\mathbf{I})
on the subcategory of semi-simple complexes follows, respectively, from Theorem
6.4.3
(2) and Theorem
8.1.1
(b) as pointed out above. The extension to the full derived category via
formality of
\mathbf{R}\textup{Hom}
is compatible with convolution, see e.g.
[
11
, §6.5]
,
this yields compatibility with monoidal actions claimed in part (1).

By Proposition
8.2.1
(3) we have an equivalence between the category
of
G^{\vee}
-equivariant
A_{0}
-modules and a regular block in the category of Lusztig’s big quantum group
U_{q}^{Lus}\mathrm{-mod}_{0}
.
Now (2) follows from
t
-exactness of the
(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\psi)
averaging functor
D_{\mathbf{I}^{0}}(\textup{Gr})\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
and
the compatibility of the coherent description of
D_{\mathbf{I}^{0}}(\textup{Gr})
with the equivalence of part (1). ∎

8.3.2 Corollary
.

(1)

The category
\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
is equivalent
to the category of finitely generated modules with nilpotent central character
over the Koszul dual to
u_{q}^{\widehat{0}}\mathrm{-mod}^{gr}
.

(2)

We have an exact full embedding
u_{q}^{0}\mathrm{-mod}\to\mathcal{P}_{\psi}
inducing a bijection between isomorphism classes of irreducible objects,
where
u_{q}^{0}
is the quotient of
u_{q}
by the regular Harish-Chandra central character.

8.3.3 Remark
.

As mentioned in §
1.4.1
, we expect the whole category
\mu\mathrm{Sh}_{\Lambda_{\psi}}(\mathcal{M}_{\psi})
to be embedded into
T^{\vee}
-equivariant ind-coherent sheaves on
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
, which can be identified with an open subset of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}
. Note that the objects
\mathfrak{d}_{\lambda}
from Section
7.2
are not objects in the category
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, however under the fully faithful embedding in Conjecture
1.4.2
, these objects correspond to the structure sheaf of the fibers of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}\to\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
over a
T^{\vee}
-fixed point of
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
, twisted by the
T^{\vee}
character
\lambda
. This should follow from the compatibility of the action of Wakimoto sheaves and the category
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

8.4.
Consequences on Grothendieck groups

By Lemma
7.5.2
, the characteristic cycles of objects in
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
are finite linear combinations of irreducible components of
\Lambda_{\psi}\cong\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
, which thus induces a map

CC:K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi})\to\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}}).

There are actions of the extended affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}=\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)\rtimes W
on both sides of the map
CC
, which we explain below.

8.4.1.
Right
\widetilde{W}
-action on
K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi})
from affine Hecke action

Consider the Grothendieck group
K(\mathcal{H}_{0})
of the the affine Hecke category. It is well-known that we have an isomorphism of rings

(8.8)

K(\mathcal{H}_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}[\widetilde{W}]

sending the class of the standard sheaf
i_{w!}E
on the
\mathbf{I}
-orbit
\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}
to
w\in\mathbb{Z}[\widetilde{W}]
for each
w\in\widetilde{W}
. The right action of the affine Hecke category
\mathcal{H}_{0}
, after passing to the Grothendieck group, induces a right action of
\widetilde{W}
on
K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi})
.

8.4.2.
Right
\widetilde{W}
-action on
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
: Springer action

The right action of the affine Weyl group
W_{\textup{aff}}
on the cohomology of affine Springer fibers is defined in
[
38
, §5]
. The extension to
\widetilde{W}=W_{\textup{aff}}\rtimes\Omega
(where
\Omega
is the subgroup of length zero elements in
\widetilde{W}
) is easy and can be found in
[
53
, 2.4]
.

8.4.3 Proposition
.

The map
CC
is equivariant under the right
\widetilde{W}
-actions up to twisting by the sign character
w\mapsto(-1)^{\ell(w)}
of
\widetilde{W}
.

Proof.

It suffices to check for each simple reflection
s
and each length zero element
\omega\in\Omega
.

For
\omega\in\Omega
, both the Hecke action and the Springer action are induced by the automorphism of
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
(resp.
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
) coming from any lifting of
\omega
to
N_{LG}(\mathbf{I})
on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I})
(resp.
\textup{Fl}=LG/\mathbf{I}
). Their agreement on characteristic cycles is clear.

For a simple reflection
s
, consider the shifted constant sheaf
\textup{IC}_{s}=E_{\mathbf{P}_{s}/\mathbf{I}}\langle{1}\rangle
supported on the flag variety of the parahoric
\mathbf{P}_{s}
of type
s
, viewed as an object in
\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Under the isomorphism (
8.8
),
\textup{IC}_{s}
corresponds to
-s-1\in\mathbb{Z}[\widetilde{W}]
. We thus reduce to proving the following statement: for
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
, we have

(8.9)

CC(\mathcal{F}\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s})=CC(\mathcal{F})\cdot s-CC(\mathcal{F})\in\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\Lambda_{\psi}}).

Using notation from §
7.1.7
,
\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s}
is the composition

\mathcal{D}_{\psi}\xrightarrow{\pi_{s*}}\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{s}^{*}[1]}\mathcal{D}_{\psi}.

Here
\pi_{s}=\pi_{\mathbf{P}_{s}}
. Referring to the diagram (
7.16
), using that
\pi_{s}
is proper and smooth, we have

(8.10)

CC(\mathcal{F}\star_{0}\textup{IC}_{s})=(d\pi_{s})_{*}(\pi_{s,\natural})^{*}(\pi_{s,\natural})_{*}(d\pi_{s})^{*}CC(\mathcal{F})

See
[
32
, Proposition 9.4.2, 9.4.3]
.

On the other hand, consider the moduli stack
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}
that classifies
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{s}},\varphi)
where
(\mathcal{E},\tau_{\infty},\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{s}})\in\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})
,
\varphi\in\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})\otimes\omega_{X}(2\cdot\infty+\underline{0})
satisfies the usual condition
\varphi\in\psi_{1}dt+\textup{Ad}^{*}(\mathcal{E})dt/t
at
\infty
, while at
0
,
\varphi\in\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{s}})dt/t
(i.e., in the lattice
(\textup{Lie}\ \mathbf{P}_{s})dt/t
after trivializing
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{s}})
on the formal disk at
0
), and the image of
\varphi
in
\textup{Ad}(\mathcal{E}_{L_{s}})dt/t
(here
\mathcal{E}_{L_{s}}
is the reduction of the
\mathbf{P}_{s}
-torsor
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{s}}
to the reductive quotient
L_{s}
of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
) is nilpotent.
We then have a Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{s}}
\textstyle{[\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L_{s}}/L_{s}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{L_{s}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{s}}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{N}_{L_{s}}/L_{s}]}

Here
\nu_{L_{s}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L_{s}}\to\mathcal{N}_{L_{s}}
is the Springer resolution of the nilpotent cone of
L_{s}
. We have the Springer action of
\langle{s}\rangle
on

\nu_{L_{s}*}\mathbb{D}\in D_{L_{s}}(\mathcal{N}_{L_{s}})
. By
!
-pullback to
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}
and proper base change, it gives an action of
\langle{s}\rangle
on
\nu_{s*}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}
, and hence on
\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})
by taking global sections. Under the isomorphism
\textup{H}_{*}({\Lambda_{\psi}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})
, this gives the action of
\langle{s}\rangle
as part of the
\widetilde{W}
-action specified in §
8.4.2
.

To compute the action of
s
on
\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})
, we first recall that the
\langle{s}\rangle
-action on
\nu_{L_{s}*}\mathbb{D}
is given by top homology classes in
\textup{H}^{\textup{BM}}_{4}({\textup{St}_{L_{s}}})
where
\textup{St}_{L_{s}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L_{s}}\times_{\mathcal{N}_{L_{s}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{L_{s}}
is the Steinberg variety of
L_{s}
. Specifically, a simple calculation shows that the action of
s-1
is given by the cycle class of the component
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\subset\textup{St}_{L_{s}}
(the preimage of the zero nilpotent orbit). Now we claim

Claim
.

The map
\epsilon_{s}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\to[\mathcal{N}_{L_{s}}/L_{s}]
is smooth.

The claim can be proved using the same infinitesimal calculations as in
[
9
, §2.9.2]
. We omit details here.

Using that
\epsilon_{s}
is smooth, the action of
s-1
on
\nu_{s*}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}
is also given by the cycle class of the preimage of
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})/L_{s}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\times_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}}\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
under the map

\displaystyle\sigma_{s}=(\widetilde{\epsilon}_{s},\widetilde{\epsilon}_{s}):\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\times_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}}\mathcal{M}_{\psi}\to\textup{St}_{L_{s}}/L_{s}.

Finally we observe that

\displaystyle\sigma_{s}^{-1}((\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})/L_{s})=\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I})\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I}).

Therefore the action of
s-1
on
\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})
is given by pull-push operators on the correspondence

(8.12)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 10.47919pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.47919pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 34.47919pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I})\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.37163pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d\pi_{s}\circ p_{12}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.4792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 302.36383pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d\pi_{s}\circ p_{13}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 331.4714pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 331.4714pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\psi}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Here
p_{12}
and
p_{13}
refer to the projections to the indicated factors, and
d\pi_{s}:\mathcal{M}_{\psi,\mathbf{P}_{s}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{P}_{s})}\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty},\mathbf{I})\to\mathcal{M}_{\psi}
is the differential of
\pi_{s}
. The correspondence (
8.12
) is the composition of the correspondence (
7.16
) and its transpose, therefore its action on
\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})
is given by the composition

(d\pi_{s})_{*}(\pi_{s,\natural})^{*}(\pi_{s,\natural})_{*}(d\pi_{s})^{*}\in\textup{End}(\textup{H}_{*}({\mathcal{M}_{\psi}})).

Here to make sense of
(d\pi_{s})^{*}
on homology we use Poincaré duality and closed restriction in compact support cohomology. Comparing with (
8.10
), we conclude that (
8.9
) holds.
∎

8.4.4.
Left
\widetilde{W}
-action on
\textup{H}^{\textup{BM}}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
from lattice and monodromy action

The action of the lattice
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
on
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}
has been explained in §
4.1.2
. To see the
W
-action, we need to vary
\psi_{1}
inside
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
. Let
\mathcal{X}\to\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
be the family of affine Springer fibers for all
\psi=t\psi_{1}
, where
\psi_{1}
varies in
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
. Using intersection with semi-infinite orbits as in
[
33
, §5, Prop. 1]
, we see that the restriction to any fiber induces an isomorphism

(8.13)

\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}}).

Now
\dot{w}\in N_{G}(T)
acts on
\mathcal{X}
sending
(\psi_{1},g\mathbf{I})
to
(w\psi_{1},\dot{w}g\mathbf{I})
. It induces a left
W
-action on
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}})
, hence on
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
via the isomorphism (
8.13
).

8.4.5.
Left
\widetilde{W}
-action on
K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi})

The action of the lattice part is induced by
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
-action on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
via the monoidal functor (
5.27
) and the
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{mon}}_{\infty}
-action on
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.

For the
W
-action, here we only give a sketch, as it is not used in the rest of the paper. We consider a version of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
with a varying
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
. Take the level subgroup
\mathbf{J}_{\infty}=\ker(\mathbf{G}_{\infty}^{1}\to\tau\mathfrak{g}\to\tau\mathfrak{t})
, where
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{t}
is the orthogonal projection under the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
.

Consider the stack

\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{\infty})\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}.

It carries an action of
\mathfrak{t}\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
, viewed as a constant additive group over
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
(where
\mathfrak{t}
is identified with
\mathbf{G}^{1}_{\infty}/\mathbf{J}_{\infty}
hence acts by changing levels). We have a surjection of group schemes over
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}

\epsilon:\mathfrak{t}\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}

sending
(X,\psi_{1})
to
(\langle{\psi_{1},X}\rangle,\psi_{1})
. Let
H
be the kernel of
\epsilon
. Consider the quotient

\mathcal{Z}=(\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{\infty})\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}})/H.

Then
\mathcal{Z}
carries the residual action of the constant group
\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
, hence an action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}
. Together with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{rot}}
-action we get an
Aff
-action
\mathcal{Z}
. We can form the Kirillov category
\Kir(\mathcal{Z})
. It contains a subcategory
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{G}}^{\textup{univ}}\subset\Kir(\mathcal{Z})
of objects that are constant along
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
, such that restriction to each fixed
\psi_{1}\in\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
gives a functor

i_{\psi_{1}}^{*}:\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{G}}^{\textup{univ}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\mathbf{G}}

that induces an isomorphism after passing to the Grothendieck groups. Similarly we may consider the universal version
\mathcal{D}^{\textup{univ}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
by taking the version
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{I}_{0})
of
\mathcal{Z}
with
\mathbf{I}_{0}
-level structure at
0
and by saturating the pullback of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{G}}^{\textup{univ}}
in
\Kir(\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{I}_{0}))
by the
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-action. Again the restriction functor

(8.14)

i_{\psi_{1}}^{*}:\mathcal{D}^{\textup{univ}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\psi}

induces an isomorphism on the Grothendieck groups.

The level group
\mathbf{J}_{\infty}
is normalized by
N_{G}(T)
, inducing an action of
N_{G}(T)
on
\textup{Bun}_{G}(\mathbf{J}_{\infty})
and on
\mathcal{Z}
that covers the
W
-action on
\mathfrak{t}^{*,\textup{rs}}
. It induces an action of
W
on
K(\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{G}}^{\textup{univ}})
, which can then be transported to an action of
W
on
K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi})
via the isomorphism induced by (
8.14
).

With the above construction, it can be checked that the map
CC
is equivariant under the left
\widetilde{W}
-actions.

8.4.6.

On the other hand, Theorem
8.3.1
induces an isomorphism on the level of Grothendieck groups

\Theta:K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))\cong K(\mathcal{D}_{\psi}).

The composition with
CC
gives a map

CC\circ\Theta:K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))\to\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}}).

8.4.7.
Left
\widetilde{W}
-action on
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))

For a translation element
\lambda\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
, it acts on
\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee})
by tensoring with
E(\lambda)\in\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
. On the other hand,
W=N_{G^{\vee}}(T^{\vee})/T^{\vee}
acts on the stack
T^{\vee}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
, inducing an action of
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))\cong K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))
. Together they give a left action of
\widetilde{W}
on
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))
.

8.4.8.
Right
\widetilde{W}
-action on
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))

The action of the coherent version of the affine Hecke category
D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{St}^{\vee})
, where
\textup{St}^{\vee}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}\times_{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}
, on
D^{b}\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee})
induces a right action of
K(\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{St}^{\vee}))
on
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))
. It follows from
[
34
]
(specializing the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant parameter to
1
) that we have an isomorphism of rings
K(\textup{Coh}^{G^{\vee}}(\textup{St}^{\vee}))\cong\mathbb{Z}[\widetilde{W}]
, hence a right action of
\widetilde{W}
on
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))
.

The following theorem is used in the work of Feng-Le Hung
[
23
]
on the Breuil-Mézard Conjecture.

8.4.9 Theorem
.

(1)

The maps
CC
and
CC\circ\Theta
are equivariant under the right
\widetilde{W}
-actions.

(2)

The map
CC
(hence
CC\circ\Theta
) is an isomorphism.

(3)

The isomorphism
CC\circ\Theta
is equivariant under the left
\widetilde{W}
-actions.

Proof.

(1) For
CC
, it follows from Proposition
8.4.3
. For
\Theta
, it follows from Theorem
8.3.1
by the compatibility with the affine Hecke category actions.

(2) Surjectivity: We can assume that the center of
G
is connected. If not consider a central isogeny
G\to\widetilde{G}
where
\widetilde{G}
has connected center. The flag variety
\textup{Fl}_{\widetilde{G}}
of
\widetilde{G}
contains
\textup{Fl}_{G}
as a union of connected components, and as such,
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi,\widetilde{G}}
(the counterpart of
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
for
\widetilde{G}
) to
\textup{Fl}_{G}
. The map
CC
splits as a sum along connected components. It is thus enough to show the surjectivity of
CC
for
\widetilde{G}
.

We use the compatibility of the map
CC
with the right
\widetilde{W}
action by (1).

Recall from Section
4.1.3
, the Hessenberg varieties
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
. By
[
27
]
the subvarieties
\textup{Fl}_{\psi}\cap(\mathbf{G}t^{\lambda}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I})
are successive affine bundles over
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
of maximal dimension. It follows that its closure is a union of components.

First note that
CC(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})=(d\pi_{\mathbf{G}})_{*}(\pi_{\mathbf{G},\natural})^{*}CC(\delta_{\lambda})
. The submodule of
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
generated by
CC(\mathcal{F}_{0})
under the Springer action contains the sum of all components over each
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
. If
w_{\lambda}
is the minimal length element in
Wt^{\lambda}\subset\widetilde{W}
, the Hessenberg variety
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(\lambda)
is connected if and only if
w_{\lambda}
lies in the fundamental box. These give
\frac{|W|}{|Z(G^{sc})|}
many components which generate
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
up to the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action together with the action of the length
0
elements in the right
\widetilde{W}
-action, as
G
has connected center.

As the map
CC
is compatible with the right
\widetilde{W}
-actions, and the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action sends
CC(\mathcal{F}_{0})
to
CC(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda})
the surjectivity follows as these generate under the right
\widetilde{W}
-action. Further as
CC
surjective and compatible with the right
\widetilde{W}
-action and the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action on generators, it follows that it is compatible with the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action on all elements.

To show injectivity of
CC
, it suffices to show that
CC\circ\Theta
is injective. By construction, the action of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and the
\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee})
action are compatible under the equivalence of categories in Theorem
8.3.1
. It follows that
\Theta
is compatible with the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action. By the compatibility of
CC
with the actions, it then follows that
CC\circ\Theta
is compatible with the right
\widetilde{W}
-action and the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action. Both
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))
and
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi}})
are free
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)]\cong K(\textup{Rep}(T^{\vee}))
-modules of rank equal to
|W|
. Since
CC\circ\Theta
is surjective, it also has to be injective.

(3) By (2)
\Theta
and
CC\circ\Theta
are compatible with the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
-action.

It remains to show the compatibility of the left
W
-action. To show this, we again reduce to the case of
G
having connected center. In this case by (1) both sides are generated under the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
- and right
\widetilde{W}
-actions by a single element
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}]\in K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))
and
CC(\mathcal{F}_{0})=CC\circ\Theta([\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}])\in\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi},\mathbb{Z}})
. By compatibility of
CC\circ\Theta
with the left
\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
- and right
\widetilde{W}
-actions, it is enough to check the compatibility of the
W
action on
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}]
and
CC(\mathcal{F}_{0})
. Both these elements are invariant under the left
W
-action and the compatibility follows.
∎

8.4.10.
Other blocks of small quantum group

Let
W_{P}\subset W\subset\widetilde{W}
a parabolic subgroup and denote by
\mathcal{B}_{P}^{\vee}
the corresponding partial flag variety for
G^{\vee}
and
\textup{Fl}^{P}
the partial affine flag variety for
G
. Then using the right
\widetilde{W}
action, we have
K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{\vee}))^{W_{P}}\cong K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))^{W_{P}}\cong K(\textup{Coh}^{T^{\vee}}(\mathcal{B}_{P}^{\vee}))
and
\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}_{\psi}})^{W_{P}}\cong\textup{H}_{\textup{top}}({\textup{Fl}^{P}_{\psi}})
, where
\textup{Fl}^{P}_{\psi}
denotes the Spaltenstein fiber of
\psi
in the partial affine flag variety
\textup{Fl}^{P}
.

Appendix A
Specialization and microlocalization

In this appendix we review the Kashiwara-Schapira microlocalization construction in the algebro-geometric context over an algebraically closed field of any characteristic. We also prove formulas for computing the specialization and microlocalization in the situation with a contracting
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.

A.1.
Specialization

A.1.1.
Monodromic sheaves

Let
X
be a stack with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Following Verdier
[
50
, Def.3.1 and §5]
, an object
\mathcal{F}\in D(X)
is called
monodromic
if for any
x\in X
with the action map
a_{x}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X
(sending
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
to
t\cdot x
), the pullback
a_{x}^{*}\mathcal{F}
has tame locally constant cohomology sheaves. Let
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(X)\subset D(X)
or
D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)\subset D(X)
denote the full subcategory of monodromic objects.

A.1.2.
Specialization

Suppose
Z\subset X
is a closed subscheme, Verdier
[
50
, §8]
has defined the specialization functor

\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}:D(X)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}X)

where
N_{Z}X
is the normal cone of
Z
in
X
with the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action fiberwise over
Z
. We may extend this definition where
Z
is locally closed in
X
. In this case, let
j:U\hookrightarrow X
be an open neighborhood of
Z
in which
Z
is closed. Define the normal cone
N_{Z}X
to be
N_{Z}U
(which is clearly independent of the choice of
U
). Then define
\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}
to be the composition

\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}:D(X)\xrightarrow{j^{*}}D(U)\xrightarrow{\textup{Sp}_{Z/U}}D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}U)=D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}X).

Below we assume
Z\subset X
is locally closed.

Recall one key property of
\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}

[
50
, §8, (SP5)]
: let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion and
i_{0}:Z\hookrightarrow N_{Z}X
be the inclusion of the zero section, then the natural transformations of functors
D(X)\to D(Z)

(A.1)

i_{0}^{*}\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}\to i^{*},\quad i^{!}\to i_{0}^{!}\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}

are isomorphisms.

Now suppose
Z\subset Y\subset X
, where
Y
is another closed subscheme of
X
. Suppose there is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on a Zariski neighborhood
U
of
Y
that contracts
U
to
Y
(and in particular fixes
Y
pointwise). We denote this
\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}
(for vertical). Let
k:Y\hookrightarrow U
and
k_{0}:N_{Z}Y\hookrightarrow N_{Z}U
be the inclusions.

A.1.3 Lemma
.

The natural transformation of functors
D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}\textup{-mon}}(U)\to D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(N_{Z}Y)
(monodromic both under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}
-action and the scaling action in the fiber direction)

k_{0}^{*}\circ\textup{Sp}_{Z/U}\to\textup{Sp}_{Z/Y}\circ k^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\pi:U\to Y
be the contraction map under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}
-action, and let
\pi_{0}:N_{Z}U\to N_{Z}Y
be the induced contraction on the normal cones. Since
k
is a section of
\pi
, we have a natural transformation

c:\pi_{*}\to\pi_{*}k_{*}k^{*}=k^{*}.

By the contraction principle, the above map is an isomorphism when applied to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}
-monodromic sheaves on
U
. Similarly, we have a natural transformation

c_{0}:\pi_{0*}\to\pi_{0*}k_{0*}k^{*}_{0}=k_{0}^{*}

which is an isomorphism when applied to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}
-monodromic sheaves on
N_{Z}U
.
A diagram chase gives a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\textup{Sp}_{Z/Y}\circ\pi_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\pi_{0*}\circ\textup{Sp}_{Z/U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{0}}
\textstyle{\textup{Sp}_{Z/Y}\circ k^{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces k_{0}^{*}\circ\textup{Sp}_{Z/U}}

When applied to objects from
D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{v}\textup{-mon}}(U)
, the vertical maps are isomorphisms, therefore the horizontal maps are inverse isomorphisms.
∎

A.2.
Monodromic vanishing cycles

Let
Y
be a stack over
k
and
X=Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, equipped with an
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action only on the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-factor with nonzero weight. Let
p:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
\pi:X\to Y
be the projections.

For
t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}(k)
, let
i_{t}:Y\cong p^{-1}(t)\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion map. The unit of the adjunction
\textup{id}\to i_{t*}i_{t}^{*}
and the counit
i_{t!}i_{t}^{!}\to\textup{id}
induce natural transformations

\alpha_{t}:\pi_{*}\to\pi_{*}i_{t*}i_{t}^{*}=i_{t}^{*},\quad\beta_{t}:i_{t}^{!}=\pi_{!}i_{t!}i_{t}^{!}\to\pi_{!}.

For
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
, by the contraction principle,
\alpha_{0}
and
\beta_{0}
applied to
\mathcal{F}
are isomorphisms

(A.2)

\alpha_{0}:\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{0}^{*}\mathcal{F},\quad\beta_{0}:i_{0}^{!}\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{!}\mathcal{F}.

We
define
the functor

\phi:D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)\to D(Y)

to fit into the distinguished triangle (using dg enhancements of the relevant triangulated categories)

(A.3)

\phi(\mathcal{F})\to i^{*}_{0}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}}i^{*}_{1}\mathcal{F}\to.

We call
\phi
the
monodromic vanishing cycles
functor.

A.2.1.
Relation with usual vanishing cycles

We now relate
\phi
with the usual vanishing cycles functor for the projection
p:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Choose a geometric generic point
\overline{\eta}
of the henselization of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
at
0
, and a specialzation
\overline{\eta}\rightsquigarrow 0
. The nearby and vanishing cycles
functors are defined

\Psi_{p},\Phi_{p}:D(X)\to D(Y).

Recall that they fit into distinguished triangles that are related to one another by Verdier duality

\displaystyle\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})[-1]\xrightarrow{\textup{can}}\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\to i_{0}^{*}\mathcal{F}\to

\displaystyle i_{0}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\textup{var}}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})[1]\to

Now choose an étale path between
\overline{\eta}
and
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. For
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
, the chosen path give isomorphisms

(A.4)

\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\cong i_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}.

Using the isomorphism (
A.4
), the rotations of the triangles above become

\displaystyle\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\to i_{0}^{*}\mathcal{F}\to i^{*}_{1}\mathcal{F}\to,

\displaystyle i^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}\to i_{0}^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\to.

Comparing the first isomorphism above with the definition of
\phi(\mathcal{F})
, we get a functorial isomorphism

\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\cong\phi(\mathcal{F}),\quad\mbox{ for }\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)

which depends on the choice of the étale path between
1
and
\overline{\eta}
. We also get a functorial distinguished triangle

(A.5)

i^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\beta_{0}^{-1}\circ\beta_{1}}i^{!}_{0}\mathcal{F}\to\phi(\mathcal{F})\to.

A.3.
Monodromic Fourier transform

Let
V
be a vector bundle of rank
r
over a stack
S
and let
V^{\vee}
be the dual vector bundle. Consider the diagram

(A.6)

\textstyle{V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{p}^{\vee}=(p^{\vee},\textup{ev})}
\textstyle{V}
\textstyle{V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}

where
\textup{ev}:V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the pairing between the two vector bundles. We define the Fourier transform functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{FT}:D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V^{\vee})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\phi(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})[r].

Here
\phi:D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\textup{-mon}}(V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V^{\vee})
is the monodromic vanishing cycles functor defined in §
A.2
(the source categoery consists of sheaf monodromic with respect to the two dilation
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-actions on individual factors of
V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
).

A.3.1.
Wang’s Fourier transform

In
[
51
]
J.Wang defines a Fourier transform using Fourier kernel
u_{*}E
, where
u:\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
. More precisely, Wang’s Fourier transform is the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}:D(V)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle D(V^{\vee})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle p^{\vee}_{!}(p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\textup{ev}^{*}u_{*}E)[r].

Below we explain the relation between
\mathrm{FT}
and
\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}
.

A.3.2 Lemma
.

There is a canonical isomorphism between
\mathrm{FT}
and the restriction of
\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}
to
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V)
.

Proof.

Letting
\delta_{1}:\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the inclusion, and using the distinguished triangle
\delta_{1*}\delta^{!}_{1}E\to\underline{E}\to u_{*}E\to
,
\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}[-r]
fits into a distinguished triangle

(A.7)

p^{\vee}_{!}(p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\textup{ev}^{*}\delta_{1*}\delta^{!}_{1}E)\xrightarrow{\gamma}p^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\to\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}(\mathcal{F})[-r]\to.

On the other hand, letting
\pi:V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to V^{\vee}
be the projection, and
i_{1}:V^{\vee}\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the inclusion, using (
A.5
),
\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F})[-r]=\phi(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})
fits into a distinguished triangle

(A.8)

i_{1}^{!}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\beta_{1}}\pi_{!}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\to\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F})[-r]\to.

We have a canonical identification

(A.9)

\pi_{!}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}=p^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}

We also have isomorphisms by the projection formula

(A.10)

p^{\vee}_{!}(p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\textup{ev}^{*}\delta_{1*}\delta^{!}_{1}E)=\pi_{!}(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\textup{pr}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{*}\delta_{1*}\delta^{!}_{1}E)\cong i_{1}^{*}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\delta^{!}_{1}E

Now we have a natural map

(A.11)

i_{1}^{*}(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})\otimes\delta^{!}_{1}E\to i_{1}^{!}(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})

that is an isomorphism since
\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\in D(V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
is monodromic with respect to the scaling action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. The isomorphisms (
A.10
) an (
A.11
) give

(A.12)

p^{\vee}_{!}(p^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\textup{ev}^{*}\delta_{1*}\delta^{!}_{1}E)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{1}^{!}(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}).

The isomorphisms (
A.12
) and (
A.9
