) intertwine the maps
\gamma
and
\beta_{1}
in (
A.7
) and (
A.8
). We conclude with a functorial isomorphism
\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F})\cong\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}(\mathcal{F})
.
∎

Because
\mathrm{FT}
coincides with
\mathrm{FT}_{\textup{Wang}}
on monodromic sheaves, Wang’s theorem
[
51
, Theorem 3.8]
implies:

A.3.3 Corollary
.

The functor
\mathrm{FT}
is an equivalence.

A.3.4 Lemma
.

Let
\xi:S\to V^{\vee}
be a section. Then we have a canonical isomorphism for
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V)

i^{*}_{\xi}\mathrm{FT}(\mathcal{F})\cong\phi(\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\mathcal{F})[r]

where
\widetilde{\xi}:V\to S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the map given by fiberwise pairing with
\xi
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{i}_{\xi}:S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\xrightarrow{\xi\times\textup{id}}V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. We use
i_{t}
to denote both the inclusion
V^{\vee}=V^{\vee}\times\{t\}\hookrightarrow V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
and the inclusion
S=S\times\{t\}\hookrightarrow S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, for
t=0,1
. Note that
\phi(\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\mathcal{F})[r]\cong\phi(\widetilde{i}_{\xi}^{*}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})[r]
. The natural transformation

(A.13)

u:i_{\xi}^{*}\phi\to\phi\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\xi}

then induces a map
i^{*}_{\xi}\mathrm{FT}\to\phi\widetilde{\xi}_{!}[r]
. It suffices to show that
u
is an isomorphism when applied to any object in
D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\textup{-mon}}(V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
. Using the defining triangle (
A.3
) for
\phi
(with
\pi_{*}
replaced by
i_{0}^{*}
using the isomorphism
\alpha_{0}
in (
A.2
)), the map
u
fits into a commutative diagram of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{i_{\xi}^{*}\phi\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{\xi}i^{*}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{0}^{-1}}
\textstyle{i_{\xi}^{*}i_{1}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{1}}
\textstyle{\phi(\widetilde{i}^{*}_{\xi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{0}^{*}\widetilde{i}_{\xi}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{0}^{-1}}
\textstyle{i_{1}^{*}\widetilde{i}_{\xi}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The maps
u_{0}
and
u_{1}
are the isomorphisms given by the equality of maps
i_{t}i_{\xi}=\widetilde{i}_{\xi}i_{t}:S\times\{t\}\xrightarrow{\xi\times i_{t}}V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
for
t=0,1
. Therefore
u
is an isomorphism.
∎

A.3.5 Lemma
.

Let
V
and
U
be vector bundles over
S
of constant ranks
r_{V}
and
r_{U}
respectively. Let
t:V\to U
be a linear map (but not necessarily of the same rank at every geometric point). Let
t^{\vee}:U^{\vee}\to V^{\vee}
be the dual map. There is a natural isomorphism of functors

(t^{\vee})^{*}\circ\mathrm{FT}_{V}\cong\mathrm{FT}_{U}\circ t_{!}[r_{V}-r_{U}]:D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(U^{\vee}).

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{t}^{\vee}=t^{\vee}\times\textup{id}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}:U^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. We use the notations
p,\widetilde{p}^{\vee}
as in the diagram (
A.6
). By definition we have

(t^{\vee})^{*}\circ\mathrm{FT}_{V}=(t^{\vee})^{*}\circ\phi(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*})[r_{V}]

A diagram chase using the pairing
V\times_{S}U^{\vee}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
together with proper base change shows that

\mathrm{FT}_{U}\circ t_{!}\cong\phi((\widetilde{t}^{\vee})^{*}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*})[r_{U}].

Here
\phi
denotes the monodromic vanishing cycles with respect to
U^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Comparing the two formulas it suffices to show that the natural transformation

(t^{\vee})^{*}\circ\phi\to\phi\circ(\widetilde{t}^{\vee})^{*}:D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\textup{-mon}}(V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(U^{\vee})

is an isomorphism.
The proof of this last fact is the same as the proof that (
A.13
) is an isomorphism, using the definition that
\phi
is the cone of the cospecialization maps between restrictions to the
0
and
1
fibers.
∎

A.3.6.
Alternative definition

Here is an alternative definition of
\mathrm{FT}
: for
\mathcal{F}\in D(V)
, consider the vanishing cycles of
p^{*}\mathcal{F}
with respect to the function
\textup{ev}:V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

A.3.7 Lemma
.

For any
\mathcal{F}\in D(V)
,
\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is concentrated on
0_{S}\times_{S}V^{\vee}
, where
0_{S}\subset V
denotes the zero section. Moreover,
\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is monodromic with respect to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-scaling action on
V^{\vee}
.

Proof.

The map
\widetilde{p}^{\prime}=(p,\textup{ev}):(V-0_{S})\times_{S}V^{\vee}\to(V-0_{S})\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is smooth. Therefore we have an isomorphism
\widetilde{p}^{\prime*}_{0}\Phi_{\textup{pr}_{2}}(\mathcal{K})\cong\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(\widetilde{p}^{\prime*}\mathcal{K})
for any
\mathcal{K}\in D((V-0_{S})\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
(here
\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{0}
is the restriction of
\widetilde{p}^{\prime}
to
\textup{ev}^{-1}(0)
, and
\textup{pr}_{2}:(V-0_{S})\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the projection). For
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{F}|_{V-0_{S}}\boxtimes E
, we have
\Phi_{\textup{pr}_{2}}(\mathcal{K})=0
, hence
\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(\widetilde{p}^{\prime*}\mathcal{K})=\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(\mathcal{F}|_{V-0_{S}}\boxtimes E)=0
, i.e.,
\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is zero when restricted to
(V-0_{S})\times_{S}V^{\vee}
.

Now the map
\textup{ev}:V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant with respect to the scaling actions on
V^{\vee}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and
p^{*}\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant on
V\times_{S}V^{\vee}
. This implies the vanishing cycle
\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic by
[
50
, Prop. 7.1]

∎

We may then define

\displaystyle\mathrm{FT}^{\prime}:D(V)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V^{\vee})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})[r],

where we view the right side as an object in
D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V^{\vee})
by Lemma
A.3.7
.

A.3.8 Proposition
.

For a choice of an étale path between
\overline{\eta}
and
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, there is a canonical isomorphism of functors
\mathrm{FT}^{\prime}|_{D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{FT}:D(V)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(V^{\vee})
.

Proof.

With the choice of étale path on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we can identify the monodromic vanishing cycles on
V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
in the definition of
\mathrm{FT}
with the usual vanishing cycles
\Phi_{\textup{pr}}
, where
\textup{pr}:V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the projection. Functoriality of vanishing cycles with respect to pushforward along
\widetilde{p}^{\vee}:V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\to V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
gives a natural transformation

(A.14)

(p_{0}^{\vee})_{!}\Phi_{\textup{ev}}\to\Phi_{\textup{pr}}\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}.

Here
p_{0}^{\vee}:\textup{ev}^{-1}(0)\to V^{\vee}
is the projection. We claim that (
A.14
) is an isomorphism when evaluated on
p^{*}\mathcal{F}
, for any
\mathcal{F}\in D(V)
.

Let
P=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{S}\oplus V)
, considered as compactifications of
V
over
S
in the obvious way. Let
W\subset P\times_{S}V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the subscheme defined in homogeneous coordinates
[x_{0},x]\in P
(where
x_{+}\in V
),
y\in V^{\vee}
and
a\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
by

\langle{x,y}\rangle=ax_{0}.

Let
q:W\to P
(which extends
p
),
q^{\vee}:W\to V^{\vee}
(extending
p^{\vee}
) and
e:W\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
(extending
ev
) be the projections.
We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{V\times_{S}V^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{p}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{q}^{\vee}=(q^{\vee},e)}
\textstyle{V^{\vee}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}

Let
\mathcal{K}=j_{!}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})\cong q^{*}(\iota_{!}\mathcal{F})\in D(W^{\prime})
, where
\iota:V\hookrightarrow P
is the inclusion. Let
q^{\vee}_{0}:e^{-1}(0)\subset W\to V^{\vee}
be the restriction of
\widetilde{q}^{\vee}
to the zero fiber of
e
. Since
\widetilde{q}^{\vee}
is proper, we have

(q^{\vee}_{0})!\Phi_{e}(\mathcal{K})\cong\Phi_{\textup{pr}}(\widetilde{q}^{\vee}_{!}\mathcal{K})\cong\Phi_{\textup{pr}}(\widetilde{p}^{\vee}_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}).

It remains to show that the natural map

(A.15)

(p_{0}^{\vee})_{!}\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})\to(q^{\vee}_{0})_{!}\Phi_{e}(j_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})=(q^{\vee}_{0})_{!}\Phi_{\textup{ev}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})

is an isomorphism. Let
j_{0}:\textup{ev}^{-1}(0)\hookrightarrow e^{-1}(0)
be the restriction of the
j
over the zero locus of
e
. The map (
A.15
) is induced from the natural map

(A.16)

(j_{0})_{!}\Phi_{\textup{ev}}(p^{*}\mathcal{F})\to\Phi_{e}(j_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})

by applying
(\widetilde{q}^{\vee}_{0})_{!}
. Thus it suffices to show that (
A.16
) is an isomorphism. Now the same argument of Lemma
A.3.7
applied to
e:W^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
and the sheaf
j_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong q^{*}\iota_{!}\mathcal{F}
shows that
\Phi_{e}(j_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is supported on the non-smooth locus of the map
\widetilde{q}=(q,e):W^{\prime}\to P\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which is still
0_{S}\times_{S}V^{\vee}\times\{0\}\subset W
. In particular, the support of
\Phi_{e}(j_{!}p^{*}\mathcal{F})
is contained in
\textup{ev}^{-1}(0)\subset V\times_{S}V^{\vee}
. This implies (
A.16
) is an isomorphism and finishes the proof.
∎

A.4.
Microlocalization

In the case
X
is a smooth scheme and
Z\subset X
a smooth locally closed subscheme,
N_{Z}X
is a vector bundle over
Z
and its dual bundle is the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{Z}X
. The microlocalization functor along
Z

\mu_{Z/X}:D(X)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T^{*}_{Z}X)

is defined as the composition

\mu_{Z/X}:D(X)\xrightarrow{\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}}D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}X)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FT}}D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T^{*}_{Z}X)

where
\mathrm{FT}:D^{\mathrm{mon}}(N_{Z}X)\to D^{\mathrm{mon}}(T^{*}_{Z}X)
is the monodromic Fourier transform with base
Z
(see §
A.3
) for sheaves monodromic with respect to the dilation
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the fibers.

A.4.1 Lemma
.

Let
X
be a scheme smooth over
k
and
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
be a closed subscheme. Suppose
Y
carries an action of an algebraic group
H
and
Z
is an
H
-orbit through
z\in Z
. Let
s:S\hookrightarrow X
be a transversal slice to
Z
at
z
. In particular,
T_{z}S
(resp.
T^{*}_{z}S
) is canonically identified with the fiber
(N_{Z}X)_{z}
(resp.
(T^{*}_{Z}X)_{z}
).
Then for any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{H}(Y)
we have a canonical isomorphism

(A.17)

\mu_{Z/X}(i_{!}\mathcal{F})|_{T^{*}_{z}S}\cong\mu_{\{z\}/S}(s^{*}i_{!}\mathcal{F})\in D(T^{*}_{z}S).

Proof.

Let
S^{Y}=\Sigma\cap Y
with inclusion
s_{Y}:S^{Y}\hookrightarrow Y
. Let
M_{Z}S^{Y}
be the total space of the deformation to the normal for the pair
Z\hookrightarrow S^{Y}
. Similarly we have
M_{Z}Y
.
We have a diagram of inclusions of normal cones

\textstyle{N_{z}(S^{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{Y}}
\textstyle{N_{Z}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{T_{z}S=N_{z}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{N_{Z}X=T_{Z}X}

The fact that
S
is transversal to
Z
implies that the map
S^{Y}\to[H\backslash Y]
is smooth. Therefore it induces a smooth map over
\mathbb{A}^{1}

M_{Z}(S^{Y})\to[H\backslash M_{Z}Y].

Since nearby cycles commute with smooth pullback, we conclude that canonical map

\sigma_{Y}^{*}\textup{Sp}_{Z/Y}(\mathcal{F})\to\textup{Sp}_{\{z\}/S^{Y}}(s^{*}_{Y}\mathcal{F})\in D_{H}(N_{z}(S^{Y}))

is an isomorphism. Applying
\iota_{S*}
to both sides, and using that specialization commutes with closed embeddings, we get a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\textup{Sp}_{Z/X}(i_{!}\mathcal{F})\to\textup{Sp}_{\{z\}/S}(s^{*}i_{!}\mathcal{F})\in D(T_{z}S).

Finally we apply Fourier transform on both sides, and using the fact that Fourier transform commutes with arbitrary base change to conclude (
A.17
).
∎

A.4.2.

Consider the following situation. Let
X
be a smooth scheme with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action contracting to its fixed point locus
Z=X^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)
be monodromic with respect to the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Let
f:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
be a regular function of weight
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, i.e.,
f(t\cdot x)=t^{w}f(x)
or
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
x\in X
. Let
c:X\to Z
be the contraction map
x\mapsto\lim_{s\to 0}s\cdot x
and
\widetilde{f}=(c,f):X\to Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. On the other hand,
\xi:=df|_{Z}
gives a section of the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{Z}X\to Z
.

A.4.3 Proposition
.

Suppose the weight
w
of
f
is the smallest among the weights of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on the fibers of the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{Z}X
. Then there is a functorial isomorphism in
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(X)

\xi^{*}\mu_{Z/X}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\phi(\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F})[\dim X-\dim Z].

Here
\phi:D^{{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\to D(Z)
is the monodromic vanishing cycles functor defined in §
A.2
.

Proof.

Let
p:M_{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
be the total space of the deformation from
X
to
V:=N_{Z}X
, where
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
is a copy of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with coordinate
t
. By construction,
M_{X}
is equipped with a map
a:M_{X}\to X
such that over
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we have a canonical isomorphism
(p,a):M^{\circ}_{X}:=p^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}\times X
.

We have a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
M_{X}
such that
p
has
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight
1
and
a
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant. To distinguish various
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-actions, we denote the above
\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}
, and denote the original
\mathbb{G}_{m}
that acts on
X
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
. The
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action on
X
induces a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action on
M_{X}
such that
p
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-invariant.

We denote the target of the function
f
by
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
. The function
M^{\circ}_{X}\to X\xrightarrow{f}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
extends to a function
f_{M}:M_{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
whose restriction to the zero fiber is
\xi:V\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
. We also have a projection
c\circ a:M_{X}\to Z
whose restriction to
V
is the projection
q:V=N_{Z}X\to Z
. Let
\widetilde{f}_{M}=(c\circ a,f_{M}):M_{X}\to Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
whose restriction to
V
is
\widetilde{\xi}=(q,\xi):V=N_{Z}X\to Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
. Together with
p
we get a morphism over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}

\textstyle{M_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F=(p,\widetilde{f}_{M})}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}}

We have a natural transformation

(A.18)

\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\Psi_{p}\to\Psi_{\textup{pr}_{1}}F_{!}.

Apply both sides of the above to
a^{*}\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}}(M_{X})
. Using that
F_{!}a^{*}\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, we may canonically identify
\Psi_{\textup{pr}_{1}}F_{!}a^{*}\mathcal{F}
with the restriction
(F_{!}a^{*}\mathcal{F})|_{\{1\}\times Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}}\cong\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}
. On the other hand we have
\Psi_{p}a^{*}\mathcal{F}=\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\in D(N_{Z}X)
by definition. Therefore (
A.18
) gives a functorial map

u:\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\to\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}\textup{-mon}}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}).

Applying the monodromic vanishing cycles
\phi
from §
A.2
we get a functorial map

u_{\phi}:\phi(\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F}))\to\phi(\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F})

By Lemma
A.3.4
we have a canonical isomorphism

\xi^{*}\mu_{Z/X}(\mathcal{F})=\xi^{*}\mathrm{FT}(\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F}))\cong\phi(\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F}))[n]

where
n=\dim X-\dim Z
. It remains to show that
u_{\phi}
is an isomorphism for all
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}\textup{-mon}}(X)
.

For
s\in\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}(k)
, let
i_{s}:Z\times\{s\}\hookrightarrow Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
be the inclusion. Let
\pi:Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}\to Z
be the projection. By the functorial distinguished triangle (
A.5
), the map
u_{\phi}
fits into a map of distinguished triangles in
D(Z)

\textstyle{i_{1}^{!}\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{1}}
\textstyle{\pi_{!}\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\pi}}
\textstyle{\phi(\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\phi}}
\textstyle{i_{1}^{!}\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{!}\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi(\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F})}

Here
u_{1}
and
u_{\pi}
are obtained from
u
by applying
i_{1}^{!}
and
\pi_{!}
. To show
u_{\phi}
is an isomorphism, it suffices to show
u_{1}
and
u_{\pi}
are.

First we show
u_{\pi}
is an isomorphism. We have
\pi\circ\widetilde{\xi}=q:V\to Z
and
\pi\circ\widetilde{f}=c:X\to Z
. Therefore
u_{\pi}:q_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\to c_{!}\mathcal{F}
. Let
i_{Z,V}:Z\hookrightarrow V
and
i_{Z,X}:Z\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusions. Using the contraction principle for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-actions on
X
and on
V
, we have
q_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\cong i_{Z,V}^{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})
and
c_{!}\mathcal{F}\cong i_{Z,X}^{!}\mathcal{F}
. The map
u_{\pi}
then gets identified with the inverse of the isomorphism
i_{Z,X}^{!}\mathcal{F}\cong i_{Z,V}^{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})
in (
A.1
). This shows
u_{\pi}
is an isomorphism.

Next we show
u_{1}
is an isomorphism. Let
X_{1}=f^{-1}(1)
and
V_{1}=\xi^{-1}(1)
. Let
q_{1}:V_{1}\to Z
be the restriction of the projection
q:V\to Z
, and
c_{1}:X_{1}\to Z
be the restrictions of the contraction map
c:X\to Z
. Using that
\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})
and
\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}
are both
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic with respect to the weight
w>0
action on the
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
-factor of
Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
, we may rewrite
i_{1}^{!}
as
i^{*}_{1}[-2](-1)
, and hence it suffices to show that

u^{\prime}_{1}:i_{1}^{*}\widetilde{\xi}_{!}\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})\to i_{1}^{*}\widetilde{f}_{!}\mathcal{F}

is an isomorphism. Using proper base change,
u^{\prime}_{1}
gets identified with the map

u^{\prime}_{1}:q_{1!}(\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})|_{V_{1}})\to c_{1!}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{1}}).

The conormal space
T^{*}_{Z}X=V^{*}
is decomposed into a direct sum of weight subbundles under
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
with positive weights by the contracting assumption. Since our question is Zariski local over
Z
, we may assume each
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-weight subbundle of
T^{*}_{Z}X
is a trivial vector bundle over
Z
. Fix a trivialization
N_{Z}X\cong\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}
where the coordinates
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}
are weight vectors with
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-weights
w_{1},w_{2},\cdots,w_{n}>0
. We may assume
\xi=\xi_{1}
so that
w_{1}=w
. Using the positivity of the weights of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action on
\mathcal{O}(X)
, each
\xi_{i}
lifts uniquely to a regular function
x_{i}\in\mathcal{O}(X)
of weight
w_{i}
. In particular,
x_{1}=f
. Using
x_{i}
as coordinates we get an isomorphism
X\cong\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}
. We may identify
M_{X}
with
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}
, so that
p
becomes the first projection. The map
a:M_{X}\to X
takes the form

\displaystyle a:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}\cong M_{X}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle X\cong\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}

\displaystyle(t,z,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n})

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(z,t\xi_{1},\cdots,t\xi_{n}).

The map
a
is invariant under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}
-action on
M_{X}
which takes the form

s\cdot(t,z,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n})=(st,z,s^{-1}\xi_{1},\cdots,s^{-1}\xi_{n}),\quad z\in Z.

The
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action on
M_{X}
takes the form

s\cdot(t,z,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n})=(t,z,s^{w_{1}}\xi_{1},s^{w_{2}}\xi_{2},\cdots,s^{w_{n}}\xi_{n}).

Combining the two actions, we consider another
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
M_{X}
given by

(A.19)

s\star(t,z,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n})=(s^{w}t,z,\xi_{1},s^{w_{2}-w}\xi_{2},\cdots,s^{w_{n}-w}\xi_{n}).

The monodromicity of
a^{*}\mathcal{F}
under both the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\textup{dil}}
-action and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action implies that
a^{*}\mathcal{F}
is also monodromic under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action (
A.19
).

Let
M_{1}\subset M_{X}
be the subscheme defined by the equation
\xi_{1}=1
. Let
p_{1}:M_{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
be the restriction of
p
. Then
p_{1}^{-1}(1)=X_{1}
and
p_{1}^{-1}(0)=V_{1}
. The
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action (
A.19
) restricts to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
M_{1}
. Since
w_{i}\geq w>0
for all
i
, this action contracts to a subspace of the
0
-fiber
V_{1}
, and the function
p_{1}
has positive weight
w
. Applying Lemma
A.4.4
to the scheme
M_{1}
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given in (
A.19
), the function
p_{1}:M_{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant map
\Pi_{1}:M_{1}\to Z
and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic sheaf
(a^{*}\mathcal{F})|_{M_{1}}
, we get an isomorphism

u^{\prime\prime}_{1}:q_{1!}\Psi_{p_{1}}((a^{*}\mathcal{F})|_{M_{1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}c_{1!}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{1}}).

By examining the construction of the isomorphisms in Lemma
A.4.4
,
u^{\prime}_{1}
is the composition

q_{1!}(\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})|_{V_{1}})\xrightarrow{v_{1}}q_{1!}\Psi_{p_{s}}((a^{*}\mathcal{F})|_{M_{1}})\xrightarrow{u^{\prime\prime}_{1}}c_{1!}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{1}}).

Here the first map is induced from the map

v_{1}:\textup{Sp}(\mathcal{F})|_{V_{1}}=(\Psi_{p}(a^{*}\mathcal{F}))|_{V_{1}}\to\Psi_{p_{1}}((a^{*}\mathcal{F})|_{M_{1}})

which is an isomorphism because
a^{*}\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-monodromic, and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{X}
-action on
M_{X}
scales the map to
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{f}
by weight
w>0
and leaves the map
p
invariant. Therefore
u^{\prime}_{1}=u^{\prime\prime}_{1}\circ v_{1}
is also an isomorphism. This completes the proof.
∎

A.4.4 Lemma
.

Let
M
be a scheme with a contracting
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Let
p:M\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with positive weight. Let
\pi:M\to N
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant map. Let
\pi_{t}:M_{t}=p^{-1}(t)\to N
and
\pi_{\overline{\eta}}:M_{\overline{\eta}}=p^{-1}(\overline{\eta})\to N_{\overline{\eta}}:=N\times\overline{\eta}
be the restrictions of
\pi
, where
\overline{\eta}
is a geometric generic point of the hensalization of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
at
0
. Then there are functorial isomorphisms for
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\textup{-mon}}(p^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m}))

(A.20)

\pi_{\overline{\eta}*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0*}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F}),\quad\pi_{0!}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{\overline{\eta}!}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}.

Here
\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}=\mathcal{F}|_{M_{\overline{\eta}}}
and
\Psi_{p}
denotes the nearby cycles towards the zero fiber
X_{0}
(defined using the same geometric generic point
\overline{\eta}
). The two sides of the isomorphisms take values in
D(N_{\overline{\eta}})
and
D(N)
respectively, which are identified.

In particular, if we choose an étale path between
1
and
\overline{\eta}
, we get functorial isomorphisms

\pi_{1*}(\mathcal{F}|_{M_{1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{0*}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F}),\quad\pi_{0!}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{1!}(\mathcal{F}|_{M_{1}}).

Proof.

By the fact that
\Psi_{p}
commutes with Verdier duality, it suffices to prove the first isomorphism in (
A.20
). By the contracting assumption,
Z=M^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\subset M_{0}
. Let
D
be the henselization of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
at
0
.
Let
\overline{j}:M_{\overline{\eta}}\to M_{D}=M\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}D
. Let
i:M_{0}\hookrightarrow M_{D}
be the inclusion. By definition,
\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})=i^{*}\overline{j}_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}
. Let
\pi_{Z}:Z\to N
be the restriction of
\pi
. Using the contracting principle for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic sheaf
\Psi_{p}\mathcal{F}
on
M_{0}
, we see

(A.21)

\pi_{0*}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{Z*}i_{Z}^{*}\Psi_{p}(\mathcal{F})\cong\pi_{Z*}((\overline{j}_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}})|_{Z}).

Let
\pi_{D}:M_{D}\to N
be induced from
\pi
. Apply the contraction principle to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic sheaf
\overline{j}_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}
on
M_{D}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(A.22)

\pi_{\overline{\eta}*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}=\pi_{D*}\overline{j}_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{Z*}((\overline{j}_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\eta}})|_{Z})

under the identification
D(N)\cong D(N_{\overline{\eta}})
.
Combining (
A.21
) and (
A.22
) we get the desired isomorphism (
A.20
).
∎
